===Original===
To 

 Dr. E. N. da C. Andrade, 

 

 From 

 

 E. Nesbit 

 Well Hall, 

 Kent

===Chinese===
献给 

 E. N. da C. 安德拉德博士（Dr. E. N. da C. Andrade），

 

 来自 

 

 E. 内斯比特（E. Nesbit）

 韦尔厅（Well Hall），

 肯特（Kent）

## I. Sabrina Fair / 一、萨布丽娜集市

===Original===
That going to the seaside was the very beginning of everything﻿—only it seemed as though it were going to be a beginning without an end, like the roads on the Sussex downs which look like roads and then look like paths, and then turn into sheep tracks, and then are just grass and furze bushes and tottergrass and harebells and rabbits and chalk.

===Chinese===
去海边度假，正是一切事情的开端——只是它看上去像是一个没有尽头的开端，就像苏塞克斯丘陵草原上的路：起初好好儿的像条路，接着变得像条小径，再变成羊肠兽道，到末了就只剩下青草、荆豆丛、摇摇草、风铃花、兔子和白垩土了。

===Original===
The children had been counting the days to The Day. Bernard indeed had made a calendar on a piece of cardboard that had once been the bottom of the box in which his new white sandshoes came home. He marked the divisions of the weeks quite neatly in red ink, and the days were numbered in blue ink, and every day he crossed off one of those numbers with a piece of green chalk he happened to have left out of a penny box. Mavis had washed and ironed all the dolls’ clothes at least a fortnight before The Day. This was thoughtful and farsighted of her, of course, but it was a little trying to Kathleen, who was much younger and who would have preferred to go on playing with her dolls in their dirtier and more familiar state.

===Chinese===
孩子们一直掰着指头数"大日子"还有几天。伯纳德（Bernard）果真用一块硬纸板做了本日历——那纸板原是装他那双新白帆布鞋的鞋盒底。他用红墨水整整齐齐地标出每周的分隔，日子用蓝墨水编上号，每天还要用一截绿粉笔划掉其中一个号码——那截粉笔，是他碰巧从一便士一盒的粉笔盒里省下来的。梅维斯（Mavis）早在"大日子"前至少两个星期，就把娃娃们的衣裳全都洗好熨平了。这当然很见她的细心与远虑，只是有点儿难为了凯思琳（Kathleen）：她年纪小得多，情愿让娃娃们保持那副更脏也更熟悉的老样子，接着跟它们玩。

===Original===
“Well, if you do,” said Mavis, a little hot and cross from the ironing board, “I’ll never wash anything for you again, not even your face.”

===Chinese===
"哼，你要是再弄脏，"梅维斯说——她在熨衣板前忙得有点儿上火动气，"我往后什么都不给你洗了，连你的脸都不洗。"

===Original===
Kathleen somehow felt as if she could bear that.

===Chinese===
凯思琳不知怎么的，倒觉得自己受得了这个。

===Original===
“But mayn’t I have just one of the dolls” was, however, all she said, “just the teeniest, weeniest one? Let me have Lord Edward. His head’s half gone as it is, and I could dress him in a clean hanky and pretend it was kilts.”

===Chinese===
不过她只说了这么一句："就不能给我留一个娃娃吗？就留一个顶顶小的小不点儿？让我留爱德华勋爵（Lord Edward）吧。反正他的脑袋已经掉了半边了，我可以拿块干净手帕给他围上，就装作那是苏格兰短裙。"

===Original===
Mavis could not object to this, because, of course, whatever else she washed she didn’t wash hankies. So Lord Edward had his pale kilts, and the other dolls were put away in a row in Mavis’s corner drawer. It was after that that Mavis and Francis had long secret consultations﻿—and when the younger ones asked questions they were told, “It’s secrets. You’ll know in good time.” This, of course, excited everyone very much indeed﻿—and it was rather a comedown when the good time came, and the secret proved to be nothing more interesting than a large empty aquarium which the two elders had clubbed their money together to buy, for eight-and-ninepence in the Old Kent Road. They staggered up the front garden path with it, very hot and tired.

===Chinese===
梅维斯没法反对，因为不用说，别的什么都洗，手帕她可是从来不洗的。于是爱德华勋爵穿上了他浅色的"短裙"，其余的娃娃一排收进梅维斯墙角的抽屉里。打那以后，梅维斯和弗朗西斯（Francis）开始了一场又一场长长的密谈——小的们一问，就得到这么一句："是秘密。到时候你们就知道了。"这自然让大家个个兴奋得了不得——可到时候真到了，秘密揭开，却叫人大跌眼镜：原来不过是只大空水族箱，是两个大的把各自的钱凑在一起，在老肯特路（Old Kent Road）花八先令九便士买来的，再没什么更稀罕的了。他们抬着它，摇摇晃晃地走上屋前花园的小径，一个个热得要命、累得要命。

===Original===
“But what are you going to do with it?” Kathleen asked, as they all stood around the nursery table looking at it.

===Chinese===
"可你们打算拿它做什么呢？"凯思琳问。这时大家都围着育儿室的桌子看它。

===Original===
“Fill it with seawater,” Francis explained, “to put sea anemones in.”

===Chinese===
"装上海水，"弗朗西斯解释说，"好养海葵。"

===Original===
“Oh yes,” said Kathleen with enthusiasm, “and the crabs and starfish and prawns and the yellow periwinkles﻿—and all the common objects of the seashore.”

===Chinese===
"哦，对，"凯思琳满腔热忱地说，"还有螃蟹、海星、虾、黄的滨螺——还有海滨一切寻常之物。"

===Original===
“We’ll stand it in the window,” Mavis added: “it’ll make the lodgings look so distinguished.”

===Chinese===
"咱们把它摆在窗口，"梅维斯补了一句，"咱们这出租屋看上去会多有气派呀。"

===Original===
“And then perhaps some great scientific gentleman, like Darwin or Faraday, will see it as he goes by, and it will be such a joyous surprise to him to come face-to-face with our jellyfish; he’ll offer to teach Francis all about science for nothing﻿—I see,” said Kathleen hopefully.

===Chinese===
"到时候说不定哪位科学界的大绅士，像达尔文（Darwin）啊、法拉第（Faraday）啊，打门口路过瞧见了它，跟咱们的水母打了个正着——对他来说，那该是多叫人欢喜的一场意外；他会主动提出来分文不取，把科学统统教给弗朗西斯——我都能想见那情景。"凯思琳满怀希望地说。

===Original===
“But how will you get it to the seaside?” Bernard asked, leaning his hands on the schoolroom table and breathing heavily into the aquarium, so that its shining sides became dim and misty. “It’s much too big to go in the boxes, you know.”

===Chinese===
"可你们怎么把它弄到海边去呢？"伯纳德问。他双手撑在家庭教室的桌子上，朝着水族箱呼哧呼哧直喘粗气，把亮晶晶的箱壁哈得又暗又雾。"要知道，它太大啦，箱子可装不下。"

===Original===
“Then I’ll carry it,” said Francis, “it won’t be in the way at all﻿—I carried it home today.”

===Chinese===
"那我就自己抱着它，"弗朗西斯说，"一点儿也不碍事——今天就是我抱回来的。"

===Original===
“We had to take the bus, you know,” said truthful Mavis, “and then I had to help you.”

===Chinese===
"可我们不得不坐公共汽车，你知道吧，"实话实说的梅维斯说，"而且到后来，还得我搭把手帮你。"

===Original===
“I don’t believe they’ll let you take it at all,” said Bernard﻿—if you know anything of grownups you will know that Bernard proved to be quite right.

===Chinese===
"我看呀，他们压根儿不会让你带的，"伯纳德说——只要你对大人有半点儿了解，就知道伯纳德这话后来完全说中了。

===Original===
“Take an aquarium to the seaside﻿—nonsense!” they said. And “What for?” not waiting for the answer. “They,” just at present, was Aunt Enid.

===Chinese===
"带只水族箱去海边——胡闹！"他们说。还有"带它干什么？"——问完也不等回答。眼下这个"他们"，指的就是伊妮德姨妈（Aunt Enid）。

===Original===
Francis had always been passionately fond of water. Even when he was a baby he always stopped crying the moment they put him in the bath. And he was the little boy who, at the age of four, was lost for three hours and then brought home by the police who had found him sitting in a horse trough in front of the Willing Mind, wet to the topmost hair of his head, and quite happy, entertaining a circle of carters with pots of beer in their hands. There was very little water in the horse trough and the most talkative of the carters explained that, the kid being that wet at the first start off, him and his mates thought he was as safe in the trough as anywhere﻿—the weather being what it was and all them nasty motors and trams about.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯天生就狂热地爱水。他还是个奶娃娃的时候，一放进澡盆就立刻不哭了。而且就是这个小男孩，四岁那年走丢了三个钟头，末了是警察把他送回家的——警察发现他正坐在"乐意心"酒馆（the Willing Mind）门前的马槽里，湿到头顶最上头那根头发，快活得很，正给一圈手捧啤酒壶的马车夫逗乐子呢。马槽里本来就没多少水，话最多的那个马车夫解释说：这小家伙一开头就湿成那副样子了，他跟他那几个伙计寻思，他待在马槽里跟待在别处一样稳妥——眼下天气又是那个样子，四下里又尽是那些讨厌的汽车和电车。

===Original===
To Francis, passionately attracted as he was by water in all forms, from the simple mud puddle to the complicated machinery by which your bath supply is enabled to get out of order, it was a real tragedy that he had never seen the sea. Something had always happened to prevent it. Holidays had been spent in green countries where there were rivers and wells and ponds, and waters deep and wide﻿—but the water had been fresh water, and the green grass had been on each side of it. One great charm of the sea, as he had heard of it, was that it had nothing on the other side “so far as eye could see.” There was a lot about the sea in poetry, and Francis, curiously enough, liked poetry.

===Chinese===
对弗朗西斯来说——凡是水他都狂热地着迷，从最简单的小泥坑，到那套能让你们家洗澡水说坏就坏的复杂机器——他居然从来没有见过大海，这实在是一场真正的悲剧。总有事发生，拦着他去不成。假期都是在绿色的乡间过的，那儿有河，有井，有池塘，有又深又宽的水——可那水是淡水，两岸长着青青的草。照他听来的说法，大海有一大妙处：在"目力所及"的范围之内，对岸什么都没有。诗里写了许许多多大海，而说来奇怪，弗朗西斯偏偏喜欢诗。

===Original===
The buying of the aquarium had been an attempt to make sure that, having found the sea, he should not lose it again. He imagined the aquarium fitted with a real rock in the middle, to which radiant sea anemones clung and limpets stuck. There were to be yellow periwinkles too, and seaweeds, and gold and silver fish (which don’t live in the sea by the way, only Francis didn’t know this), flitting about in radiant scaly splendor, among the shadows of the growing water plants. He had thought it all out﻿—how a cover might be made, very light, with rubber in between, like a screw-top bottle, to keep the water in while it traveled home in the guard’s van to the admiration of passengers and porters at both stations. And now﻿—he was not to be allowed to take it.

===Chinese===
买下这只水族箱，就是想保证一件事：找到了大海之后，他不会再失去它。他想象水族箱正中央安上一块真正的岩石，绚烂的海葵攀在岩上，笠贝紧贴在石上。还要有黄滨螺，有海藻，还有金鱼银鱼（顺便说一句，这两种鱼并不住在海里，只是弗朗西斯不知道罢了），披着鳞光闪闪的华彩，在渐渐长高的水草暗影间穿来穿去。他连细节都盘算好了——盖子怎么做：要非常轻，中间夹一层橡胶，像螺旋盖瓶子那样，好让水在回家路上一滴不洒；水族箱就搁在押车员的车厢里一路回家，让两个车站上的旅客和搬运夫啧啧赞叹。可如今——人家不许他带了。

===Original===
He told Mavis, and she agreed with him that it was a shame.

===Chinese===
他把这事讲给梅维斯听，梅维斯也同意他的看法：这太不像话了。

===Original===
“But I’ll tell you what,” she said, for she was not one of those comforters who just say, “I’m sorry,” and don’t try to help. She generally thought of something that would make things at any rate just a little better. “Let’s fill it with fresh water, and get some goldfish and sand and weeds; and I’ll make Eliza promise to put ants’ eggs in﻿—that’s what they eat﻿—and it’ll be something to break the dreadful shock when we have to leave the sea and come home again.”

===Chinese===
"不过我有个主意，"她说。因为她不是那种只会说声"真遗憾"、却不想法子帮忙的安慰人的人。她总能想出点什么，叫事情至少好转那么一丝丝。"咱们给它灌上淡水，买几条金鱼，添些沙子和水草；我再让伊莱扎（Eliza）答应喂蚂蚁蛋——金鱼吃的就是这个——等咱们不得不离开海边回家的时候，有它挡一挡，那个可怕的打击也就好受些。"

===Original===
Francis admitted that there was something in this and consented to fill the aquarium with water from the bath. When this was done the aquarium was so heavy that the combined efforts of all four children could not begin to move it.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯承认这话有几分道理，同意用洗澡水把水族箱灌满。灌好之后，水族箱沉得吓人，四个孩子一齐上阵，也休想挪动它一星半点儿。

===Original===
“Never mind,” said Mavis, the consoler; “let’s empty it out again and take it back to the common room, and then fill it by secret jugfuls, carried separately, you know.”

===Chinese===
"不要紧，"安慰人的梅维斯说，"咱们把水倒出去，把它搬回公共休息室，然后偷偷地灌——一罐一罐地运，每回单提一罐，你们懂吧。"

===Original===
This might have been successful, but Aunt Enid met the first secret jugful﻿—and forbade the second.

===Chinese===
这办法本来兴许能成，可惜头一罐偷偷摸摸的水就撞上了伊妮德姨妈——第二罐当场就被禁止了。

===Original===
“Messing about,” she called it. “No, of course I shan’t allow you to waste your money on fish.” And Mother was already at the seaside getting the lodgings ready for them. Her last words had been﻿—

===Chinese===
她管这叫"瞎折腾"。"不行，我当然不能容许你们把钱糟蹋在鱼身上。"这时候，妈妈已经先去了海边，替他们收拾租住的房子。她临别的最后一句话是——

===Original===
“Be sure you do exactly what Aunt Enid says.” So, of course, they had to. Also Mother had said, “Don’t argue,” so they had not even the melancholy satisfaction of telling Aunt Enid that she was quite wrong, and that they were not messing about at all.

===Chinese===
"千万记住，伊妮德姨妈说什么就做什么。"于是他们当然只好照办。妈妈还说过"不许争辩"，所以他们连那一点点凄凄惨惨的痛快也没有——没法告诉伊妮德姨妈，说她大错特错，说他们压根儿不是在瞎折腾。

===Original===
Aunt Enid was not a real aunt, but just an old friend of Grandmamma’s, with an aunt’s name and privileges and rather more than an aunt’s authority. She was much older than a real aunt and not half so nice. She was what is called “firm” with children, and no one ever called her auntie. Just Aunt Enid. That will tell you in a moment.

===Chinese===
伊妮德姨妈不是亲姨妈，只是外婆的一位老朋友，占了姨妈的名分和好处，权威却比真姨妈还要大上一截。她比真姨妈老得多，可爱劲儿还不及人家一半。她属于那种对孩子们"严格"得出名的人，从来没有人叫过她一声"姨妈咪"。就叫伊妮德姨妈。光凭这一点，你立刻就明白她是个怎样的人了。

===Original===
So there the aquarium was, dishearteningly dry﻿—for even the few drops left in it from its first filling dried up almost at once.

===Chinese===
于是水族箱就那么搁着，干得叫人心灰——头一回灌水剩下的那几滴，也几乎转眼就干了。

===Original===
Even in its unwatery state, however, the aquarium was beautiful. It had not any of that ugly ironwork with red lead showing between the iron and the glass which you may sometimes have noticed in the aquariums of your friends. No, it was one solid thick piece of clear glass, faintly green, and when you stooped down and looked through you could almost fancy that there really was water in it.

===Chinese===
不过，水族箱虽然没有水，却依然是美的。它没有那种难看的铁框子，铁和玻璃之间露着一道红丹——朋友家的水族箱上，你有时候兴许见过那玩意儿。不，它是一整块厚厚的透亮玻璃，微微泛绿；你弯下腰透过它望过去，几乎能当真以为里头盛着水。

===Original===
“Let’s put flowers in it,” Kathleen suggested, “and pretend they’re anemones. Do let’s, Francis.”

===Chinese===
"咱们往里头摆花吧，"凯思琳出主意，"假装是海葵。就让咱们摆嘛，弗朗西斯。"

===Original===
“I don’t care what you do,” said Francis. “I’m going to read *The Water Babies*.”

===Chinese===
"你们爱怎么弄随你们，"弗朗西斯说，"我要读《水孩子》（The Water Babies）了。"

===Original===
“Then we’ll do it, and make it a lovely surprise for you,” said Kathleen cheerily.

===Chinese===
"那我们就动手，还要给你一个大大的惊喜，"凯思琳乐呵呵地说。

===Original===
Francis sat down squarely with *The Water Babies* flat before him on the table, where also his elbows were, and the others, respecting his sorrow, stole quietly away. Mavis just stepped back to say, “I say, France, you don’t mind their putting flowers? It’s to please you, you know.”

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯端端正正地坐下，《水孩子》摊平在面前的桌上，胳膊肘也照旧支在桌上。其余几个体谅他的伤心，蹑手蹑脚地溜出去了。只有梅维斯折回来交代了一句："我说，弗兰（France），你不介意他们摆花吧？你知道，这是为了讨你高兴。"

===Original===
“I tell you I don’t mind *anything*,” said Francis savagely.

===Chinese===
"我跟你说了，我*什么*都不介意，"弗朗西斯恶声恶气地说。

===Original===
When the three had finished with it, the aquarium really looked rather nice, and, if you stooped down and looked sideways through the glass, like a real aquarium.

===Chinese===
等三个人拾掇完，水族箱看上去当真相当不错；你要是弯下腰，从侧面透过玻璃看，活脱脱就是一只真水族箱。

===Original===
Kathleen took some clinkers from the back of the rockery﻿—“where they won’t show,” she said﻿—and Mavis induced these to stand up like an arch in the middle of the glassy square. Tufts of long grass, rather sparingly arranged, looked not unlike waterweed. Bernard begged from the cook some of the fine silver sand which she uses to scrub the kitchen tables and dressers with, and Mavis cut the thread of the Australian shell necklace that Uncle Robert sent her last Christmas, so that there should be real, shimmery, silvery shells on the sand. (This was rather self-sacrificing of her, because she knew she would have to put them all back again on their string, and you know what a bother shells are to thread.) They shone delightfully through the glass. But the great triumph was the sea anemones﻿—pink and red and yellow﻿—clinging to the rocky arch just as though they were growing there.

===Chinese===
凯思琳从假山后头搬来几块煤渣石——"搁在不显眼的地方"，她说——梅维斯设法让它们在玻璃方框正中立起一道拱门。几丛长草，插得疏疏朗朗，看上去竟和水草有几分相像。伯纳德向厨娘讨来一点细银沙，就是她用来擦洗厨房桌案碗柜的那种；梅维斯则铰断了罗伯特叔叔（Uncle Robert）去年圣诞寄给她的那条澳大利亚贝壳项链的线，好让沙地上躺上真正闪闪发亮、银灿灿的贝壳。（这在她可算得自我牺牲，因为她知道自己回头还得把贝壳一个个重新穿回线上，而穿贝壳有多烦人，你是知道的。）贝壳透过玻璃闪闪发亮，可爱极了。不过最了不起的胜利还是海葵——粉的、红的、黄的——攀在石拱上，就像当真长在那儿一样。

===Original===
“Oh, lovely, lovely,” Kathleen cried, as Mavis fixed the last delicate flesh-tinted crown. “Come and look, France.”

===Chinese===
"哦，美极了，美极了，"凯思琳喊道——这时梅维斯正安放最后一顶娇嫩的肉色花冠。"快来看呀，弗兰。"

===Original===
“Not yet,” said Mavis, in a great hurry, and she tied the thread of the necklace round a tin goldfish (out of the box with the duck and the boat and the mackerel and the lobster and the magnet that makes them all move about﻿—you know) and hung it from the middle of the arch. It looked just as though it were swimming﻿—you hardly noticed the thread at all.

===Chinese===
"还不到时候，"梅维斯急急忙忙地说。她把项链的线拴上一条马口铁金鱼（就是从那个盒子里拿的——盒子里有鸭子、小船、鲭鱼、龙虾，还有一块磁铁，能让它们统统动来动去——你们知道的），把它挂在拱门正中央。它看上去就像正在游——那根线你几乎注意不到。

===Original===
“*Now*, France,” she called. And Francis came slowly with his thumb in *The Water Babies*. It was nearly dark by now, but Mavis had lighted the four dollhouse candles in the gilt candlesticks and set them on the table around the aquarium.

===Chinese===
"*现在*看吧，弗兰。"她喊道。弗朗西斯慢吞吞地走来，拇指还夹在《水孩子》里。这会儿天差不多黑了，梅维斯点亮了插在镀金烛台里的四支娃娃屋小蜡烛，围着水族箱摆上桌面。

===Original===
“Look through the side,” she said; “isn’t it ripping?”

===Chinese===
"从侧面看，"她说，"是不是棒极了？"

===Original===
“Why,” said Francis slowly, “you’ve got water in it﻿—and real anemones! Where on earth﻿ ﻿… ?”

===Chinese===
"咦，"弗朗西斯慢吞吞地说，"你们往里头装了水——还是真海葵！到底是打哪儿……？"

===Original===
“Not real,” said Mavis. “I wish they were; they’re only dahlias. But it does look pretty, doesn’t it?”

===Chinese===
"不是真的，"梅维斯说，"我倒希望是呢；那不过是几朵大丽花。不过看着确实美，是不是？"

===Original===
“It’s like Fairyland,” said Kathleen, and Bernard added, “I *am* glad you bought it.”

===Chinese===
"这简直像仙境。"凯思琳说。伯纳德又添了一句："买了它，我*真*高兴。"

===Original===
“It just shows what it will be like when we *do* get the sea creatures,” said Mavis. “Oh, Francis, you do like it, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
"这正好叫人瞧瞧，等咱们*真的*把海里的活物弄到手，它该是什么模样，"梅维斯说，"哦，弗朗西斯，你确实喜欢它，是不是？"

===Original===
“Oh, I like it all right,” he answered, pressing his nose against the thick glass, “but I wanted it to be waving weeds and mysterious wetness like the Sabrina picture.”

===Chinese===
"哦，喜欢，我当然喜欢，"他答道，一边把鼻子压在厚玻璃上，"可我原本想要的，是飘摇的水草、湿漉漉的神秘，就像萨布丽娜（Sabrina）那幅画里的样子。"

===Original===
The other three glanced at the picture which hung over the mantelpiece﻿—Sabrina and the water nymphs, drifting along among the waterweeds and water lilies. There were words under the picture, and Francis dreamily began to say them:

===Chinese===
另外三个瞥了一眼挂在壁炉台上方的那幅画——画上是萨布丽娜和水仙女，漂行在水草与睡莲之间。画底下题着字，弗朗西斯恍恍惚惚地念了起来：

===Original===
> “ ‘Sabrina fair,
 

 Listen where thou art sitting,
 

 Under the glassie, cool, translucent wave
 

 In twisted braids of Lillies knitting
 

 The loose train of thy amber-dropping hair.﻿ ﻿…’ ”

===Chinese===
> "'美丽的萨布丽娜，

 请听，就在你端坐之处，

 在玻璃般清凉澄澈的水波底下，

 百合编成扭缠的发辫，织就

 你滴淌琥珀的松散长发。……'"

===Original===
“Hullo﻿—what was that?” he said in quite a different voice, and jumped up.

===Chinese===
"喂——那是什么？"他说话的调子完全变了，人一下子跳了起来。

===Original===
“What was what?” the others naturally asked.

===Chinese===
"什么是什么？"其余几个自然要这么问。

===Original===
“Did you put something alive in there?” Francis asked.

===Chinese===
"你们是不是往里头放了什么活物？"弗朗西斯问。

===Original===
“Of course not,” said Mavis. “Why?”

===Chinese===
"当然没有，"梅维斯说，"怎么啦？"

===Original===
“Well, I saw something move, that’s all.”

===Chinese===
"呃，我看见有个东西动了一下，就这么回事。"

===Original===
They all crowded around and peered over the glass walls. Nothing, of course, but the sand and the grass and the shells, the clinkers and the dahlias and the little suspended tin goldfish.

===Chinese===
大家都围拢过来，越过玻璃壁往里张望。当然什么也没有，只有沙、青草、贝壳，煤渣石、大丽花，还有那条悬空挂着的马口铁小金鱼。

===Original===
“I expect the goldfish swung a bit,” said Bernard. “That’s what it must have been.”

===Chinese===
"我看是金鱼晃了一晃，"伯纳德说，"准是那么回事。"

===Original===
“It didn’t look like that,” Francis answered. “It looked more like﻿—”

===Chinese===
"看着可不像，"弗朗西斯回答，"它更像是——"

===Original===
“Like what?”

===Chinese===
"像什么？"

===Original===
“I don’t know﻿—get out of the light. Let’s have another squint.”

===Chinese===
"我说不上来——别挡着光。让我再眯着眼瞅一眼。"

===Original===
He stooped down and looked again through the glass.

===Chinese===
他弯下腰，又透过玻璃看。

===Original===
“It’s not the goldfish,” he said. “That’s as quiet as a trout asleep. No﻿—I suppose it was a shadow or something.”

===Chinese===
"不是金鱼，"他说，"它安静得像条睡熟的鳟鱼。不——我看，八成是个影子什么的。"

===Original===
“You might tell us what it looked like,” said Kathleen.

===Chinese===
"你总可以告诉我们它长什么模样吧。"凯思琳说。

===Original===
“Was it like a rat?” Bernard asked with interest.

===Chinese===
"像不像一只老鼠？"伯纳德颇感兴趣地问。

===Original===
“Not a bit. It was more like﻿—”

===Chinese===
"一点儿也不像。它更像是——"

===Original===
“Well, like what?” asked three aggravated voices.

===Chinese===
"到底像什么呀？"三个声音一齐恼了。

===Original===
“Like Sabrina﻿—only very, very tiny.”

===Chinese===
"像萨布丽娜——只是非常非常小。"

===Original===
“A sort of doll﻿—Sabrina,” said Kathleen, “how awfully jolly!”

===Chinese===
"一个洋娃娃萨布丽娜，"凯思琳说，"多来劲呀！"

===Original===
“It wasn’t at all like a doll, and it wasn’t jolly,” said Francis shortly﻿—“only I wish it would come again.”

===Chinese===
"它一点儿也不像洋娃娃，也谈不上来劲，"弗朗西斯没好气地说——"我只是盼着它再来一次。"

===Original===
It didn’t, however.

===Chinese===
然而它没有再来。

===Original===
“I say,” said Mavis, struck by a new idea, “perhaps it’s a magic aquarium.”

===Chinese===
"我说，"梅维斯开了口，一个新念头忽然击中她，"说不定这是只魔法水族箱。"

===Original===
“Let’s play it is,” suggested Kathleen﻿—“let’s play it’s a magic glass and we can see what we like in it. I see a fairy palace with gleaming spires of crystal and silver.”

===Chinese===
"咱们就装作它是呗，"凯思琳提议，"装作这是块魔法玻璃，想看什么就能看见什么。我看见一座仙宫，塔尖的水晶和白银闪闪发光。"

===Original===
“I see a football match, and our chaps winning,” said Bernard heavily, joining in the new game.

===Chinese===
"我看见一场足球赛，咱们这队赢了。"伯纳德瓮声瓮气地说，也加入了这个新游戏。

===Original===
“Shut up,” said Francis. “That isn’t play. There was something.”

===Chinese===
"闭嘴，"弗朗西斯说，"这不是闹着玩。刚才真有个东西。"

===Original===
“Suppose it is magic,” said Mavis again.

===Chinese===
"万一它真是魔法呢，"梅维斯又说。

===Original===
“We’ve played magic so often, and nothing’s ever happened﻿—even when we made the fire of sweet-scented woods and eastern gums, and all that,” said Bernard; “it’s much better to pretend right away. We always have to in the end. Magic just wastes time. There isn’t any magic really, is there, Mavis?”

===Chinese===
"咱们玩魔法玩过多少回了，回回什么事儿也不出——连那回烧起香木和东方树胶的火，还有那一整套，也没用，"伯纳德说，"不如干脆一上来就装假。到头来反正都得装假。魔法纯粹耽误工夫。世上根本就没有魔法，是不是，梅维斯？"

===Original===
“Shut up, I tell you,” was the only answer of Francis, his nose now once more flattened against the smooth green glass.

===Chinese===
"我叫你闭嘴，"弗朗西斯只回了这么一句，鼻子这会儿又贴平在那块光滑的绿玻璃上。

===Original===
Here Aunt Enid’s voice was heard on the landing outside, saying, “Little ones﻿—bed,” in no uncertain tones.

===Chinese===
正在这时，外面楼梯平台上响起了伊妮德姨妈的声音："小的们——上床。"口气毫不含糊。

===Original===
The two grunted as it were in whispers, but there was no appeal against Aunt Enid, and they went, their grunts growing feebler as they crossed the room, and dying away in a despairing silence as they and Aunt Enid met abruptly at the top of the stairs.

===Chinese===
两个小的咕哝了一声，算是压着嗓子的抗议；可伊妮德姨妈的话没有上诉一说，他们只好去了。咕哝声在他们穿过房间时越来越弱，等他们在楼梯顶上冷不防迎面撞见伊妮德姨妈，那点声音就在一片绝望的静默里咽了下去。

===Original===
“Shut the door,” said Francis, in a strained sort of voice. And Mavis obeyed, even though he hadn’t said “please.” She really was an excellent sister. Francis, in moments of weakness, had gone so far as to admit that she wasn’t half bad.

===Chinese===
"把门关上，"弗朗西斯说，声音有点发紧。梅维斯照办了，尽管他没说一个"请"字。她真是个顶好的姐姐。弗朗西斯在意志薄弱的时刻，甚至承认过：她还真不赖。

===Original===
“I say,” she said when the click of the latch assured her that they were alone, “how could it be magic? We never said any spell.”

===Chinese===
"我说，"等门闩咔哒一响、让她确信屋里只剩他们两个，她才开口，"它怎么会是魔法呢？咱们又没念咒语。"

===Original===
“No more we did,” said Francis, “unless﻿—And besides, it’s all nonsense, of course, about magic. It’s just a game we play, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
"可不是没念嘛，"弗朗西斯说，"除非——再说，什么魔法不魔法的，当然全是胡扯。那不过是咱们玩的一种游戏，对不对？"

===Original===
“Yes, of course,” Mavis said doubtfully; “but what did you mean by ‘unless’?”

===Chinese===
"对，当然，"梅维斯将信将疑地说，"可你说'除非'是什么意思？"

===Original===
“We weren’t saying any spells, were we?”

===Chinese===
"咱们刚才没念什么咒语，对吧？"

===Original===
“No, of course we weren’t﻿—we weren’t saying anything﻿—”

===Chinese===
"没有，当然没有——咱们什么也没说——"

===Original===
“As it happens *I* was.”

===Chinese===
"说来凑巧，*我*念了。"

===Original===
“Was what? When?”

===Chinese===
"念了什么？什么时候？"

===Original===
“When it happened.”

===Chinese===
"那件事发生的时候。"

===Original===
“What happened?”

===Chinese===
"什么事发生了？"

===Original===
Will it be believed that Aunt Enid chose this moment for opening the door just wide enough to say, “Mavis﻿—bed.” And Mavis had to go. But as she went she said again: “What happened?”

===Chinese===
说来叫人不敢相信，伊妮德姨妈偏偏挑中这个当口，把门推开一条缝，刚好够说一句："梅维斯——上床。"梅维斯只好去。可临走她又问了一遍："什么事发生了？"

===Original===
“*It*,” said Francis, “whatever it was. I was saying.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
"*它*，"弗朗西斯说，"不管它究竟是什么。我当时正念着。……"

===Original===
“**Mavis!**” called Aunt Enid.

===Chinese===
"**梅维斯！**"伊妮德姨妈喊道。

===Original===
“Yes, Aunt Enid﻿—you were saying *what*?”

===Chinese===
"来了，伊妮德姨妈——你当时念的*什么*？"

===Original===
“I was saying, ‘Sabrina fair,’ ” said Francis, “do you think﻿—but, of course, it couldn’t have been﻿—and all dry like that, no water or anything.”

===Chinese===
"我当时念的是：'美丽的萨布丽娜，'"弗朗西斯说，"你想会不会——不过，当然，不可能是——再说它干成那样，没有水，什么都没有。"

===Original===
“Perhaps magic *has* to be dry,” said Mavis. “Coming, Aunt Enid! It seems to be mostly burning things, and, of course, that wouldn’t do in the water. What *did* you see?”

===Chinese===
"说不定魔法*非*得是干的不可，"梅维斯说，"来了，伊妮德姨妈！魔法好像多半是把东西烧掉，那当然不能在水里烧。你*到底*看见了什么？"

===Original===
“It looked like Sabrina,” said Francis﻿—“only tiny, tiny. Not doll-small, you know, but live-small, like through the wrong end of a telescope. I do wish you’d seen it.”

===Chinese===
"它看上去像萨布丽娜，"弗朗西斯说——"只是小小的，小小的。你知道，不是洋娃娃那么小，是活物那么小，就像从望远镜倒过来的那一头望出去似的。我真希望你也看见了。"

===Original===
“Say, ‘Sabrina fair’ again quick while I look.”

===Chinese===
"快，趁我看着的时候，再念一遍'美丽的萨布丽娜'。"

===Original===
> “ ‘Sabrina fair,
 

 Listen where thou art sitting,
 

 Under the﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
> "'美丽的萨布丽娜，

 请听，就在你端坐之处，

 在那——'"

===Original===
“Oh, Mavis, it is﻿—it did. There’s something there truly. Look!”

===Chinese===
"哦，梅维斯，是真的——真有。那里头真有个东西。快看！"

===Original===
“Where?” said Mavis. “I can’t see﻿—oh, let me look.”

===Chinese===
"在哪儿？"梅维斯说，"我看不见——哦，让我瞧瞧。"

===Original===
“**Mavis!**” called Aunt Enid very loud indeed; and Mavis tore herself away.

===Chinese===
"**梅维斯！**"伊妮德姨妈这回喊得可是真响；梅维斯只得硬生生把自己拽开。

===Original===
“I must go,” she said. “Never mind, we’ll look again tomorrow. Oh, France, if it *should* be﻿—magic, I mean﻿—I’ll tell you what﻿—”

===Chinese===
"我必须走了，"她说，"别管啦，咱们明天再看。哦，弗兰，万一它真的是——我是说，万一真是魔法——我告诉你一件事——"

===Original===
But she never told him what, for Aunt Enid swept in and swept out, bearing Mavis away, as it were, in a whirlwind of impatient exasperation, and, without seeming to stop to do it, blowing out the four candles as she came and went.

===Chinese===
可她永远没能告诉他那件事，因为伊妮德姨妈一阵风似的卷进来又卷出去，仿佛把梅维斯裹在一团不耐烦的火气里带走了；她来去之间，顺手就吹灭了那四支蜡烛，连像是停下来吹一下的工夫都没花。

===Original===
At the door she turned to say, “Good night, Francis. Your bath’s turned on ready. Be sure you wash well behind your ears. We shan’t have much time in the morning.”

===Chinese===
到门口她回过头来说："晚安，弗朗西斯。洗澡水给你放好了。耳朵后头务必好好洗。明天早上咱们工夫不多。"

===Original===
“But Mavis always bathes first,” said he. “I’m the eldest.”

===Chinese===
"可平时总是梅维斯先洗呀，"他说，"我才是老大。"

===Original===
“Don’t argue, child, for goodness’ sake,” said Aunt Enid. “Mavis is having the flat bath in my bedroom to save time. Come﻿—no nonsense,” she paused at the door to say. “Let me see you go. Right about face﻿—quick march!”

===Chinese===
"看在老天爷份上，别顶嘴，孩子，"伊妮德姨妈说，"梅维斯这会儿在我卧室里用浅底浴盆洗，为的是省工夫。走吧——少来那套，"她在门口顿了顿，又补一句，"让我看着你走。向后转——齐步走！"

===Original===
And he had to.

===Chinese===
他只好照办。

===Original===
“If she must pretend to give orders like drill, she might at least learn to say ‘ ’Bout turn!’ ” he reflected, struggling with his collar stud in the steaming bathroom. “Never mind. I’ll get up early and see if I can’t see it again.”

===Chinese===
"她既然非要摆出操练下口令的架势，那起码也该学会喊一声像样的'向后转！'"他一边在热气腾腾的浴室里跟领扣搏斗，一边想。"不要紧。我要早早起来，看看能不能再见到它。"

===Original===
And so he did﻿—but early as he was, Aunt Enid and the servants were earlier. The aquarium was empty﻿—clear, clean, shining and quite empty.

===Chinese===
他也果真起来了——可他起得再早，伊妮德姨妈和仆人们起得更早。水族箱空了——透亮、干净、闪光，空空如也。

===Original===
Aunt Enid could not understand why Francis ate so little breakfast.

===Chinese===
伊妮德姨妈怎么也想不明白，弗朗西斯的早饭为什么吃得那么少。

===Original===
“What has she done with them?” he wondered later.

===Chinese===
"她把它们怎么处置了？"他后来直犯嘀咕。

===Original===
“*I* know,” said Bernard solemnly. “She told Esther to put them on the kitchen fire﻿—I only just saved my fish.”

===Chinese===
"*我*知道，"伯纳德一本正经地说，"她叫埃丝特（Esther）把它们搁上厨房的炉火——我的金鱼是好不容易才抢下来的。"

===Original===
“And what about my shells?” asked Mavis in sudden fear.

===Chinese===
"那我的贝壳呢？"梅维斯突然害怕起来，忙问。

===Original===
“Oh, she took those to take care of. Said you weren’t old enough to take care of them yourself.”

===Chinese===
"哦，贝壳她收去'代为保管'了。说你年纪还不够大，自己保管不了。"

===Original===
You will wonder why the children did not ask their Aunt Enid right out what had become of the contents of the aquarium. Well, you don’t know their Aunt Enid. And besides, even on that first morning, before anything that really *was* anything could be said to have happened﻿—for, after all, what Francis said he had seen might have been just fancy﻿—there was a sort of misty, curious, trembling feeling at the hearts of Mavis and her brother which made them feel that they did not want to talk about the aquarium and what had been in it to any grownup﻿—and least of all to their Aunt Enid.

===Chinese===
你会纳闷，孩子们为什么不直截了当地问伊妮德姨妈，水族箱里的东西都哪儿去了。唉，那是因为你不认识他们这位伊妮德姨妈。再说，就连在头一个早晨，在任何一件真正*算得上事*的事还谈不上发生之前——毕竟，弗朗西斯说他看见的那个东西，也可能只是眼花——梅维斯和她哥哥心里就已经生出一种朦朦胧胧、又好奇又发颤的感觉，叫他们不愿意同任何大人谈论这只水族箱和它装过的东西——最不愿意同他们的伊妮德姨妈谈。

===Original===
And leaving the aquarium, that was the hardest thing of all. They thought of telegraphing to Mother, to ask whether, after all, they mightn’t bring it﻿—but there was first the difficulty of wording a telegram so that their mother would understand and not deem it insanity or a practical joke﻿—secondly, the fact that ten-pence halfpenny, which was all they had between them, would not cover the baldest statement of the facts.

===Chinese===
而要把水族箱留下来，那是所有事情里最难的一件。他们想过给妈妈打电报，问问到底能不能把它带上——可是第一，电报怎么措辞就是个大难题，既要让妈妈看得懂，又不能让她当成疯话或是恶作剧；第二，他们所有人加在一起的全部家当也只有十便士半，连最光秃秃的事实陈述都打不起。

===Original===
> Mrs. Desmond, 

 Care of Mrs. Pearce, 

 East Cliff Villa, 

 Lewis Road, 

 West Beachfield-on-Sea, Sussex

===Chinese===
> 德斯蒙德太太（Mrs. Desmond），

 请皮尔斯太太（Mrs. Pearce）转交，

 东崖别墅（East Cliff Villa），

 刘易斯路（Lewis Road），

 萨塞克斯郡滨海西比奇菲尔德（West Beachfield-on-Sea, Sussex）

===Original===
alone would be eightpence﻿—and the simplest appeal, such as “May we bring aquarium please say yes wire reply” brought the whole thing hopelessly beyond their means.

===Chinese===
光地址这一项就得八便士——而最简单的请求，比如"可否携水族箱速回电允许"，一算下来，就把整件事毫无指望地推到了他们的财力之外。

===Original===
“It’s no good,” said Francis hopelessly. “And, anyway,” said Kathleen, “there wouldn’t be time to get an answer before we go.”

===Chinese===
"没用的，"弗朗西斯绝望地说。"再说，"凯思琳接了话，"出发以前也来不及等到回电了。"

===Original===
No one had thought of this. It was a sort of backhanded consolation.

===Chinese===
这一点谁都没想过。这算是种拐着弯的安慰。

===Original===
“But think of coming back to it,” said Mavis: “it’ll be something to live for, when we come back from the sea and everything else is beastly.”

===Chinese===
"可是想想回来还能见到它，"梅维斯说，"等我们从海边回来、别的什么都糟心透顶的时候，它总归是个盼头。"

===Original===
And it was.

===Chinese===
它也确实是个盼头。

## II The Captive / 二、俘虏

===Original===
The delicate pinkish bloom of newness was on the wooden spades, the slick smoothness of the painted pails showed neither scratch nor dent on their green and scarlet surface﻿—the shrimping nets were full and fluffy as, once they and sand and water had met, they never could be again. The pails and spades and nets formed the topmost layer of a pile of luggage﻿—you know the sort of thing, with the big boxes at the bottom; and the carryall bulging with its wraps and mackers; the old portmanteau that shows its striped lining through the crack and is so useful for putting boots in; and the sponge bag, and all the little things that get left out. You can almost always squeeze a ball or a paint box or a box of chalks or any of those things﻿—which grownups say you won’t really want till you come back﻿—into that old portmanteau﻿—and then when it’s being unpacked at the journey’s end the most that can happen will be that someone will say, “I thought I told you not to bring that,” and if you don’t answer back, that will be all. But most likely in the agitation of unpacking and settling in, your tennis ball, or pencil box, or whatever it is, will pass unnoticed. Of course, you can’t shove an aquarium into the old portmanteau﻿—nor a pair of rabbits, nor a hedgehog﻿—but anything in reason you can.
The luggage that goes in the van is not much trouble﻿—of course, it has to be packed and to be strapped, and labeled and looked after at the junction, but apart from that the big luggage behaves itself, keeps itself to itself, and like your elder brothers at college never occasions its friends a moment’s anxiety. It is the younger fry of the luggage family, the things you have with you in the carriage that are troublesome﻿—the bundle of umbrellas and walking sticks, the golf clubs, the rugs, the greatcoats, the basket of things to eat, the books you are going to read in the train and as often as not you never look at them, the newspapers that the grownups are tired of and yet don’t want to throw away, their little bags or dispatch cases and suitcases and card cases, and scarfs and gloves﻿—
The children were traveling under the care of Aunt Enid, who always had far more of these tiresome odds and ends than Mother had﻿—and it was at the last moment, when the cab was almost to be expected to be there, that Aunt Enid rushed out to the corner shop and returned with four new spades, four new pails, and four new shrimping nets, and presented them to the children just in time for them to be added to the heap of odds and ends with which the cab was filled up.

===Chinese===
木铲上还罩着一层新物件特有的浅粉光泽，漆桶滑腻光洁，绿的红的桶面上找不出一道划痕、一个凹坑；捞虾网蓬蓬松松、鼓鼓囊囊——等它们一旦同沙子、同海水打过照面，就再也蓬松不起来了。桶啊铲啊网啊，堆在一大堆行李的最上层——你知道那种堆法：大箱子垫底；大旅行袋塞满了披肩外套和雨衣，撑得鼓鼓囊囊；旧皮箱的裂缝里露出条纹衬里，拿来装靴子再合适不过；还有洗漱包，还有所有那些临了被落在外头的小零碎。你几乎总能把一只球、一盒颜料、一盒粉笔，或者随便什么诸如此类的东西——大人们说你反正要等回了家才用得着——塞进那只旧皮箱——这样等旅程终点开箱的时候，最坏的下场也不过是有人说一句"我记得我说过别带这个"，你只要不还嘴，事情就算过去了。不过十有八九，在开箱和安顿的忙乱里，你的网球也好，铅笔盒也好，随便什么吧，都会神不知鬼不觉地混过去。当然，鱼缸是塞不进旧皮箱的——一对兔子塞不进，刺猬也塞不进——但凡讲点道理的东西，都塞得进。
装进行李车的大行李倒不怎么麻烦——当然，得打包、捆扎、贴标签，到了枢纽站还得照看；可撇开这些不谈，大行李规规矩矩，安分守己，就像你上了大学的哥哥们一样，从不让朋友们为它操一刻心。麻烦的是行李家族里的小字辈，就是随身带进客车车厢的那些东西——雨伞手杖扎成的一捆、高尔夫球杆、毯子、大衣、装吃食的篮子、你打算在火车上看的书（十有八九你压根不翻一眼）、大人们看腻了却又舍不得扔的报纸，还有他们的小手袋、公文箱、旅行箱、名片盒，以及围巾和手套——
孩子们这趟出行，归伊妮德姨妈（Aunt Enid）照管，而这种恼人的零七碎八，她总比母亲带得多得多——就在最后一刻，眼看马车按说就要到了，伊妮德姨妈冲进街角的铺子，抱着四把新木铲、四只新桶、四张新捞虾网回来，赶趟儿似的把东西送给孩子，正好来得及添进马车上已经堆得满满登登的那堆零碎里头。

===Original===
“I hope it’s not ungrateful,” said Mavis at the station as they stood waiting by the luggage mound while Aunt Enid went to take the tickets﻿—“but why couldn’t she have bought them at Beachfield?”
“Makes us look such babies,” said Francis, who would not be above using a wooden spade at the proper time and place but did not care to be branded in the face of all Waterloo Junction as one of those kids off to the seaside with little spades and pails.
Kathleen and Bernard were, however, young enough to derive a certain pleasure from stroking the smooth, curved surface of the spades till Aunt Enid came fussing back with the tickets and told them to put their gloves on for goodness’ sake and try not to look like street children.
I am sorry that the first thing you should hear about the children should be that they did not care about their Aunt Enid, but this was unfortunately the case. And if you think this was not nice of them I can only remind you that you do not know their Aunt Enid.

===Chinese===
"但愿我这么说不算没良心，"到了车站，大家站在行李堆旁，等伊妮德姨妈去买票，梅维斯（Mavis）说，"可她为什么就不能在比奇菲尔德（Beachfield）买呢？"
"这不是显得我们跟小娃娃似的嘛。"弗朗西斯（Francis）说。倒不是说他多有架子，到了合适的时机场合，木铲他也照样肯用，只是他不愿意当着整个滑铁卢枢纽站（Waterloo Junction）的乘客，被人看成那种拎着小铲小桶去海边的小孩子。
不过凯思琳（Kathleen）和伯纳德（Bernard）年纪还小，抚摸着木铲光滑的弧面，倒也自有一番乐趣，一直摸到伊妮德姨妈风风火火拿着车票回来，叫他们看在老天爷份上戴上手套，尽量别弄得像街头的野孩子。
很抱歉，你们听到的关于这几个孩子的头一件事，居然是他们不喜欢他们的伊妮德姨妈，可惜事实偏偏如此。要是你们觉得他们这样不像话，我只能说：那是因为你们不认识他们的伊妮德姨妈。

===Original===
There was a short, sharp struggle with the porter, a flustered passage along the platform and the children were safe in the carriage marked “Reserved”﻿—thrown into it, as it were, with all that small fry of luggage which I have just described. Then Aunt Enid fussed off again to exchange a few last home truths with the porter, and the children were left.
“We breathe again,” said Mavis.
“Not yet we don’t,” said Francis, “there’ll be some more fuss as soon as she comes back. I’d almost as soon not go to the sea as go with her.”
“But you’ve never seen the sea,” Mavis reminded him.
“I know,” said Francis, morosely, “but look at all this﻿—” he indicated the tangle of their possessions which littered seats and rack﻿—“I do wish﻿—”

===Chinese===
跟搬运工短短狠狠地较劲了一场，又慌慌张张穿过站台，孩子们总算安安稳稳进了那节标着"已预订"的车厢——可以说，是同我刚才描述过的那一小窝行李一起，被一股脑儿扔了进去。随后伊妮德姨妈又风风火火地走开了，去同搬运工最后交流几句逆耳的忠言，孩子们这才落了单。
"咱们又喘上气了。"梅维斯说。
"还早着呢。"弗朗西斯说，"她一回来，保准还有一通忙乱。与其跟她一块儿去海边，我差不多宁可不去。"
"可你还从来没见过海啊。"梅维斯提醒他。
"我知道。"弗朗西斯没精打采地说，"可你瞧瞧这些——"他指了指散乱在座位和行李架上、缠成一团的家当——"我真希望——"

===Original===
He stopped, for a head appeared in the open doorway﻿—in a round hat very like Aunt Enid’s﻿—but it was not Aunt Enid’s. The face under the hat was a much younger, kinder one.
“I’m afraid this carriage is reserved,” said the voice that belonged to the face.
“Yes,” said Kathleen, “but there’s lots of room if you like to come too.”
“I don’t know if the aunt we’re with would like it,” said the more cautious Mavis. “We should, of course,” she added to meet the kind smiling eyes that looked from under the hat that was like Aunt Enid’s.
The lady said: “I’m an aunt too﻿—I’m going to meet my nephew at the junction. The train’s frightfully crowded.﻿ ﻿… If I were to talk to your aunt﻿ ﻿… perhaps on the strength of our common aunthood. The train will start in a minute. I haven’t any luggage to be a bother﻿—nothing but one paper.”﻿—she had indeed a folded newspaper in her hands.

===Chinese===
他收住了口，因为敞开的车门口探进来一颗脑袋——戴着一顶跟伊妮德姨妈那顶很像的圆帽——可那不是伊妮德姨妈的脑袋。帽子底下那张脸要年轻得多，也和善得多。
"恐怕这节车厢是预订了的。"那张脸的主人开口说。
"是啊，"凯思琳说，"不过您要是愿意一起坐，地方有的是。"
"不知道跟我们一起的那位姨妈肯不肯。"比较谨慎的梅维斯说。"我们当然欢迎。"她又补了一句，迎着帽子底下那双望着她的、和善含笑的眼睛——那顶帽子跟伊妮德姨妈的很像。
那位太太说："我也是当姨妈的——我要去枢纽站接我外甥。车上挤得吓人。……要是我去跟你们姨妈说一声……也许凭着咱们同是姨妈的交情。车马上就要开了。我没有行李添麻烦——就一张报纸。"——她手里确实拿着一张折好的报纸。

===Original===
“Oh, do get in,” said Kathleen, dancing with anxiety, “I’m sure Aunt Enid won’t mind,”﻿—Kathleen was always hopeful﻿—“suppose the train were to start or anything!”
“Well, if you think I may,” said the lady, and tossed her paper into the corner in a lighthearted way which the children found charming. Her pleasant face was rising in the oblong of the carriage doorway, her foot was on the carriage step, when suddenly she retreated back and down. It was almost as though someone pulled her off the carriage step.
“Excuse me,” said a voice, “this carriage is reserved.” The pleasant face of the lady disappeared and the﻿—well, the face of Aunt Enid took its place. The lady vanished. Aunt Enid trod on Kathleen’s foot, pushed against Bernard’s waistcoat, sat down, partly on Mavis and partly on Francis and said﻿—“Of all the impertinence!” Then someone banged the door﻿—the train shivered and trembled and pulled itself together in the way we all know so well﻿—grunted, snorted, screamed, and was off. Aunt Enid stood up arranging things on the rack, so that the children could not even see if the nice lady had found a seat in the train.

===Chinese===
"哦，快上来吧。"凯思琳说，急得直打转，"伊妮德姨妈肯定不会介意的。"——凯思琳永远这么满怀希望——"万一车开了还是怎么的！"
"好吧，要是你们觉得我可以的话。"太太说着，轻快地把报纸往角落里一抛，孩子们觉得这个样子可爱极了。她那张愉快的脸正从车厢门口那方长方形的门洞里往上升，一只脚已经踩上车梯，忽然间她却往后一缩、退了下去，简直像是叫人从车梯上拽了下去似的。
"对不起，"一个声音说，"这节车厢是预订的。"太太那张愉快的脸不见了，顶上来的是——嗯，伊妮德姨妈的脸。太太无影无踪。伊妮德姨妈一脚踩上凯思琳的脚，一膀子撞着伯纳德的背心，坐了下来——半边压着梅维斯，半边压着弗朗西斯——说道："真是放肆透顶！"接着不知是谁砰地摔上车门——火车一个哆嗦、一阵颤抖，又像我们大家都熟透了的那样振作起来——哼哧、呼哧、尖叫一声，开走了。伊妮德姨妈站起来张罗行李架上的东西，孩子们连那位好心的太太在车上找到座没有，都没法看上一眼。

===Original===
“Well﻿—I do think﻿—” Francis could not help saying.
“Oh﻿—do you?” said Aunt Enid, “I should never have thought it of you.”
When she had arranged the things in the rack to her satisfaction she pointed out a few little faults that she had noticed in the children and settled down to read a book by Miss Marie Corelli. The children looked miserably at each other. They could not understand why Mother had placed them under the control of this most unpleasant mock aunt.

===Chinese===
"真是的——我确实觉得——"弗朗西斯忍不住说。
"哦——是吗？"伊妮德姨妈说，"我可真想不到你会是这个样子。"
等她把行李架上的东西张罗得称心如意之后，又指出了她注意到的孩子们身上的几样小毛病，然后坐定下来，读一本玛丽·科雷利（Marie Corelli）小姐写的书。孩子们愁眉苦脸地你看我、我看你。他们不明白，母亲为什么把他们交给这么一位顶顶讨厌的假姨妈管。

===Original===
There was a reason for it, of course. If your parents, who are generally so kind and jolly, suddenly do a thing that you can’t understand and can hardly bear, you may be quite sure they have a good reason for it. The reason in this case was that Aunt Enid was the only person who offered to take charge of the children at a time when all the nice people who usually did it were having influenza. Also she was an old friend of Granny’s. Granny’s taste in friends must have been very odd, Francis decided, or else Aunt Enid must have changed a good deal since she was young. And there she sat reading her dull book. The children also had been provided with books﻿—*Eric, or Little by Little*; *Elsie, or Like a Little Candle*; *Brave Bessie* and *Ingenious Isabel* had been dealt out as though they were cards for a game, before leaving home. They had been a great bother to carry, and they were impossible to read. Kathleen and Bernard presently preferred looking out of the windows, and the two elder ones tried to read the paper left by the lady, “looking over.”
Now, that is just where it was, and really what all that has been written before is about. If that lady hadn’t happened to look in at their door, and if she hadn’t happened to leave the paper they would never have seen it, because they weren’t the sort of children who read papers except under extreme provocation.

===Chinese===
这当然是有缘故的。要是你那一向和和气气、有说有笑的父母，忽然做下一件你弄不懂、也几乎受不了的事，你尽管放心，他们自有充分的理由。这回的理由是：在平日肯照管孩子们的那些好人全都闹流感的当口，只有伊妮德姨妈一个人肯揽这桩差事。再说，她还是外婆的老朋友。外婆交朋友的口味一定古怪得很，弗朗西斯断定，要不然，就是伊妮德姨妈打年轻时起变了许多。她就这样坐在那儿，读她那本乏味的书。孩子们也领到了书——《埃里克，或曰积少成多》（*Eric, or Little by Little*）、《埃尔茜，或曰像一支小蜡烛》（*Elsie, or Like a Little Candle*）、《勇敢的贝西》（*Brave Bessie*）和《机灵的伊莎贝尔》（*Ingenious Isabel*），出门前像发牌似的分发给了他们。带着它们累赘得很，读又没法读。凯思琳和伯纳德没多久就宁可看窗外去了，两个大的便头挨着头，共读那位太太留下的报纸。
瞧，关键就在这儿——其实，前头写下的这一大篇，为的正是这个。倘若那位太太没有恰好在他们门口张望一眼，倘若她没有恰好把报纸留下，孩子们是无论如何看不到它的，因为他们不是那种孩子——除非逼到了极处，绝不读报。

===Original===
You will not find it easy to believe, and I myself can’t see why it should have happened, but the very first word they saw in that newspaper was Beachfield, and the second was On, and the third was Sea, and the fifth was Mermaid. The fourth which came between Sea and Mermaid was Alleged.
“I say,” said Mavis, “let’s look.”
“Don’t pull then, you can see all right,” said Francis, and this is what they read together:

> “ ‘At this season of the year, which has come to be designated the silly season, the public press is deluged with puerile old-world stories of gigantic gooseberries and enormous sea serpents. So that it is quite in keeping with the weird traditions of this time of the year to find a story of some wonder of the deep, arising even at so well-known a watering place as Beachfield. Close to an excellent golf course, and surrounded by various beauty spots, with a thoroughly revised water supply, a newly painted pier and three rival Cinematograph Picture Palaces, Beachfield has long been known as a rising plage of exceptional attractions, the quaint charm of its.﻿ ﻿…’ ”

===Chinese===
你们不会轻易相信，我自己也闹不明白怎么会有这样的巧事：他们在这张报纸上看到的第一个词是"比奇菲尔德"，第二个是"海"，第三个是"滨"，第五个是"美人鱼"。而夹在"滨"和"美人鱼"之间的第四个词，是"疑现"。
"喂，"梅维斯说，"咱们看看吧。"
"那就别拽呀，你还看得见呢。"弗朗西斯说。下面就是他们一起读到的内容：

> " '每年的这个时节，都被人称作新闻淡季，大小报章灌满了老掉牙的幼稚奇谈——什么大得惊人的鹅莓啦，长得出奇的海蛇啦。因此，就在这样一个素来流传怪谈的季节里，冒出一桩深水奇闻，而且偏偏出在像比奇菲尔德这样远近闻名的海滨浴场，倒也正合时宜。比奇菲尔德紧邻一流的高尔夫球场，四周名胜环抱，供水系统经过彻底改造，码头新漆过一遍，还有三家互别苗头的电影宫，久已被誉为一处蒸蒸日上、魅力非凡的海滨胜境，其古雅的风韵令……' "

===Original===
“Hold on,” said Francis, “this isn’t about any old Mermaid.”
“Oh, that’ll be further on,” said Mavis. “I expect they have to put all that stuff in to be polite to Beachfield﻿—let’s skip﻿—‘agreeable promenade, every modern convenience, while preserving its quaint.﻿ ﻿…’ What does quaint mean, and why do they keep on saying it?”
“I don’t think it means anything,” said Francis, “it’s just a word they use, like weird and dainty. You always see it in a newspaper. Ah﻿—got her. Here she is﻿—‘The excitement may be better imagined than described’﻿—no, that’s about the Gymkhana﻿—here we are:

===Chinese===
"打住，"弗朗西斯说，"这讲的根本不是什么美人鱼。"
"哦，那准在后头呢。"梅维斯说，"我看这些话是免不了的——写给比奇菲尔德捧场的——咱们跳过去——'宜人的滨海步道，一切现代便利设施应有尽有，同时不失其古雅的……''古雅'是什么意思？他们干嘛翻来覆去老说它？"
"我看它没什么意思，"弗朗西斯说，"就是个他们爱用的词儿，跟'离奇'呀、'精致'呀一个样。报纸上老见着。啊——找着了。在这儿——'其轰动之状，可想而不可言'——不对，这是说竞技大会的——这就对了：

===Original===
> “ ‘Master Wilfred Wilson, the son of a well-known and respected resident, arrived home yesterday evening in tears. Inquiry elicited a statement that he had been paddling in the rock pools, which are to be found in such profusion under the West Cliff, when something gently pinched his foot. He feared that it might be a lobster, having read that these crustaceans sometimes attack the unwary intruder, and he screamed. So far his story, though unusual, contains nothing inherently impossible. But when he went on to state that a noise “like a lady speaking” told him not to cry, and that, on looking down, he perceived that what held him was a hand “coming from one of the rocks under water,” his statement was naturally received with some incredulity. It was not until a boating party returning from a pleasure trip westward stated that they had seen a curious sort of white seal with a dark tail darting through the clear water below their boat that Master Wilfred’s story obtained any measure of credence.’ ”

(“What’s credence?” said Mavis.
“Oh, never mind. It’s what you believe with, I think. Go on,” said Francis.)

> “ ‘﻿—of credence. Mr. Wilson, who seems to have urged an early retirement to bed as a cure for telling stories and getting his feet wet, allowed his son to rise and conduct him to the scene of adventure. But Mr. Wilson, though he even went to the length of paddling in some of the pools, did not see or feel any hands nor hear any noise, ladylike or otherwise. No doubt the seal theory is the correct one. A white seal would be a valuable acquisition to the town, and would, no doubt, attract visitors. Several boats have gone out, some with nets and some with lines. Mr. Carrerras, a visitor from South America, has gone out with a lariat, which in these latitudes is, of course, quite a novelty.’ ”

===Chinese===
> " '威尔弗雷德·威尔逊（Wilfred Wilson）小少爷——本地一位素孚众望的居民之子——昨天晚上哭着回了家。经询问，他陈述道：当时他正在西崖（West Cliff）下多不胜数的石洼里蹚水玩，有什么东西轻轻夹了一下他的脚。他读过大鳌虾有时会袭击不留神的闯入者的文章，生怕那是一只鳌虾，便尖叫起来。到这里为止，他的说法虽然离奇，倒还没有什么根本不可能的地方。可是他接着又说，有个"像女士说话"的声音叫他别哭，还说低头一看，发现攥住他的原来是一只"从水下岩石里伸出来的手"，他的说法自然就被人将信将疑地收下了。直到一支乘船出游、自西边尽兴而归的人报告说，他们看见一只模样古怪的白海豹拖着一条深色尾巴，在他们船底下清澈的水里一蹿而过，小威尔弗雷德的说法才算得到了几分采信。' "

（"采信是什么？"梅维斯说。
"哦，不用管。我想，大概就是人家拿来相信事儿用的东西吧。往下念。"弗朗西斯说。）

> " '——几分采信。威尔逊先生似乎力主，治编瞎话和弄湿脚的灵丹妙药就是早早上床，不过他还是让儿子起身，领着他去了出事的现场。可是威尔逊先生哪怕亲自下到几个水洼里蹚了蹚，也没看见或摸到什么手，也没听见什么声响——不管像不像女士。毫无疑问，海豹之说才是正解。一只白海豹将是本镇一笔宝贵的进项，毫无疑问会招来游客。已经开出去了好几条船，有的带网，有的带钓线。来自南美的游客卡雷拉斯（Carrerras）先生带着一条套索出海了——在这种纬度，这当然算得上一件稀罕玩意儿。' "

===Original===
“That’s all,” whispered Francis, and glanced at Aunt Enid. “I say﻿—she’s asleep.” He beckoned the others, and they screwed themselves along to that end of the carriage farthest from the slumbering aunt. “Just listen to this,” he said. Then in hoarse undertones he read all about the Mermaid.
“I say,” said Bernard, “I do hope it’s a seal. I’ve never seen a seal.”
“I hope they *do* catch it,” said Kathleen. “Fancy seeing a real live Mermaid.”
“If it’s a real live Mermaid I jolly well hope they don’t catch her,” said Francis.
“So do I,” said Mavis. “I’m certain she would die in captivity.”

===Chinese===
"完了。"弗朗西斯低声说，朝伊妮德姨妈瞟了一眼。"喂——她睡着了。"他招呼其他几个过来，大家蜷着身子一点点挪到车厢里离熟睡的姨妈最远的那一头。"你们听好了。"他说。然后他压着一副沙哑的嗓子，把美人鱼的始末全念给他们听。
"喂，"伯纳德说，"我还真希望那是只海豹。我还从没见过海豹呢。"
"我倒希望他们*真能*逮住它。"凯思琳说。想想看，能亲眼见到一条活生生的美人鱼。
"要真是条活生生的美人鱼，我可巴不得他们逮不着她。"弗朗西斯说。
"我也巴不得。"梅维斯说，"我敢肯定，她被俘必死。"

===Original===
“But I’ll tell you what,” said Francis, “we’ll go and look for her, first thing tomorrow. I suppose,” he added thoughtfully, “Sabrina was a sort of Mermaid.”
“She hasn’t a tail, you know,” Kathleen reminded him.
“It isn’t the tail that makes the Mermaid,” Francis reminded her. “It’s being able to live underwater. If it was the tail, then mackerels would be Mermaids.”
“And, of course, they’re not. *I* see,” said Kathleen.
“I wish,” said Bernard, “that she’d given us bows and arrows instead of pails and spades, and then we could have gone seal-shooting﻿—”
“Or Mermaid-shooting,” said Kathleen. “Yes, that would have been ripping.”

===Chinese===
"不过我跟你们说，"弗朗西斯说，"明天头一件事，咱们就去找她。我琢磨着，"他若有所思地补了一句，"萨布丽娜（Sabrina）也算是一种美人鱼吧。"
"她可没有尾巴，你知道吧。"凯思琳提醒他。
"光有尾巴可当不成美人鱼。"弗朗西斯反过来提醒她，"得能在水底下过日子才算。要是看尾巴，那鲭鱼个个都是美人鱼了。"
"它们当然不是。*我*明白了。"凯思琳说。
"我倒希望，"伯纳德说，"她送咱们的是弓箭，不是铲子桶，那咱们就能去猎海豹了——"
"或者猎美人鱼。"凯思琳说，"对呀，那才叫带劲呢。"

===Original===
Before Francis and Mavis could say how shocked they were at the idea of shooting Mermaids, Aunt Enid woke up and took the newspaper away from them, because newspapers are not fit reading for children.
She was somehow the kind of person before whom you never talk about anything that you really care for, and it was impossible therefore to pursue either seals or Mermaids. It seemed best to read *Eric* and the rest of the books. It was uphill work.
But the last two remarks of Bernard and Kathleen had sunk into the minds of the two elder children. That was why, when they had reached Beachfield and found Mother and rejoiced over her, and when Aunt Enid had unexpectedly gone on by that same train to stay with her really relations at Bournemouth, they did not say any more to the little ones about Mermaids or seals, but just joined freely in the chorus of pleasure at Aunt Enid’s departure.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯和梅维斯还没来得及说出一听"猎美人鱼"他们心里有多不是滋味，伊妮德姨妈就醒了，把报纸从他们手里收走了，因为报纸可不是给孩子们读的东西。
她偏偏是那么一种人——在她面前，你真正放在心上的事是一句也提不得的，因此海豹也好美人鱼也好，都谈不下去了。看来顶好还是读《埃里克》和其余那几本书。那可是桩一步一喘的苦活。
可是伯纳德和凯思琳最后那两句话，已经落进了两个大孩子的心里。所以，等他们到了比奇菲尔德、找到了母亲、欢天喜地团聚过了，等伊妮德姨妈出人意料地坐着同一班车继续前行、去伯恩茅斯（Bournemouth）投奔她真正的亲戚之后，他们没有再对两个小的提起美人鱼或海豹，只管痛痛快快加入了大家对伊妮德姨妈离去的齐声欢呼。

===Original===
“I thought she was going to stay with us all the time,” said Kathleen. “Oh, Mummy, I am so glad she isn’t.”
“Why? Don’t you like Aunt Enid? Isn’t she kind?”
All four thought of the spades and pails and shrimping nets, and of *Eric* and *Elsie* and the other books﻿—and all said:
“Yes.”
“Then what was it?” Mother asked. And they could not tell her. It is sometimes awfully difficult to tell things to your mother, however much you love her. The best Francis could do was:

===Chinese===
"我还以为她要一直陪着咱们呢。"凯思琳说，"哦，妈妈，她不陪着我可太高兴了。"
"怎么啦？你不喜欢伊妮德姨妈？她待你不好吗？"
四个孩子都想起了那些铲子、桶和捞虾网，想起了《埃里克》《埃尔茜》和其余那几本书——齐声答道：
"好。"
"那到底是为了什么呢？"母亲问。他们却说不出口。有些事要说给母亲听，有时候难得很——哪怕你多么爱她。弗朗西斯至多也只说得出一句：

===Original===
“Well﻿—you see we’re not used to her.”
And Kathleen said: “I don’t think perhaps she’s used to being an aunt. But she was kind.”
And Mother was wise and didn’t ask any more questions. Also she at once abandoned an idea one had had of asking Aunt Enid to come and stay at Beachfield for part of the holidays; and this was just as well, for if Aunt Enid had not passed out of the story exactly when she did, there would not have been any story to pass out of. And as she does now pass out of the story I will say that she thought she was very kind, and that she meant extremely well.

===Chinese===
"嗯——你知道，我们跟她处不惯。"
凯思琳则说："我想，也许她还没当惯姨妈吧。不过她是好意的。"
母亲很明智，没有再问下去。她还立刻打消了一个先前有人起过的念头——请伊妮德姨妈到比奇菲尔德来，一起度过假期的一部分日子。这样正好，因为伊妮德姨妈若不是恰恰在那个节骨眼上退出了这个故事，这故事也就根本无从谈起了。既然她如今确实退出了故事，我愿意在这儿补上一句：她自认为非常和善，也确实是一片至诚的好心。

===Original===
There was a little whispering between Francis and Mavis just after tea, and a little more just before bed, but it was tactfully done and the unwhispered-to younger ones never noticed it.
The lodgings were very nice﻿—a little way out of the town﻿—not a villa at all as everyone had feared. I suppose the landlady thought it grander to call it a villa, but it was really a house that had once been a mill house, and was all made of a soft-colored gray wood with a red-tiled roof, and at the back was the old mill, also gray and beautiful﻿—not used now for what it was built for﻿—but just as a store for fishing nets and wheelbarrows and old rabbit hutches and beehives and harnesses and odds and ends, and the sack of food for the landlady’s chickens. There was a great corn bin there too﻿—that must have been in some big stable﻿—and some broken chairs and an old wooden cradle that hadn’t had any babies in it since the landlady’s mother was a little girl.

===Chinese===
喝过下午茶，弗朗西斯和梅维斯悄悄嘀咕了一小阵，上床前又嘀咕了一小阵，不过干得很有分寸，没被嘀咕到的两个小的始终没有察觉。
住处非常好——离镇子稍稍偏出去一点——完全不是大家担心的那种"别墅"。我猜女房东管它叫别墅，是觉得这么叫着更气派；可它其实是一座从前做过磨坊坊舍的房子，通体是柔和的灰色木料，顶上铺着红瓦，屋后就是那座老磨坊，也是灰色的，很好看——如今已不干它本行的营生——只用来堆放渔网、手推车、旧兔笼、蜂箱、马具和各色零碎，还有一袋喂女房东家鸡的饲料。那儿还有一只很大的谷箱——从前准是摆在哪个大马厩里的——还有几把破椅子和一只旧木摇篮，从女房东的母亲还是个小姑娘的年代起，那摇篮里就再没睡过婴儿。

===Original===
On any ordinary holiday the mill would have had all the charm of a magic palace for the children, with its wonderful collection of pleasant and unusual things to play with, but just now all their thoughts were on Mermaids. And the two elder ones decided that they would go out alone the first thing in the morning and look for the Mermaid.
Mavis woke Francis up very early indeed, and they got up and dressed quite quietly, not washing, I am sorry to say, because water makes such a noise when you pour it out. And I am afraid their hair was not very thoroughly brushed either. There was not a soul stirring in the road as they went out, unless you count the mill cat who had been out all night and was creeping home very tired and dusty looking, and a yellowhammer who sat on a tree a hundred yards down the road and repeated his name over and over again in that conceited way yellowhammers have, until they got close to him; and then he wagged his tail impudently at them and flew on to the next tree where he began to talk about himself as loudly as ever.

===Chinese===
要是搁在平常的假日，这座磨坊收着那么多好玩又不寻常的宝贝，在孩子们眼里准会像一座魔法宫殿那样迷人；可是眼下，他们满脑子都是美人鱼。两个大的商量定了：明天一大早，他们要单独出门去找那条美人鱼。
梅维斯叫醒弗朗西斯的时候，天确实还早得很。他们起了床，蹑手蹑脚地穿好衣裳——很遗憾，没有洗脸，因为水倒出来的时候，动静实在太大了。恐怕他们的头发也没怎么好好梳过。出门时，路上一个人影也不见，除非你把磨坊的猫算在内——它在外面逛了一整夜，正蔫头耷脑、灰扑扑地往家蹭；还有一只黄鹀，蹲在沿路一百来码远的树上，用它黄鹀特有的那副自鸣得意的腔调，把自己的名字翻来覆去念个没完，直到他们走近跟前，它才没轻没重地朝他们摆摆尾巴，飞到下一棵树上，照旧扯开嗓门自吹自擂起来。

===Original===
This desire to find the Mermaid must have been wonderfully strong in Francis, for it completely swallowed the longing of years﻿—the longing to see the sea. It had been too dark the night before to see anything but the winking faces of the houses as the fly went past them. But now as he and Mavis ran noiselessly down the sandy path in their rubber shoes and turned the corner of the road, he saw a great pale-gray something spread out in front of him, lit with points of red and gold fire where the sun touched it. He stopped.
“Mavis,” he said, in quite an odd voice, “that’s the sea.”
“Yes,” she said and stopped too.
“It isn’t a bit what I expected,” he said, and went on running.
“Don’t you like it?” asked Mavis, running after him.
“Oh﻿—like,” said Francis, “it isn’t the sort of thing you *like*.”

===Chinese===
想找到美人鱼的愿望，在弗朗西斯心里一定强烈得出奇，因为它把积攒了多年的渴望整个吞了下去——看海的渴望。前一天夜里天太黑，马车驶过时，除了房子一眨一眨的脸，什么也看不见。可是现在，他和梅维斯穿着胶鞋，悄没声儿地跑下沙土小径，拐过路角，他看见一大片灰白的什么东西在自己眼前铺展开来，太阳照到的地方，燃起一星星红的、金的火光。他站住了。
"梅维斯。"他说，嗓音相当古怪，"那就是海。"
"是啊。"她说，也站住了。
"跟我原先想的一点也不一样。"他说着，接着跑。
"你不喜欢吗？"梅维斯问，跟在他后面跑。
"哦——喜欢不喜欢的，"弗朗西斯说，"它可不是那种能让你拿来*喜欢*的东西。"

===Original===
When they got down to the shore the sands and the pebbles were all wet because the tide had just gone down, and there were the rocks and the little rock pools, and the limpets, and whelks, and the little yellow periwinkles looking like particularly fine Indian corn all scattered among the red and the brown and the green seaweed.
“Now, this *is* jolly,” said Francis. “This is jolly if you like. I almost wish we’d wakened the others. It doesn’t seem quite fair.”
“Oh, they’ve seen it before,” Mavis said, quite truly, “and I don’t think it’s any good going by fours to look for Mermaids, do you?”
“Besides,” said Francis, saying what had been in their thoughts since yesterday in the train, “Kathleen wanted to shoot Mermaids, and Bernard thought it was seals, anyhow.”
They had sat down and were hastily pulling off their shoes and stockings.

===Chinese===
他们下到岸边时，沙子和卵石还全是湿的，因为潮水刚刚退下去；礁石就在那儿，还有一个个小小的石洼水，帽贝呀、蛾螺呀，还有小小的黄色滨螺，看着就像颗粒特别饱满的玉米，撒落在红的、褐的、绿的海藻中间。
"这才叫*痛快*呢。"弗朗西斯说，"要说痛快，这才真是痛快。我几乎后悔没叫醒他们俩。好像有点不够意思。"
"哦，他们早见过了。"梅维斯说，说得一点不假，"再说，四个人一齐出动去找美人鱼，我看也没什么好处，你说呢？"
"还有，"弗朗西斯说，把从昨天在火车上起就搁在他们心里的想法说了出来，"凯思琳是想拿箭射美人鱼的，伯纳德呢，反正一口咬定那是海豹。"
他们已经坐下来，正急急忙忙脱鞋脱袜子。

===Original===
“Of course,” said he, “we shan’t find anything. It isn’t likely.”
“Well,” she said, “for anything we jolly well know, they may have found her already. Take care how you go over these rocks, they’re awfully slippy.”
“As if I didn’t know that,” said he, and ran across the narrow strip of sand that divided rocks from shingle and set his foot for the first time in The Sea. It was only a shallow little green and white rock pool, but it was the sea all the same.
“I say, isn’t it cold,” said Mavis, withdrawing pink and dripping toes; “do mind how you go﻿—”
“As if I﻿—” said Francis, again, and sat down suddenly and splashingly in a large, clear sparkling pool.

===Chinese===
"当然啦，"他说，"咱们什么也找不着。本来就没什么指望。"
"那可说不准，"她说，"就咱们所知，说不定人家早就把她找着了呢。走这些石头当心点，滑得厉害。"
"说得好像我不知道似的。"他说着，跑过隔开礁石和卵石滩的那窄窄一条沙带，平生第一次把脚踏进了大海。那不过是浅浅的一小洼绿一块白一块的石洼水，可它终究是海。
"哎呀，真凉啊。"梅维斯说着，缩回粉红的、滴滴答答淌水的脚趾，"千万当心脚下——"
"说得好像我——"弗朗西斯又来了这么一句，随即扑通一声，猛地坐进了一汪又大又清、波光闪闪的水洼里。

===Original===
“Now, I suppose we’ve got to go home at once and you change,” said Mavis, not without bitterness.
“Nonsense,” said Francis, getting up with some difficulty and clinging wetly to Mavis to steady himself. “I’m quite dry, almost.”
“You know what colds are like,” said Mavis, “and staying indoors all day, or perhaps bed, and mustard plasters and gruel with butter in it. Oh, come along home, we should never have found the Mermaid. It’s much too bright and light and everyday-ish for anything like magic to happen. Come on home, do.”
“Let’s just go out to the end of the rocks,” Francis urged, “just to see what it’s like where the water gets deep and the seaweed goes swish, swish, all long and lanky and grassy, like in the Sabrina picture.”
“Halfway then, not more,” said Mavis, firmly, “it’s dangerous﻿—deep outside﻿—Mother said so.”

===Chinese===
"这下好了，我看咱们只好马上回家，给你换衣裳了。"梅维斯说，话音里不是没有怨气。
"胡说，"弗朗西斯说，颇费了点劲才爬起来，湿淋淋地攀住梅维斯站稳了身子，"我干着呢——差不多干着呢。"
"你知道感冒是什么滋味，"梅维斯说，"一整天闷在屋里，说不定还得卧床，贴芥末膏，喝搁了黄油的麦片稀糊。哦，还是回家吧，咱们反正也找不着美人鱼。眼下这么亮堂、这么敞亮、这么家常日子里似的，哪会发生什么魔法。快回家吧，求你了。"
"咱们就走到礁石尽头去，"弗朗西斯央求道，"就去看看水变深的地方是什么样子，看看海藻唰啦、唰啦地摆动，长长的、瘦瘦的、跟草似的，就像萨布丽娜那幅画里画的。"
"那就走到一半，不能再多了。"梅维斯斩钉截铁地说，"危险——外边是深水——母亲说过的。"

===Original===
And halfway they went, Mavis still cautious, and Francis, after his wetting, almost showing off in his fine carelessness of whether he went in again or not. It was very jolly. You know how soft and squeezy the blobby kind of seaweed is to walk on, and how satin smooth is the ribbon kind; how sharp are limpets, especially when they are covered with barnacles, and how comparatively bearable to the foot are the pale primrose-colored hemispheres of the periwinkle.
“Now,” said Mavis, “come on back. We’ll run all the way as soon as we get our shoes and stockings on for fear of colds.”
“I almost wish we hadn’t come,” said Francis, turning with a face of gloom.
“You didn’t really think we should find a Mermaid, did you?” Mavis asked, and laughed, though she was really annoyed with Francis for getting wet and cutting short this exciting morning game. But she was a good sister.
“It’s all been so silly. Flopping into that pool, and talking and rotting, and just walking out and in again. We ought to have come by moonlight, and been very quiet and serious, and said﻿—

===Chinese===
于是他们走到了一半，梅维斯还是小心在意，弗朗西斯挨过那一浸之后，倒近乎卖弄起来，拿出满不在乎的漂亮劲儿，仿佛再掉进去一回也无所谓。一路非常痛快。你们知道的：那种一团一团的海藻踩上去多么软和、多么绵乎，那种缎带似的海藻又多么像缎子一样光滑；帽贝多么硌脚，尤其是浑身长满藤壶的时候；而滨螺那淡报春花色的半球，搁在脚底下，又算得上多么好受的了。
"行了，"梅维斯说，"往回走吧。一穿上鞋和袜子，咱们就一路跑回去，免得着凉。"
"我几乎后悔来这一趟了。"弗朗西斯说着转过身，一脸的阴沉。
"你不会真以为咱们能找着美人鱼吧，啊？"梅维斯问，还笑了——虽然她心里着实生弗朗西斯的气，怪他弄湿了身，把这场来劲的清晨游戏拦腰截断了。可她是个好妹妹。
"这一趟全完了，蠢透了。一头栽进水洼里，又是说又是闹，走出去又走回来。咱们本该趁着月光来，本该安安静静、郑重其事，然后念道——

===Original===
> “ ‘Sabrina fair,
 

 Listen where thou art sitting﻿—’ ”

“Ow﻿—Hold on a minute. I’ve caught my foot in something.”
Mavis stopped and took hold of her brother’s arm to steady him; and as she did so both children plainly heard a voice that was not the voice of either of them. It was the sweetest voice in the world they thought, and it said:
“Save her. We die in captivity.”
Francis looked down and had a sort of sudden sight of something white and brown and green that moved and went quickly down under the stone on which Mavis was standing. There was nothing now holding his foot.
“I say,” he said, on a deep breath of awe and wonder, “did you hear that?”

===Chinese===
> " '美丽的萨布丽娜，
 

 且听，你今在何处端坐——' "

"哎哟——等一下。我的脚叫什么东西绊住了。"
梅维斯站住脚，抓住哥哥的胳膊扶稳他；就在这时，两个孩子清清楚楚听见了一个声音——不是他们俩任何一个的声音。他们觉得那是世界上最甜美的声音。它说：
"救救她。我们被俘必死。"
弗朗西斯低头一看，恍惚瞥见一团白的、褐的、绿的东西动了动，飞快地钻进梅维斯脚下那块石头的底下去了。这会儿，攥着他脚的东西没有了。
"喂。"他深深吸进一口又敬又奇的气，"你听见了吗？"

===Original===
“Of course, I heard it.”
“We couldn’t both have fancied it,” he said, “I wish it had told us who to save, and where, and how﻿—”
“Whose do you think that voice was?” Mavis asked softly.
“The Mermaid’s,” said Francis, “who else’s could it have been?”
“Then the magic’s really begun﻿—”
“Mermaids aren’t magic,” he said, “anymore than flying fishes or giraffes are.”

===Chinese===
"当然听见了。"
"咱们俩总不至于一起出了幻觉，"他说，"它要肯告诉咱们救谁、上哪儿救、怎么救就好了——"
"你觉得那是谁的声音？"梅维斯轻声问。
"美人鱼的，"弗朗西斯说，"还能是谁的？"
"这么说，魔法真的开始了——"
"美人鱼可不是魔法，"他说，"就跟飞鱼、长颈鹿不是魔法一个道理。"

===Original===
“But she came when you said ‘Sabrina fair,’ ” said Mavis.
“Sabrina wasn’t a Mermaid,” said Francis firmly. “It’s no use trying to join things on when they won’t. Come on, we may as well be getting home.”
“Mightn’t she be?” suggested Mavis. “A Mermaid, I mean. Like salmon that live in rivers and go down to the sea.”
“I say, I never thought of that. How simply ripping if it turned out to be really Sabrina﻿—wouldn’t it be? But which do you suppose could be her﻿—the one who spoke to us or the one she’s afraid will die in captivity﻿—the one she wants us to save.”
They had reached the shore by now and Mavis looked up from turning her brown stockings right way out to say:

===Chinese===
"可是你一念'美丽的萨布丽娜'，她就来了呀。"梅维斯说。
"萨布丽娜又不是美人鱼。"弗朗西斯斩钉截铁地说，"两件搭不上的事硬要往一块儿搭，也是白搭。走吧，咱们还是趁早回家的好。"
"她就不能是吗？"梅维斯提议道，"我是说，当一条美人鱼。就像鲑鱼，住在河里，又游下海去。"
"哎呀，我可从没想过这个。要是到头来真是萨布丽娜，那该多带劲——是不是？可你猜她们俩哪个才是她——是跟咱们说话的那个，还是她怕被俘必死的那个——她要咱们去救的那个。"
这时他们已经到了岸边，梅维斯正把棕色长袜翻回正面，抬起头来，说道：

===Original===
“I suppose we didn’t really both fancy it. Could we have? Isn’t there some sort of scientific magic that makes people think the same things as each other when it’s not true at all, like with Indian mango tricks? Uncle Fred said so, you know, they call it ‘Tell-ee-something.’ ”
“I’ll tell *you* something,” said Francis, urgent with shoelace, “if we keep on saying things weren’t when we know perfectly well they were, we shall soon dish up any sort of chance of magic we may ever have had. When do you find people in books going on like that? They just say ‘This is magic!’ and behave as if it was. They don’t go pretending they’re not sure. Why, no magic would stand it.”
“Aunt Dorothea once told me that all magic was like Prince Rupert’s drop,” Mavis owned: “if once you broke it there was nothing left but a little dust.”

===Chinese===
"我看咱们俩并不是真一起出了幻觉。会吗？不是有一种科学的魔法吗，能让人俩同时想到一模一样的事，其实压根儿没那回事，就像印度人的芒果戏法那样？弗雷德叔叔（Uncle Fred）就说过，他们管它叫'心电感应'来着。"
"我倒要告诉你一件事，"弗朗西斯说，手上急着系鞋带，"咱们要是明明白白知道事情发生了，还一个劲儿嚷嚷没发生过，那用不了多久，咱们这辈子可能沾上的那一丁点儿魔法的机会，就全给葬送光了。你在书里几时见过谁这样磨叽的？书里的人只说一声'这是魔法！'然后就照真有那么回事的样子行事。他们可不会装出一副拿不准的样子。嗨，什么魔法也受不了这个。"
"多萝西娅阿姨（Aunt Dorothea）有一回跟我说过，天下的魔法都跟鲁珀特之泪（Prince Rupert's drop）一个样，"梅维斯承认道，"一旦打破了，剩下的就只有一小撮粉末。"

===Original===
“That’s just what I’m saying, isn’t it? We’ve always felt there was magic right enough, haven’t we? Well, now we’ve come across it, don’t let’s be silly and pretend. Let’s believe in it as hard as ever we can. Mavis﻿—shall we, eh? Believing in things makes them stronger. Aunt Dorothea said that too﻿—you remember.”
They stood up in their shoes.
“Shall we tell the others?” Mavis asked.
“We must,” said Francis, “it would be so sneakish not to. But they won’t believe us. We shall have to be like Cassandra and not mind.”
“I only wish I knew who it is we’ve got to save,” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
"我说的不就是这个理儿吗？咱们一直都觉着世上分明有魔法，不是吗？好，如今真叫咱们撞上了，就别犯傻、别装蒜了。咱们要使足了劲儿去相信它。梅维斯——信不信，啊？相信一件事，就能让它变得更有力气。多萝西娅阿姨也这么说过——你记得的。"
他们穿好鞋，站起身来。
"要不要告诉他们俩？"梅维斯问。
"必须告诉，"弗朗西斯说，"瞒着他们，太鬼鬼祟祟了。不过他们是不会信的。咱们只好学卡珊德拉（Cassandra）的样，别往心里去。"
"我只盼着能知道，咱们要救的到底是谁。"梅维斯说。

===Original===
Francis had a very strong and perfect feeling that they would know this all in good time. He could not have explained this, but he felt it. All he said was, “Let’s run.”
And they ran.
Kathleen and Bernard met them at the gate, dancing with excitement and impatience.
“Where have you been?” they cried and “What on earth?” and “Why, you’re all wet, France.”
“Down to the sea﻿—shut up, I know I am﻿—” their elder brother came in and passed up the path to the gate.
“You might have called us,” said Kathleen in a more-in-sorrow-than-in-anger sort of voice, “but anyhow you’ve lost something by going out so early without us.”

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯心里有一种十分强烈、十分完满的感觉：到了时候，他们自然会知道的。这道理他讲不出来，可他感觉得到。他只说了一句："跑吧。"
于是他们跑了起来。
凯思琳和伯纳德在院门口迎住他们，兴奋加着急，蹦个不停。
"你们上哪儿去了？"他们嚷道，还嚷"到底怎么回事？"还嚷"哎呀，你浑身都湿了，弗兰（France）。"
"去海边了——闭嘴，我知道湿了——"他们的大哥一边答，一边跨进院门，顺着小径往屋里走。
"你们本来该叫上我们的，"凯思琳用一种哀多于怒的口气说，"不过不管怎么说，你们撇下我们这么早出去，可是错过东西了。"

===Original===
“Lost something. What?”
“Hearing the great news,” said Bernard, and he added, “Aha!”
“What news?”
“Wouldn’t you like to know?” Bernard was naturally annoyed at having been left out of the first expedition of the holidays. Anyone would have. Even you or I.
“Out with it,” said Francis, with a hand on Bernard’s ear. There came a yell from Bernard and Mother’s voice from the window, saying, “Children, children.”

===Chinese===
"错过东西。什么？"
"错过听天大的新闻，"伯纳德说，还补上一声："啊哈！"
"什么新闻？"
"你们不是想知道吗？偏不告诉。"假期里的头一回探险被撇在了外头，伯纳德自然憋着气。换了谁都会憋气的。连你或者我都一样。
"快说，"弗朗西斯说着，一只手捏上了伯纳德的耳朵。伯纳德一声怪叫，窗口传来母亲的声音："孩子们，孩子们。"

===Original===
“All right, Mummy. Now, Bear﻿—don’t be a young rotter. What’s the news?”
“You’re hurting my ear,” was all Bernard’s rejoinder.
“All right,” said Francis, “we’ve got some news too. But we won’t tell, will we, Mavis?”
“Oh *don’t*,” said Kathleen, “don’t let’s be sneaky, the very first day too. It’s only that they’ve caught the Mermaid, and I’m afraid she’ll die in captivity, like you said. What’s yours?”
Francis had released Bernard’s ear and now he turned to Mavis.

===Chinese===
"没事儿，妈妈。好了，小熊（Bear）——别耍小无赖。什么新闻？"
"你把我的耳朵拧疼了。"伯纳德回敬的就这一句。
"行啦，"弗朗西斯说，"我们也有条新闻。可我们偏不说，对吧，梅维斯？"
"哦，*别这样*，"凯思琳说，"别搞鬼鬼祟祟那一套，还是头一天呢。不就是他们把美人鱼逮住了嘛，我怕她像你说的那样，被俘必死。你们的是什么？"
弗朗西斯早松开了伯纳德的耳朵，这时他转向梅维斯。

===Original===
“So that’s it,” he said slowly﻿—“who’s got her?”
“The circus people. What’s your news?” asked Kathleen eagerly.
“After brek,” said Francis. “Yes, Mother, half a sec! I apologize about the ear, Bernard. We will tell you all. Oh, it’s quite different from what you think. We meet and discuss the situation in the mill the minute we’re free from brek. Agreed? Right! Yes, Mother, coming!”

===Chinese===
"原来是这样。"他慢慢地说，"谁逮住她了？"
"马戏团的人。你们的新闻是什么？"凯思琳急切地问。
"吃完早饭再说，"弗朗西斯说，"哎，妈妈，马上来！我给耳朵的事道歉，伯纳德。我们会全告诉你们的。哦，这事跟你们想的完全不是一码事。等咱们从早饭里一解放出来，就到磨坊碰头，商议当前局势。同意？好！哎，妈妈，来了！"

===Original===
“Then there must,” Mavis whispered to Francis, “be two Mermaids. They can’t both be Sabrina﻿ ﻿… then which﻿ ﻿… ?”
“We’ve got to save one of them anyhow,” Francis answered with the light of big adventure in his eye, “*they die in captivity*.”

===Chinese===
"那么一定，"梅维斯悄悄对弗朗西斯说，"有两条美人鱼。她们总不会都是萨布丽娜……那么哪一条……？"
"不管怎样，咱们总得救出其中一条，"弗朗西斯答道，眼里闪着大冒险的光，"*她们被俘必死*。"

## III The Rescue / 三、营救

===Original===
The great question, of course, was﻿—Would Mother take them to the circus, or would she, if she wouldn’t herself take them, let them go alone? She had once, in Buckinghamshire, allowed them to go to a traveling menagerie, after exacting from them a promise that they were not to touch any of the animals, and they had seen reason to regret their promise when the showman offered to let them stroke his tame performing wolf, who was so very like a collie. When they had said, “No, thank you,” the showman had said, “Oh, frightened, are you? Run along home to Mammy then!” and the bystanders had laughed in a most insulting way. At a circus, of course, the horses and things aren’t near enough for you to stroke them, so this time they might not be asked to promise. If Mother came with them her presence, though agreeable, would certainly add to the difficulties, already quite enough﻿—as even Mavis could not but see﻿—of rescuing the Mermaid. But suppose Mother didn’t come with them.
“Suppose we have to promise we won’t touch any of the animals?” suggested Cathay. “You can’t rescue a person without touching it.”
“That’s just it,” said Mavis, “a Mermaid isn’t an animal. She’s a person.”
“But suppose it isn’t that sort of Mermaid,” said Bernard. “Suppose it’s the sort that other people call seals, like it said in the paper.”
“Well, it isn’t,” said Francis briefly, adding, “so there!”

===Chinese===
眼下最大的问题当然是——妈妈肯不肯带他们去看马戏？要是她不肯亲自带，肯不肯放他们自己去？从前在白金汉郡（Buckinghamshire），她有一次准他们去看一个流动动物园，条件是要他们保证不碰任何动物；后来卖艺人主动提出，让他们摸摸他那匹温驯的会表演的狼——那狼活脱脱像条牧羊犬——他们这才明白这个许诺有多难守。他们说“不了，谢谢”，卖艺人便说：“哦，害怕啦？那就快回家找妈妈去吧！”一旁看热闹的人还笑得极其无礼。在马戏场里，马呀什么的当然隔得没那么近，够不着去摸，所以这一回他们未必会被要求许诺。要是妈妈同去，她在场虽然叫人高兴，却肯定会给营救美人鱼这件事再添难处——这难处本来就已经够多了，连梅维斯（Mavis）也不能不看到。可是话又说回来，要是妈妈不同他们一起去呢。

“要是咱们得保证不碰任何动物呢？”凯西（Cathay）提议道。“要救一个人，总不能连碰都不碰一下吧。”

“问题就出在这儿，”梅维斯说，“美人鱼不是动物。她是人。”

“可要是她不是那一种美人鱼呢，”伯纳德（Bernard）说，“要是她是别人叫做海豹的那一种呢？报纸上就是这么说的嘛。”

“反正她不是。”弗朗西斯（Francis）言简意赅，还补上一句：“哼，就是这样！”

===Original===
They were talking in the front garden, leaning over the green gate while Mother upstairs unpacked the luggage that had been the mound with spades on top only yesterday, at Waterloo.
“Mavis!” Mother called through the open window. “I can only find﻿—but you’d better come up.”
“I ought to offer to help Mother unpack,” said Mavis, and went walking slowly.
She came back after a little while, however, quickly running.
“It’s all right,” she said. “Mother’s going to meet Daddy at the Junction this afternoon and buy us sunbonnets. And we’re to take our spades and go down to the sea till dinnertime﻿—it’s roast rabbit and apple dumps﻿—I asked Mrs. Pearce﻿—and we can go to the circus by ourselves﻿—and she never said a word about promise not to touch the animals.”

===Chinese===
他们是在前花园里说这些话的，个个趴在绿漆栅门上；楼上，妈妈正开箱收拾行李——就在昨天，这些行李在滑铁卢（Waterloo）车站还只是顶上插着几把铲子的一座小山呢。

“梅维斯！”妈妈隔着敞开的窗户喊道，“我只找得到——算了，你还是上楼来一趟吧。”

“我本来是该主动提出帮妈妈开箱的。”梅维斯说着，慢吞吞地上了楼。

不过没过一会儿，她就一路飞跑着回来了。

“都妥啦。”她说，“妈妈今天下午要去枢纽站接爸爸，顺便给我们买遮阳帽。我们呢，带上铲子，上海边一直玩到晚饭时候——晚饭是烤兔肉和苹果团子——我问过皮尔斯太太（Mrs. Pearce）了——马戏我们可以自己去——还有，关于不许碰动物的保证，她一个字也没提。”

===Original===
So off they went, down the white road where the yellowhammer was talking about himself as usual on the tree just beyond wherever you happened to be walking. And so to the beach.
Now, it is very difficult to care much about a Mermaid you have never seen or heard or touched. On the other hand, when once you have seen one and touched one and heard one speak, you seem to care for very little else. This was why when they got to the shore Kathleen and Bernard began at once to dig the moat of a sandcastle, while the elder ones walked up and down, dragging the new spades after them like some new kind of tail, and talking, talking, talking till Kathleen said they might help dig or the tide would be in before the castle was done.
“You don’t know what a lark sandcastles are, France,” she added kindly, “because you’ve never seen the sea before.”
So then they all dug and piled and patted and made molds of their pails to stand as towers to the castle and dug out dungeons and tunnels and bridges, only the roof always gave way in the end unless you had beaten the sand very tight beforehand. It was a glorious castle, though not quite finished when the first thin flat wash of the sea reached it. And then everyone worked twice as hard trying to keep the sea out till all was hopeless, and then everyone crowded into the castle and the sea washed it away bit by bit till there was only a shapeless island left, and everyone was wet through and had to change every single thing the minute they got home. You will know by that how much they enjoyed themselves.

===Chinese===
于是他们出发了，沿着白色的路面一路走下去；那只黄鹀呢，照例待在你走过的地方再往前一点儿的树上，絮絮叨叨地谈论着它自己。就这样，到了海边。

要知道，对一个你从没见过、没听见过、也没摸过的美人鱼，你是很难多么牵挂的。反过来，一旦你见过一条、摸过一条、听过一条说话，别的事你仿佛都顾不上了。正因为这样，到了岸边，凯思琳（Kathleen）和伯纳德马上动手给一座沙堡挖护城河，两个大的却在沙滩上踱来踱去，把新铲子拖在身后，活像拖着一条新式的尾巴，说呀，说呀，说个没完，直到凯思琳发话说，他们要么来帮忙挖，不然等城堡完工，潮水都要涨上来了。

“你还不知道堆沙堡有多好玩呢，弗兰，”她好心地补了一句，“因为你从前没见过海呀。”

于是大家一齐动手，挖呀，堆呀，拍呀，把小桶扣出模子来立在城堡上充作塔楼，又挖出地牢、隧道和桥梁——只是屋顶到头来总免不了塌，除非你事先把沙子捶得非常结实。那是一座了不起的城堡，可惜还没大功告成，海上第一片薄薄平平的潮水就漫过来了。于是人人加倍卖力，想方设法把海水挡在外面，直到彻底没了指望；接着大家全都挤进城堡里，海水把它一点一点冲垮，最后只剩下一座没个形状的小岛，而人人都湿了个透，一进家门就得从头到脚换掉每一件东西。从这儿你就能知道，他们玩得有多痛快。

===Original===
After the roast rabbit and the apple dumplings Mother started on the sunbonnet-and-meet-Daddy expedition. Francis went with her to the station and returned a little sad.
“I had to promise not to touch any of the animals,” he said. “And perhaps a Mermaid *is* an animal.”
“Not if she can speak,” said Kathleen. “I say, don’t you think we ought to wear our best things﻿—I do. It’s more respectable to the wonders of the deep. She’d like us to look beautiful.”
“I’m not going to change for anybody,” said Bernard firmly.
“All right, Bear,” said Mavis. “Only we will. Remember it’s magic.”

===Chinese===
吃过烤兔肉和苹果团子，妈妈便出发去办买遮阳帽兼接爸爸的差事。弗朗西斯陪她去了车站，回来时有点闷闷不乐。

“我不得不保证不碰任何动物。”他说，“说不定美人鱼*就是*动物呢。”

“她要是会说话，就不是。”凯思琳说，“我说，你们不觉得咱们该穿上最好的衣裳吗——我觉得该穿。对着深海的奇观，这样才够体面。她也乐意看到我们漂漂亮亮的。”

“我才不为任何人换衣服呢。”伯纳德斩钉截铁地说。

“好吧，小熊（Bear），”梅维斯说，“不过我们可要换。别忘了，这是魔法。”

===Original===
“I say, France,” he said, “do you think we *ought* to change?”
“No, I don’t,” Francis answered. “I don’t believe Mermaids care a bit what you’ve got on. You see, they don’t wear anything but tails and hair and looking glasses themselves. If there’s any beautifulness to be done they jolly well do it themselves. But I don’t say you wouldn’t be better for washing your hands again, and you might as well try to get *some* of the sand out of your hair. It looks like the wrong end of a broom as it is.”
He himself went so far as to put on the blue necktie that Aunt Amy had given him, and polished his silver watch chain on the inside of his jacket. This helped to pass the time till the girls were ready. At last this happened though they had put on their best things, and they started.

===Chinese===
“我说，弗兰，”他问，“你觉得咱们*该*换吗？”

“不觉得。”弗朗西斯回答，“我才不信美人鱼管你身上穿了什么呢。你想，她们自己除了尾巴、头发和小镜子，什么也不穿戴。要讲漂亮，她们自己就够漂亮了。不过我并不是说，你们再去洗洗手没好处；你头发里的沙子，也*多少*该抠出来一点儿。照现在这样，你的脑袋活像一把倒栽着的扫帚。”

他自己呢，居然郑重其事地系上了艾米阿姨（Aunt Amy）送他的蓝领带，还在外套襟内把银表链擦得锃亮。这倒帮着把等姑娘们打扮的工夫打发了。她们终于收拾停当——虽说真的换上了最好的衣裳——一行人便出发了。

===Original===
The yellowhammer went on about himself﻿—he was never tired of the subject.
“It’s just as if that bird was making fun of us,” Bernard said.
“I daresay it is a wild-goose step we’re taking,” said Kathleen; “but the circus will be jolly, anyhow.”
There is a piece of wasteland just beyond Beachfield on the least agreeable side of that village﻿—the side where the flat-faced shops are and the yellow brick houses. At the nice end of Beachfield the shops have little fat bow windows with greenish glass that you can hardly see through. Here also are gaunt hoardings plastered with tattered, ugly-colored posters, asking you in red to wear Ramsden’s Really Boots or to Vote for Wilton Ashby in blue. Some of the corners of the posters are always loose and flap dismally in the wind. There is always a good deal of straw and torn paper and dust at this end of the village, and bits of dirty rag, and old boots and tins are found under the hedges where flowers ought to be. Also there are a great many nettles and barbed wires instead of pleasant-colored fences. Don’t you sometimes wonder who is to blame for all the uglification of places that might be so pretty, and wish you could have a word with them and ask them not to? Perhaps when these people were little nobody told them how wrong it is to throw orange peel about, and the bits of paper off chocolate, and the paper bag which once concealed your bun. And it is a dreadful fact that the children who throw these things about are little uglifiers, and they grow up to be perfect monsters of uglification, and build hideous yellow brick cottages, and put up hoardings, and sell Ramsden’s Really Boots (in red), and vote passionately for Wilton Ashby (in blue), and care nothing for the fields that used to be green and the hedges where once flowers used to grow. Some people like this, and see nothing to hate in such ugly waste places as the one, at the wrong end of the town, where the fair was being held on that never-to-be-forgotten day when Francis, Mavis, Bernard and Kathleen set out in their best clothes to rescue the Mermaid because Mermaids “die in captivity.”

===Chinese===
黄鹀还在那儿谈论它自己——这个话题它是永远不腻的。

“这只鸟简直像在拿咱们开心。”伯纳德说。

“我看咱们这是在干一件没影儿的糊涂事，”凯思琳说，“不过不管怎么说，马戏总是好玩的。”

比奇菲尔德（Beachfield）外有一片荒地，正在村子最不顺眼的那一头——扁脸铺面和黄砖房子所在的那一头。在比奇菲尔德体面的那一头，铺子都有一扇扇胖墩墩的凸窗，嵌着绿莹莹的玻璃，几乎望不出去。在这一头，却立着一片瘦嶙嶙的广告牌，糊满破破烂烂、颜色恶俗的海报，红字劝你穿“拉姆斯登真靴子”（Ramsden’s Really Boots），蓝字劝你“投威尔顿·阿什比（Wilton Ashby）一票”。海报总有几个角翘着，在风里凄凄惨惨地拍打。村子这一头永远少不了稻草、碎纸和尘土，树篱底下——本该开花的地方——塞着脏布条、破靴子和空罐头盒。还有大片大片的荨麻和带刺铁丝，顶替了颜色悦目的栅篱。你难道从不纳闷，这些本可以那么可爱的地方被糟蹋成这样，该怪谁？难道就不想找他们说说，求他们别这么干？也许这些人小时候，没人告诉过他们，乱扔橘子皮、巧克力糖纸，还有装过圆面包的纸袋，是多么不应该。而一个可怕的事实是：乱扔这些玩意儿的孩子，就是小小的糟蹋精，长大了就长成十全十美的糟蹋大王，盖起丑陋的黄砖小屋，竖起广告牌，卖“拉姆斯登真靴子”（红字的），狂热地投威尔顿·阿什比的票（蓝字的），对从前绿茵茵的田野、对曾经开满鲜花的树篱，无动于衷。有些人偏偏就好这一口，在这种丑陋的荒地里——比如镇子不对头那一头的这一片——瞧不出半点可恨之处；就在那个永难忘怀的日子，弗朗西斯、梅维斯、伯纳德和凯思琳穿着最好的衣裳出发去营救美人鱼，因为美人鱼“被俘必死”，集市正办在那片荒地上。

===Original===
The fair had none of those stalls and booths which old-fashioned fairs used to have, where they sold toys, and gilt gingerbread, and carters’ whips, and cups and saucers, and mutton pies, and dolls, and china dogs, and shell boxes, and pincushions, and needle cases, and penholders with views of the Isle of Wight and Winchester Cathedral inside that you see so bright and plain when you put your eye close to the little round hole at the top.
The steam roundabouts were there﻿—but hardly a lean back of their spotted horses was covered by a rider. There were swings, but no one happened to be swinging. There were no shows, no menagerie, no boxing booth, no marionettes. No penny gaff with the spangled lady and the fat man who beats the drum. Nor were there any stalls. There were pink-and-white paper whips and bags of dust-colored minced paper﻿—the English substitute for confetti﻿—there were little metal tubes of dirty water to squirt in people’s faces, but except for the sale of these crude instruments for making other people uncomfortable there was not a stall in the fair. I give you my word, there was not a single thing that you could buy﻿—no gingerbread, no sweets, no crockery dogs, not even a halfpenny orange or a bag of nuts. Nor was there anything to drink﻿—not as much as a lemonade counter or a ginger beer stall. The revelers were no doubt drinking elsewhere. A tomblike silence reigned﻿—a silence which all the steam roundabout’s hideous hootings only emphasized.
A very dirty-nosed boy, overhearing a hurried council, volunteered the information that the circus had not yet opened.

===Chinese===
这集市可没有老式集市惯有的那些货摊和棚子——老式摊子上卖玩具、镀金姜饼、车把式的马鞭、杯子和碟子、羊肉馅饼、布娃娃、瓷狗、贝壳匣子、针插、针线盒，还有笔杆，笔杆顶上开着个小圆孔，把眼睛凑上去，里面的怀特岛（Isle of Wight）风光和温彻斯特大教堂（Winchester Cathedral）看得又鲜亮又真切。

蒸汽旋转木马倒是来了——可那些斑斑点点的小木马，一个个后仰的马背上几乎没骑着人。秋千有，只是碰巧没人在荡。没有杂耍戏台，没有兽棚，没有拳击棚，没有提线木偶戏。也没有亮片女郎配敲鼓胖子的那种一便士小剧场。货摊更是没有。有粉白相间的纸鞭子，有一袋袋土黄色的碎纸屑——英国人拿它顶替彩纸花——还有一管管装着脏水的小铁筒，专往人脸上滋；可除了出卖这些叫人不好受的粗劣玩意儿，整个集市上连一个货摊也没有。我向你担保，这里没有一件你买得到的东西——没有姜饼，没有糖果，没有瓷狗，连半便士的橘子或一袋坚果都没有。喝的也没有——连个柠檬水柜台、姜汁汽水摊都寻不见。寻欢作乐的人们想必在别处喝着呢。一片坟冢般的寂静笼罩全场——那蒸汽转马发出的讨厌呜咽，反倒把这寂静衬得更深了。

一个鼻子脏得可以的男孩，偷听到他们行色匆匆的商议，主动通风报信说，马戏还没开门。

===Original===
“Never mind,” they told each other﻿—and turned to the sideshows. These were all of one character﻿—the arrangement by which you throw something or roll something at something else, and if you hit the something you get a prize﻿—the sort of prize that is sold in Houndsditch at ninepence a gross.
Most of these arrangements are so ordered that to get a prize is impossible. For instance, a peculiarly offensive row of masks with open mouths in which pipes are set up.
