In the golden days of long ago if you hit a pipe it broke﻿—and you got a “prize” worth﻿—I can’t do sums﻿—put it briefly at the hundred and forty-fourth part of ninepence. But the children found that when their wooden ball struck the pipe it didn’t break. They wondered why! Then, looking more closely, they saw that the pipes were not of clay, but of painted wood. They could never be broken﻿—and the whole thing was a cruel mockery of hope.
The coconut-shy was not what it used to be either. Once one threw sticks, three shies a penny. Now it is a penny a shy, with light wooden balls. You can win a coconut if you happen to hit one that is not glued onto its support. If you really wish to win one of these unkindly fruits it is well to stand and watch a little and not to aim at those coconuts which, when they are hit, fail to fall off the sticks. Are they glued on? One hopes not. But if they are, who can wonder or reprove? It is hard to get a living, anyhow.

===Chinese===
“没关系。”他们互相打气——转身奔杂耍摊去了。这些摊子清一色是一个路数：你朝什么东西扔点什么、滚点什么过去，砸中了那什么东西，就得一份奖品——就是亨兹迪奇（Houndsditch）九便士一罗批发来的那种奖品。

这类把戏，大多数都布置得叫你得奖根本没门儿。比如有一排呲牙咧嘴的面具，特别气人，张开的嘴里立着一杆杆烟斗。在遥远往日的黄金时代，你砸中烟斗，它就碎了——你就得一件“奖品”，值多少——我算术不行——简而言之，就算它值九便士的一百四十四分之一吧。可孩子们发现，木球砸在烟斗上，烟斗竟不碎。他们纳闷这是为什么！再细看才看清，那些烟斗不是陶土烧的，是漆过的木头做的。永远砸不碎——整个儿是一场拿希望开涮的残忍把戏。

掷椰子摊也不复当年气象了。从前扔的是木棍，一便士扔三回；如今一回一便士，改扔轻飘飘的木球。你要是碰巧砸中一个没被粘死在支架上的椰子，就能把它赢走。你要是真想赢一个这种不肯成人之美的果子，最好先站在边上瞧一会儿，别去瞄准那些挨了砸却不肯从棍上掉下来的椰子。是粘上去的吗？但愿不是。可就算是，谁又怪得了谁、骂得了谁呢？反正讨生活不容易。

===Original===
There was one thing, though, that roused the children’s resentment﻿—chiefly, I think, because its owners were clean and did not look half-starved, so there was no barrier of pity between them and dislike﻿—a sort of round table sloping up to its center. On this small objects were arranged. For a penny you received two hoops. If you could throw a hoop over an object that object was yours. None of the rustic visitors to the fair could, it seemed, or cared to. It did not look difficult, however. Nor was it. At the first shot a tiny candlestick was encircled. Between pride and shame Mavis held out a hand.
“Hard luck,” said one of the two young women, too clean to be pitied. “Has to go flat on﻿—see?”
Francis tried again. This time the ring encircled a matchbox, “flat on.”
“Hard luck,” said the lady again.
“What’s the matter now?” the children asked, baffled.
“Hoop has to be red side up,” said she. So she scored. Now they went to the other side and had another penn’orth of hoops from the other too clean young woman. And the same thing happened. Only on the second winning she said:

===Chinese===
不过有一样东西，激起了孩子们的义愤——我想，主要因为摊主干干净净，也不像饿得半死的样子，怜悯心没插在中间挡着他们厌恶——那是一张往中心渐渐隆起的圆台。台上摆着些小物件。一便士换两个圈。你若能把圈套上一件东西，那东西就归你。来赶集的乡下客人，看样子是一个也套不中，要么就是懒得套。可这看着并不难。事实上也真不难。第一投，一只小小的烛台就被套住了。梅维斯又得意又惭愧，伸出了手。

“手气不好。”两个年轻女子里的一个说——她们干净得让人可怜不起来。“圈得平平地落上去——瞧见没？”

弗朗西斯又试了一回。这回圈套住了一只火柴盒，落得“平平的”。

“手气不好。”那位女士又说了一遍。

“这回又怎么了？”孩子们一愣，问道。

“圈得红面朝上。”她说。这一分算她赢了。于是他们转到摊子另一边，向另一个干净得让人可怜不起来的年轻女子又买了一便士的圈。同样的戏码重演了一遍。只是第二回套中时，她说：

===Original===
“Hard luck. Hoops have to be blue side up.”
It was Bernard’s blood that was up. He determined to clear the board.
“Blue side up, is it,” he said sternly, and took another penn’orth. This time he brought down a tin pin tray and a little box which, I hope, contained something. The girl hesitated and then handed over the prizes. “Another penn’orth of hoops,” said Bernard, warming to the work.
“Hard luck,” said she. “We don’t give more than two penn’orth to any one party.”

===Chinese===
“手气不好。圈得蓝面朝上。”

这下子伯纳德的血冲上了脑门。他打定主意，要把台面扫荡个干净。

“蓝面朝上，是吧。”他绷着脸说，又买了一便士的圈。这一回，他扫落了一只马口铁针盘和一个小盒子——但愿里头真装了点什么。姑娘迟疑了一下，还是把奖品交了出来。“再来一便士的圈。”伯纳德说着，干劲上来了。

这些奖品可不是你乐意留在家里的东西，连当战绩纪念都不配——何况你面前还压着营救美人鱼的大事呢。孩子们把奖品送给了一位围观的小姑娘，便朝打靶棚去了。那地方至少耍不了花招。你瞄准瓶子，打中了，瓶子就碎。再贪心自私的看摊人，也夺不走瓶子碎裂时那一声悦耳的叮当。哪怕家伙不济，瞄准瓶子打中它，也并非不可能。这话不假——可在这打靶棚，麻烦偏偏*不是*打不中瓶子。瓶子又多，又近。孩子们十四枪，打出了十三声脆响。瓶子挂在十五英尺开外，而不是三十英尺。为什么？集市的地面又不金贵——总不至于萨塞克斯（Sussex）人的枪法太潮，三十英尺对他们就算遥不可及了吧？

===Original===
The prizes were not the kind of things you care to keep, even as trophies of victory﻿—especially when you have before you the business of rescuing a Mermaid. The children gave their prizes to a small female bystander and went to the shooting gallery. That, at least, could have no nonsense about it. If you aimed at a bottle and hit it it would break. No sordid self-seeking custodian could rob you of the pleasant tinkling of the broken bottle. And even with a poor weapon it is not impossible to aim at a bottle and hit it. This is true﻿—but at the shooting gallery the trouble was *not* to hit the bottles. There were so many of them and they were so near. The children got thirteen tinkling smashes for their fourteen shots. The bottles were hung fifteen feet away instead of thirty. Why? Space is not valuable at the fair﻿—can it be that the people of Sussex are such poor shots that thirty feet is to them a prohibitive distance?
They did not throw for coconuts, nor did they ride on the little horses or pull themselves to dizzy heights in the swings. There was no heart left in them for such adventures﻿—and besides everyone in the fair, saving themselves and the small female bystander and the hoop girls, was dirtier than you would believe possible. I suppose Beachfield has a water supply. But you would have doubted it if you had been at the fair. They heard no laughter, no gay talk, no hearty give-and-take of holiday jests. A dull heavy silence brooded over the place, and you could hear that silence under the shallow insincere gaiety of the steam roundabout.
Laughter and song, music and good-fellowship, dancing and innocent revelry, there were none of these at Beachfield Fair. For music there was the steam roundabout’s echoes of the sordid musical comedy of the year before the year before last﻿—laughter there was not﻿—nor revelry﻿—only the dirty guardians of the machines for getting your pennies stood gloomily huddled, and a few groups of dejected girls and little boys shivered in the cold wind that had come up with the sunset. In that wind, too, danced the dust, the straw, the newspaper and the chocolate wrappers. The only dancing there was. The big tent that held the circus was at the top of the ground, and the people who were busy among the ropes and pegs and between the bright vans resting on their shafts seemed gayer and cleaner than the people who kept the little arrangements for people not to win prizes at. And now the circus at last was opened; the flap of the tent was pinned back, and a gypsy-looking woman, with oily black ringlets and eyes like bright black beads, came out at the side to take the money of those who wished to see the circus. People were now strolling toward it in twos and threes, and of these our four were the very first, and the gypsy woman took four warm sixpences from their four hands.

===Chinese===
他们没有去掷椰子，也没有去骑那些小马，更没有坐上秋千把自己荡到头晕眼花的高处。玩这些的心思是一点儿也不剩了——再说，集市上的人，除了他们自己、那个围观的小姑娘和套圈摊的两位姑娘，个个脏得超乎你的想象。我估摸比奇菲尔德总归是有自来水的。可你要是到过那集市，准会疑心这一点。他们听不见笑声，听不见快活的谈天，听不见假日玩笑里那种痛痛快快的你来我往。一片沉闷滞重的寂静伏在场子上，在蒸汽转马那浅薄虚伪的热闹底下，你听得见的，恰恰是这寂静。

笑声与歌唱，音乐与情谊，舞蹈与无邪的欢闹——比奇菲尔德集市上一样也没有。要说音乐，只有蒸汽转马放出的、大前年那出俗气歌舞喜剧的回声——笑声没有——欢闹也没有——只有那些替赚便士的机器看摊的、脏兮兮的人愁眉苦脸地挤作一团，还有几伙垂头丧气的姑娘和小男孩，在日落时刮起的冷风里打哆嗦。风里还舞着尘土、稻草、报纸和巧克力糖纸。全场唯一的舞蹈就是这个。装马戏的大帐篷扎在场地高处，那些在缆绳木桩间忙活、在支起车辕歇脚的漂亮篷车之间穿来穿去的人，看着倒比看守那些让人赢不着奖的小把戏的人快活些，也干净些。这时马戏总算开场了；帐篷的门帘用钉子别了回去，一个长得像吉卜赛人的女人从边上走出来，收想看马戏的人的钱——她一头油亮的黑卷发，一双眼睛像乌亮的小黑珠。人们开始三三两两朝那边溜达过去，我们的四个打头一个到，吉卜赛女人从四只小手里收走了四枚热乎乎的六便士。

“往里请，往里请，亲爱的小少爷小姑娘们，来看白象呀。”说话的是个蓄着黑唇髭的壮胖子，一身晚礼服——绿了吧唧的，接缝处还磨得发亮。他说话时挥着一条长鞭。孩子们虽然付过了六便士，还是站住了脚，要听听进去以后能看上什么。“白象——尾巴、长鼻子、象牙，样样齐全，只收六便士。瞧巴克特里亚骆驼，阿拉伯的沙漠之船——逮着机会就猛灌一气的海量酒客——横穿沙漠期间绝对滴酒不沾的禁酒模范。快来呀，快来呀。瞧受训狼与狼獾同跳盛大的万国旗帜舞。快来呀，快来呀，快来呀。瞧有学问的海豹，还有东方独一份的莲花姑娘，表演她那著名的无鞍马术，一人同骑三匹马，王公贵胄又惊又妒。快来瞧这条如假包换的美人鱼，就是昨天——千真万确——在你们自家海边捉到的。”

===Original===
“Walk in, walk in, my little dears, and see the white elephant,” said a stout, black-mustached man in evening dress﻿—greenish it was and shiny about the seams. He flourished a long whip as he spoke, and the children stopped, although they had paid their sixpences, to hear what they were to see when they did walk in. “The white elephant﻿—tail, trunk, and tusks all complete, sixpence only. See the Back Try A or Camels, or Ships of the Arabs﻿—heavy drinker when he gets the chance﻿—total abstainer while crossing the desert. Walk up, walk up. See the Trained Wolves and Wolverines in their great National Dance with the flags of all countries. Walk up, walk up, walk up. See the Educated Seals and the Unique Lotus of the Heast in her famous barebacked act, riding three horses at once, the wonder and envy of royalty. Walk up and see the very table Mermaid caught on your own coast only yesterday as ever was.”
“Thank you,” said Francis, “I think we will.” And the four went through the opened canvas into the pleasant yellow dusty twilight which was the inside of a squarish sort of tent, with an opening at the end, and through that opening you could see the sawdust-covered ring of the circus and benches all around it, and two men just finishing covering the front benches with red cotton strips.
“Where’s the Mermaid?” Mavis asked a little boy in tights and a spangled cap.

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，我们就去看。”弗朗西斯说。于是四个孩子从掀开的帆布钻了进去，钻进一片黄蒙蒙、灰扑扑的宜人幽暗里——那是一顶四四方方的帐篷的内部，顶头开着一个口子，从那口子望出去，看得见铺着锯末的马戏场子和围成一圈的条凳，还有两个人，正给前排条凳绑红布条，眼看就要绑完了。

“美人鱼在哪儿？”梅维斯问一个穿紧身衣、戴亮片帽的小男孩。

“在那儿。”他指着方帐篷侧面一扇小帆布门说。“不过我劝你们别碰她。凶着呢，她。抡尾巴乱甩——把李婆婆（Mother Lee）溅了个浑身是水，真的——还危险着呢：咱们的比尔想按住她，手腕子的骨头都给闹错位了。凑近看还得另加三便士。”

===Original===
“In there,” he said, pointing to a little canvas door at the side of the squarish tent. “I don’t advise you to touch her, though. Spiteful, she is. Lashes out with her tail﻿—splashed old Mother Lee all over water she did﻿—an’ dangerous too: our Bill ’e got ’is bone set out in his wrist a-trying to hold on to her. An’ it’s thruppence extry to see her close.”
There are times, as we all know, when threepence extra is a baffling obstacle﻿—a cruel barrier to desire, but this was not, fortunately, such a moment. The children had plenty of money, because Mother had given them two half-crowns between them to spend as they liked.
“Even then,” said Bernard, in allusion to the threepence extra, “we shall have two bob left.”
So Mavis, who was treasurer, paid over the extra threepences to a girl with hair as fair and lank as hemp, and a face as brown and round as a tea cake, who sat on a kitchen chair by the Mermaid door. Then one by one they went in through the narrow opening, and at last there they were alone in the little canvas room with a tank in it that held﻿—well, there was a large label, evidently written in a hurry, for the letters were badly made and arranged quite crookedly, and this label declared:

===Chinese===
谁都经历过这样的时刻：额外的三便士成了一道解不开的难关——一道拦住心愿的残酷关卡；所幸眼下不是那种时刻。孩子们的钱宽裕得很，因为妈妈给了他们合起来两枚半克朗，随他们高兴怎么花。

“就算这样，”伯纳德说——他指的是那额外的三便士——“咱们还能剩下两先令呢。”

于是当司库的梅维斯，把额外的三便士付给了一个姑娘——她头发像麻丝似的又淡又直，脸像茶点麦饼似的又圆又褐，就坐在美人鱼门口一张厨房椅上。然后他们一个跟着一个，从窄窄的开口钻进去，终于，他们独自站在了那间小帆布屋里，屋里立着一只水箱，里面盛着——这么说吧，水箱上贴着一张大标签，一望便知是匆匆忙忙写的，因为字母写得歪歪扭扭、排得七倒八歪，标签上宣告道：

===Original===
> Said To Be Fabulus, But Now True
> Caught Here
> Please Do Not Touch
> Dangerous

The little Spangled Boy had followed them in and pointed to the last word.
“What I tell you?” he asked proudly.
The children looked at each other. Nothing could be done with this witness at hand. At least.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
> 从前只当瞎编 如今眼见为实
> 本地捕获
> 请勿触摸
> 危险

那个小亮片男孩（Spangled Boy）也跟着他们进来了，指着最后一个词。

“我没说错吧？”他扬扬得意地问。

孩子们面面相觑。有这位见证人在跟前，什么都干不成。至少——眼下还不行……

===Original===
“Perhaps if it’s going to be magic,” Mavis whispered to Francis, “outsiders wouldn’t notice. They don’t sometimes﻿—I believe. Suppose you just said a bit of ‘Sabrina’ to start the magic.”
“Wouldn’t be safe,” Francis returned in the same low tones. “Suppose he *wasn’t* an outsider, and *did* notice.”
So there they stood helpless. What the label was hung on was a large zinc tank﻿—the kind that they have at the tops of houses for the water supply﻿—you must have seen one yourself often when the pipes burst in frosty weather, and your father goes up into the roof of the house with a candle and pail, and the water drips through the ceilings and the plumber is sent for, and comes when it suits him. The tank was full of water and at the bottom of it could be seen a mass of something dark that looked as if it were partly browny-green fish and partly greeny-brown seaweed.

===Chinese===
“也许——要是这就快要是魔法了呢，”梅维斯悄悄对弗朗西斯说，“外人也未必会察觉。有时候他们就是不察觉——我相信。要不，你先念一小句‘萨布丽娜’（Sabrina），把魔法发动起来。”

“那可不稳妥。”弗朗西斯用同样低的声调回答。“万一他*不是*外人，*偏偏*察觉到了呢。”

于是他们束手无策地站在那里。挂着标签的是一只大锌皮水箱——就是屋顶上储水用的那一种——水管冻裂的严寒天，你一定常常见到它：你爸爸举着蜡烛、提着水桶爬上屋顶，水顺着天花板滴答漏下来，水管工被人去请，他什么时候得空什么时候才来。水箱里满是水，水底能看见一团黑乎乎的东西，瞧着像一半是褐绿的鱼，一半是绿褐的海藻。

===Original===
“Sabrina fair,” Francis suddenly whispered, “send him away.”
And immediately a voice from outside called “Rube﻿—Reuben﻿—drat the boy, where’s he got to?”﻿—and the little spangled intruder had to go.
“There, now,” said Mavis, “if *that* isn’t magic!” Perhaps it was, but still the dark fish-and-seaweed heap in the tank had not stirred. “Say it all through,” said Mavis.
“Yes, do,” said Bernard, “then we shall know for certain whether it’s a seal or not.”
So once again﻿—

===Chinese===
“美丽的萨布丽娜，”弗朗西斯突然悄声念道，“把他打发走吧。”

话音未落，外面立刻响起一个声音：“鲁布——鲁本（Reuben）——这该死的小子，又钻到哪儿去了？”——那位亮片小闯入者只好去了。

“瞧见没有，”梅维斯说，“这*要不是*魔法才怪呢！”也许真是魔法，可水箱里那团鱼和海藻的黑影仍旧一动没动。“把它从头到尾念完。”梅维斯说。

“对，念吧，”伯纳德说，“这样咱们就能确定，她到底是不是海豹了。”

于是，又念了一遍——

===Original===
> “ ‘Sabrina fair,
 

 Listen where thou art sitting,
 

 Under the glassie, cool, translucent wave﻿—’ ”

He got no further. There was a heaving and stirring of the seaweed and fish tail, something gleamed white, through the brown something white parted the seaweed, two white hands parted it, and a face came to the surface of the rather dirty water and﻿—there was no doubt about it﻿—spoke.
“ ‘Translucent wave,’ indeed!” was what the face said. “I wonder you’re not ashamed to speak the invocation over a miserable cistern like this. What do you want?”
Brown hair and seaweed still veiled most of the face, but all the children, who, after their first start back had pressed close to the tank again, could see that the face looked exceedingly cross.

===Chinese===
> “‘美丽的萨布丽娜，

> 请听，你此刻正坐在

> 那晶莹、清凉、透明的波浪之下——’”

他没能再往下念。海藻和鱼尾一阵翻腾，有什么东西白光一闪，一团白色从褐色中分开海藻，是两只白手拨开了它，一张脸浮上了那相当之脏的水面——而且——这一点错不了——开了口。

“还‘透明的波浪’呢！”那张脸说道。“在这么个寒酸的蓄水槽上头念召咒，亏你们不害臊。你们要什么？”

褐色的头发和海藻仍旧罩着那张脸的大半，可孩子们——起初吓得倒退了一步，随后又紧紧围到水箱边上——个个都看得出，那张脸看上去气得不得了。

===Original===
“We want,” said Francis in a voice that would tremble though he told himself again and again that he was not a baby and wasn’t going to behave like one﻿—“we want to help you.”
“Help *me*? You?” She raised herself a little more in the tank and looked contemptuously at them. “Why, don’t you know that I am mistress of all water magic? I can raise a storm that will sweep away this horrible place and my detestable captors and you with them, and carry me on the back of a great wave down to the depths of the sea.”
“Then why on earth don’t you?” Bernard asked.
“Well, I was thinking about it,” she said, a little awkwardly, “when you interrupted with your spells. Well, you’ve called and I’ve answered﻿—now tell me what I can do for you.”

===Chinese===
“我们想，”弗朗西斯开口说，嗓音抖个不停，虽然他一遍又一遍地告诉自己，自己不是小娃娃，也决不打算做出小娃娃的德行——“我们想帮助你。”

“帮助*我*？就凭你们？”她在水箱里又把自己撑高了一点，轻蔑地扫了他们一眼。“怎么，你们不知道我是一切水魔法的主宰么？我唤得起一场风暴，把这可怕的地方、把我那些可恨的捕捉者，连同你们一起统统刮走，再驮着我踏上一个巨浪的浪背，直下大海深处。”

“那你究竟为什么不动手？”伯纳德问。

“这个嘛，我正盘算着呢，”她说，口气有点发窘，“你们就用咒语把我打断了。好吧，你们唤了，我应了——现在说吧，我能为你们做点什么？”

===Original===
“We’ve told you,” said Mavis gently enough, though she was frightfully disappointed that the Mermaid after having in the handsomest manner turned out to be a Mermaid, should be such a very short-tempered one. And when they had talked about her all day and paid the threepence each extra to see her close, and put on their best white dresses too. “We’ve told you﻿—we want to help you. Another Sabrina in the sea told us to. *She* didn’t tell us anything about you being a magic-mistress. She just said ‘they die in captivity.’ ”
“Well, thank you for coming,” said the Mermaid. “If she really said that it must be one of two things﻿—either the sun is in the House of Liber﻿—which is impossible at this time of the year﻿—or else the rope I was caught with must be made of llama’s hair, and *that’s* impossible in these latitudes. Do you know anything about the rope they caught me with?”
“No,” said Bernard and Kathleen. But the others said, “It was a lariat.”
“Ah,” said the Mermaid, “my worst fears are confirmed﻿—But who could have expected a lariat on these shores? But that must have been it. Now I know why, though I have been on the point of working the magic of the Great Storm at least five hundred times since my capture, some unseen influence has always held me back.”

===Chinese===
“我们已经告诉你了。”梅维斯的口气倒是够温和的，尽管她失望透顶：这位美人鱼既然以最漂亮的姿态证明了自己真是一条美人鱼，脾气却偏偏这么坏。何况他们念叨了她一整天，每人多付三便士凑近看了她，还穿上了最好的白裙子。“我们告诉你了——我们想帮助你。是大海里另一位萨布丽娜吩咐我们这么做的。*她*可一个字也没提过你是什么魔法女主宰。她只说了一句：‘她们被俘必死。’”

“好吧，谢谢你们跑这一趟。”美人鱼说。“她要是真这么说了，那必定是两件事里的一件——要么太阳正在天秤宫里——这在一年里的这个节令是不可能的——要么，捉我的那条绳子必定是羊驼毛搓的，而*这*在咱们这个纬度也是不可能的。他们捉我用的绳子，你们知道点儿什么吗？”

“不知道。”伯纳德和凯思琳说。可另外两个说：“那是条套索。”

“啊，”美人鱼说，“我最坏的担忧得到证实了——可是，谁能想得到这片海岸上会冒出套索来呢？可必定就是这么回事。这下我明白了，为什么自打被俘，起码有五百次我都眼看要施展大风暴的魔法了，却总有一股看不见的力量拽住我。”

===Original===
“You mean,” said Bernard, “you feel that it wouldn’t work, so you didn’t try.”
A rattling, ripping sound outside, beginning softly, waxed louder and louder so as almost to drown their voices. It was the drum, and it announced the beginning of the circus. The Spangled Child put his head in and said, “Hurry up or you’ll miss my Infant Prodigious Act on the Horse with the Tambourines,” and took his head out again.
“Oh, dear,” said Mavis, “and we haven’t arranged a single thing about rescuing you.”
“No more you have,” said the Mermaid carelessly.

===Chinese===
“你的意思是，”伯纳德说，“你心里有数，知道施了也不灵，所以干脆没试。”

外面响起一阵咔啦撕裂般的声音，起先轻轻的，越滚越响，响得几乎盖过他们说话。是鼓。它宣告马戏开演了。亮片男孩把头探进来，说：“快点，不然就误了我的‘神童惊人’绝技——铃鼓马表演了。”说完又把头缩了回去。

“哎呀，”梅维斯说，“营救你的事，咱们还一件都没商量定呢。”

“可不是嘛。”美人鱼漫不经心地说。

===Original===
“Look here,” said Francis, “you do *want* to be rescued, don’t you?”
“Of course I do,” replied the Mermaid impatiently, “now I know about the llama rope. But I can’t walk even if they’d let me, and you couldn’t carry me. Couldn’t you come at dead of night with a chariot﻿—I could lift myself into it with your aid﻿—then you could drive swiftly hence, and driving into the sea I could drop from the chariot and escape while you swam ashore.”
“I don’t believe we could﻿—any of it,” said Bernard, “let alone swimming ashore with horses and chariots. Why, Pharaoh himself couldn’t do that, you know.” And even Mavis and Francis added helplessly, “I don’t see how we’re to get a chariot,” and “do you think of some other way.”
“I shall await you,” said the lady in the tank with perfect calmness, “at dead of night.”
With that she twisted the seaweed closely around her head and shoulders and sank slowly to the bottom of the tank. And the children were left staring blankly at each other, while in the circus tent music sounded and the soft heavy pad-pad of hoofs on sawdust.

===Chinese===
“我说，”弗朗西斯道，“你*想*被救出去的，对不对？”

“我当然想。”美人鱼不耐烦地答道，“如今我知道了羊驼毛绳这回事。可就算他们肯放我，我也走不了路，你们也抬不动我。你们能不能半夜三更赶一辆战车来——有你们搭把手，我自己能撑上去——然后你们飞快驶离此地，一路赶进海里，我从战车上一翻身就逃脱了，你们再游上岸。”

“我看我们哪样也办不成。”伯纳德说，“更别提赶着马匹战车游上岸了。要知道，连法老（Pharaoh）本人都办不到呢。”连梅维斯和弗朗西斯也束手无策地一人一句：“我看不出咱们上哪儿弄战车去。”“你还是想个别的主意吧。”

“我恭候你们。”水箱里的女士以十足的平静说道，“半夜三更。”

说罢，她把海藻紧紧密密地缠在头上肩上，缓缓沉到了水箱底。孩子们被撂在那里，茫茫然你瞪我、我瞪你；马戏棚里乐声悠扬，还有马蹄踩在锯末上那又软又沉的噗噗声。

===Original===
“What shall we do?” Francis broke the silence.
“Go and see the circus, of course,” said Bernard.
“Of course we can talk about the chariot afterward,” Mavis admitted.
“There’ll be lots of time to talk between now and dead of night,” said Kathleen. “Come on, Bear.”
And they went.

===Chinese===
“咱们怎么办？”弗朗西斯打破了沉默。

“当然是去看马戏。”伯纳德说。

“战车的事，当然可以回头再商量。”梅维斯承认。

“从现在到半夜三更，有的是工夫商量。”凯思琳说，“走吧，小熊。”

于是他们去了。

===Original===
There is nothing like a circus for making you forget your anxieties. It is impossible to dwell on your troubles and difficulties when performing dogs are displaying their accomplishments, and wolves dancing their celebrated dance with the flags of all nations, and the engaging lady who jumps through the paper hoops and comes down miraculously on the flat back of the white horse, cannot but drive dull care away, especially from the minds of the young. So that for an hour and a half﻿—it really was a good circus, and I can’t think how it happened to be at Beachfield Fair at all﻿—a solid slab of breathless enjoyment was wedged in between the interview with the Mermaid and the difficult task of procuring for her the chariot she wanted. But when it was all over and they were part of a hot, tightly packed crowd pouring out of the dusty tent into the sunshine, their responsibilities came upon them with renewed force.
“Wasn’t the clown ripping?” said Bernard, as they got free of the crowd.
“I liked the riding-habit lady best, and the horse that went like that, best,” said Kathleen, trying with small pale hands and brown shod legs to give an example of a horse’s conduct during an exhibition of the haute école.
“Didn’t you think the elephant﻿—” Mavis was beginning, when Francis interrupted her.
“About that chariot,” he said, and after that they talked of nothing else. And whatever they said it always came to this in the end, that they hadn’t got a chariot, and couldn’t get a chariot, and that anyhow they didn’t suppose there was a chariot to be got, at any rate in Beachfield.

===Chinese===
天下再没有比马戏更叫人忘掉心事的东西了。表演狗正在卖弄浑身本事，狼们挥着万国旗跳它们那出了名的舞，还有那位讨人喜欢的女郎纵身钻过纸圈，又不可思议地稳稳落在白马平坦的背上——看着这些，你没法再惦记自己的烦恼和难处；闷沉沉的忧虑不能不被赶跑，尤其是从年轻人的心里。于是，一个半钟头里——那真是一场好马戏，我简直想不通它怎么会上比奇菲尔德的集市来——一整块屏住呼吸的快乐，结结实实地嵌在了会见美人鱼与为她张罗战车这桩难事之间。可等一切都完了，他们随着又热又挤、密不透风的人流，从满是尘土的帐篷涌到太阳底下，肩上的担子便以加倍的力量压了回来。

“小丑带劲不带劲？”挤出人群后，伯纳德说。

“我最喜欢骑装小姐，还有那匹这么走步的马，最喜欢。”凯思琳说，一边用小小的白手和穿棕色鞋的腿，示范高等马术表演中一匹马的做派。

“你们不觉得那头象——”梅维斯刚起了个头，弗朗西斯就把她打断了。

“说说那辆战车吧。”他说。打那以后，他们谈的就没别的了。而且不管怎么起头，到头来总落到这一条上：他们没有战车，弄不到战车，横竖他们也不信有战车可弄——至少在比奇菲尔德弄不到。

===Original===
“It wouldn’t be any good, I suppose,” said Kathleen’s last and most helpful suggestion﻿—“be the slightest good saying ‘Sabrina fair’ to a pumpkin?”
“We haven’t got even a pumpkin,” Bernard reminded her, “let alone the rats and mice and lizards that Cinderella had. No, that’s no good. But I’ll tell you what.” He stopped short. They were near home now﻿—it was late afternoon, in the road where the talkative yellowhammer lived. “What about a wheelbarrow?”
“Not big enough,” said Francis.
“There’s an extra big one in the mill,” said Bernard. “Now, look here. I’m not any good at magic. But Uncle Tom said I was a born general. If I tell you exactly what to do, will you two do it, and let Cathay and me off going?”

===Chinese===
“我估摸那也白搭，”这是凯思琳最后也是最顶用的主意——“对着一只南瓜念‘美丽的萨布丽娜’，能顶半点儿用吗？”

“咱们连一只南瓜都没有，”伯纳德提醒她，“更别提灰姑娘有的那些老鼠、田鼠和蜥蜴了。不行，那不管用。不过我倒有个主意。”他猛地收住了话头。这时他们快到家了——已是傍晚，正走在那只多嘴多舌的黄鹀住的那条路上。“用独轮手推车，怎么样？”

“不够大。”弗朗西斯说。

“磨坊里有一辆特大号的。”伯纳德说，“听着。我搞魔法是不成的。可汤姆叔叔（Uncle Tom）说过，我是天生的将军。要是我把该干的事一条条给你们交代清楚，你们俩去干，放我和凯西免了这趟，成不成？”

===Original===
“Going to sneak out of it?” Francis asked bitterly.
“It isn’t. It’s not my game at all, and I don’t want to play. And if I do, the whole thing will be muffed﻿—you know it will. I’m so unlucky. You’d never get out at dead of night without me dropping a boot on the stairs or sneezing﻿—you know you wouldn’t.”
Bernard took a sort of melancholy pride in being the kind of boy who always gets caught. If you are that sort of boy, perhaps that’s the best way to take it. And Francis could not deny that there was something in what he said. He went on: “Then Kathleen’s my special sister and I’m not going to have her dragged into a row.” (“I want to,” Kathleen put in ungratefully.) “So will you and Mavis do it on your own or not?”
After some discussion, in which Kathleen was tactfully dealt with, it was agreed that they would. Then Bernard unfolded his plan of campaign.
“Directly we get home,” he said, “we’ll begin larking about with that old wheelbarrow﻿—giving each other rides, and so on, and when it’s time to go in we’ll leave it at the far end of the field behind the old sheep hut near the gate. Then it’ll be handy for you at dead of night. You must take towels or something and tie around the wheel so that it doesn’t make a row. You can sleep with my toy alarm under your pillow and it won’t wake anyone but you. You get out through the dining room window and in the same way. I’ll lend you my new knife, with three blades and a corkscrew, if you’ll take care of it, to cut the canvas, and go by the back lane that comes out behind where the circus is, but if you took my advice you wouldn’t go at all. She’s not a nice Mermaid at all. I’d rather have had a seal, any day. Hullo, there’s Daddy and Mother. Come on.”

===Chinese===
“想溜号？”弗朗西斯挖苦地问。

“才不是溜号。这压根儿不是我的项目，我不想玩。而且只要有我，整件事准砸——你们自己也清楚。我晦气透了。你们半夜三更想溜出去，有我在，不是在楼梯上掉一只靴子，就是打个喷嚏——你们心里明白，错不了。”

伯纳德天生就是那种回回都要被抓个正着的男孩，他对此还怀着一种忧郁的自豪。你要是那种男孩，这样想也许就是最好的自处之道。弗朗西斯也无法否认他的话有几分道理。他接着说：“还有，凯思琳是我特别的妹妹，我不能眼看她被拖进一场乱子。”（“我愿意去。”凯思琳不领情地插了一句。）“那么，你和梅维斯自己去干，还是不干？”

又商量了一阵——凯思琳被婉转周到地安顿了——大家一致同意：干。于是伯纳德摊开了他的作战计划。

“咱们一到家，”他说，“就开始拿那辆旧手推车撒欢儿——你推我坐、我推你坐，什么的；该进屋的时候，就把它留在田那头，大门旁边旧羊棚的后头。这样你们半夜三更用起来就顺手。你们得带上毛巾什么的，绑在轮子上，省得它吱嘎乱响。可以带着我的玩具闹钟睡，搁在枕头底下，到时候除了你谁也吵不醒。出去走饭厅的窗户，回来也走那儿。我把我的新折刀借给你们——三个刀片，还带一个开塞钻——只要你们肯爱惜——用来割帆布。走后头那条小巷，出口正开在马戏场后身。不过，你们要是肯听我的，干脆就别去。她压根儿不是条好美人鱼。换了我，哪天都宁可要一头海豹。哟，爸爸和妈妈来了。走。”

===Original===
They came on.
The program sketched by Bernard was carried out without a hitch. Everything went well, only Francis and Mavis were both astonished to find themselves much more frightened than they had expected to be. Any really great adventure like the rescuing of a Mermaid does always look so very much more serious when you carry it out, at night, than it did when you were planning it in the daytime. Also, though they knew they were not doing anything wrong, they had an uncomfortable feeling that Mother and Daddy might not agree with them on that point. And of course they could not ask leave to go and rescue a Mermaid, with a chariot, at dead of night. It is not the sort of thing you can ask leave to do, somehow. And the more you explained your reasons the less grown-up people would think you fit to conduct such an expedition.
Francis lay down fully dressed, under his nightshirt. And Mavis under hers wore her short blue skirt and jersey. The alarm, true to its trust, went off into an earsplitting whizz and bang under the pillow of Francis, but no one else heard it. He crept cautiously into Mavis’s room and wakened her, and as they crept down in stockinged feet not a board creaked. The French window opened without noise, the wheelbarrow was where they had left it, and they had fortunately brought quite enough string to bind wads of towels and stockings to the tire of its wheel. Also they had not forgotten the knife.
The wheelbarrow was heavy and they rather shrank from imagining how much heavier it would be when the discontented Mermaid was curled up in it. However, they took it in turns, and got along all right by the back lane that comes out above the waste ground where Beachfield holds its fairs.
“I hope the night’s dead enough,” Mavis whispered as the circus came in sight, looking very white in the starlight, “it’s nearly two by now I should think.”

===Chinese===
他们走了过去。

伯纳德草拟的方案，执行起来一环不差。样样顺当，只有一桩：弗朗西斯和梅维斯都吃惊地发现，自己比原先料想的害怕得多。任何真正了不起的冒险，比如营救一条美人鱼，夜里当真去做的时候，总比白天做计划的时候显得沉重得多。再说，虽然他们知道自己没干什么坏事，心里却总有一丝不安，觉得妈妈和爸爸未必同意这个看法。当然，半夜三更驾着战车去营救美人鱼，这种事是没法开口请假的。不知怎么，它就不是那种可以请求许可的事。而且你的理由摆得越多，大人们越会觉得你指挥不了这样的远征。

弗朗西斯和衣躺下，睡衣底下穿得齐齐整整。梅维斯的睡衣底下，穿的是蓝短裙和针织衫。闹钟不负所托，在弗朗西斯的枕头底下爆出一声刺耳的尖啸炸响，可除他之外谁也没听见。他小心翼翼地溜进梅维斯的房间叫醒她，两人穿着袜子往楼下摸，一路没一块地板出声。落地窗无声地开了，手推车还在原处，而且幸亏他们带的绳子绰绰有余，足够把一团团毛巾和长袜绑上车轮的轮胎。刀，也没有忘。

手推车本身就沉，想到那位一肚子不情愿的美人鱼蜷进去以后还得重上多少，他们简直不敢深想。不过他们轮换着推，顺着后巷一路顺当——那条后巷的出口，就在比奇菲尔德办集市的荒地坡上头。

“但愿今夜够死寂。”马戏场望得见了，星光下白蒙蒙的，梅维斯悄声说，“我琢磨着，这会儿快两点了。”

===Original===
“Quite dead enough, if that’s all,” said Francis; “but suppose the gypsies are awake? They do sit up to study astronomy to tell fortunes with, don’t they? Suppose this is their astronomy night? I vote we leave the barrow here and go and reconnoiter.”
They did. Their sandshoes made no noise on the dewy grass, and treading very carefully, on tiptoe, they came to the tent. Francis nearly tumbled over a guy rope; he just saw it in time to avoid it.
“If I’d been Bernard I should have come a beastly noisy cropper over that,” he told himself. They crept around the tent till they came to the little square bulge that marked the place where the tank was and the seaweed and the Mermaid.
“They die in captivity, they die in captivity, they die in captivity,” Mavis kept repeating to herself, trying to keep up her courage by reminding herself of the desperately urgent nature of the adventure. “It’s a matter of life and death,” she told herself﻿—“life and death.”
And now they picked their way between the pegs and guy ropes and came quite close to the canvas. Doubts of the strength and silence of the knife possessed the trembling soul of Francis. Mavis’s heart was beating so thickly that, as she said afterward, she could hardly hear herself think. She scratched gently on the canvas, while Francis felt for the knife with the three blades and the corkscrew. An answering signal from the imprisoned Mermaid would, she felt, give her fresh confidence. There was no answering scratch. Instead, a dark line appeared to run up the canvas﻿—it was an opening made by the two hands of the Mermaid which held back the two halves of the tent side, cut neatly from top to bottom. Her white face peered out.

===Chinese===
“单说这一条，那够死寂的了。”弗朗西斯说，“可万一吉卜赛人还醒着呢？他们不是常熬夜钻研天文，好拿去算命么？万一今夜正是他们的观星夜呢？我提议把车撂在这儿，咱们先去侦察一番。”

他们照办了。帆布鞋踩着带露的草，悄然无声；他们踮起脚尖，一步一留神，摸到了帐篷跟前。弗朗西斯差点绊上一根拉绳，好在及时看见，躲了过去。

“换了我伯纳德，非在那上头栽个惊天动地的大跟头不可。”他对自己说。他们绕着帐篷匍匐过去，一直挪到那一小块方方的鼓包跟前——那底下正是水箱、海藻和美人鱼的所在。

“她们被俘必死，她们被俘必死，她们被俘必死。”梅维斯在心里一遍又一遍地默念，靠默想这次冒险十万火急的性质，给自己撑着胆子。“这是人命关天的大事。”她告诉自己——“人命关天。”

这时他们在木桩和拉绳间择着路，挨到了帆布跟前。弗朗西斯那颗发抖的心里，满是对那把刀够不够结实、够不够无声的疑虑。梅维斯的心跳得又沉又急，照她后来自己说的，她连自己的念头都快听不见了。她轻轻在帆布上挠了挠，弗朗西斯则摸出那把三刃带开塞钻的折刀。她想，被囚的美人鱼若能回个暗号，她便能重拾几分信心。可没有等到回应的挠声。反倒是一道黑线顺着帆布向上绽开——那是美人鱼的两只手扒开的一道口子，帐篷壁被从上到下整整齐齐割成了两半，两片都拢在她手里。她那张白脸探了出来。

===Original===
“Where is the chariot?” she asked in the softest of whispers, but not too soft to carry to the children the feeling that she was, if possible, crosser than ever.
Francis was afraid to answer. He knew that his voice could never be subdued to anything as soft as the voice that questioned him, a voice like the sound of tiny waves on a summer night, like the whisper of wheat when the wind passes through it on a summer morning. But he pointed toward the lane where they had left the wheelbarrow and he and Mavis crept away to fetch it.
As they wheeled it down the waste place both felt how much they owed to Bernard. But for his idea of muffling the wheel they could never have got the clumsy great thing down that bumpy uneven slope. But as it was they and the barrow stole toward the gypsy’s tent as silently as the Arabs in the poem stole away with theirs, and they wheeled it close to the riven tent side. Then Mavis scratched again, and again the tent opened.
“Have you any cords?” the soft voice whispered, and Francis pulled what was left of the string from his pocket.
She had made two holes in the tent side, and now passing the string through these she tied back the flaps of the tent.

===Chinese===
“战车在哪儿？”她用最轻最轻的耳语问——可这耳语又不至于轻到让孩子们听不出：要是还能更生气的话，她比先前气得更厉害了。

弗朗西斯不敢答话。他知道自己的嗓门，怎么压也压不成问话人那种轻——那声音像夏夜里细浪的声响，像夏日清晨风穿过麦田时麦穗的低语。他朝撂着手推车的小巷指了指，同梅维斯蹑手蹑脚地回去取车。

两人推着车下荒坡时，都觉得欠了伯纳德的情。要不是他想出给轮子裹上东西的主意，这笨重的庞然大物，他们根本别想弄下那道坑坑洼洼的陡坡。亏得如此，他们和车子才像诗里的阿拉伯人悄悄卷走帐篷那样，悄没声儿地溜到了吉卜赛人的帐篷跟前，把车推到了割开的帐篷壁边。梅维斯又挠了挠，帐篷又一次张开了。

“有带子吗？”那轻柔的声音低声问。弗朗西斯从口袋里掏出剩下的绳子。

她已在帐篷壁上戳了两个洞，这时把绳子穿过去，把两片帘子拴向了一边。

===Original===
“Now,” she said, raising herself in the tank and resting her hands on its side. “You must both help﻿—take hold of my tail and lift. Creep in﻿—one on each side.”
It was a wet, sloppy, slippery, heavy business, and Mavis thought her arms would break, but she kept saying: “Die in captivity,” and just as she was feeling that she could not bear it another minute the strain slackened and there was the Mermaid curled up in the barrow.
“Now,” said the soft voice, “go﻿—quickly.”
It was all very well to say go quickly. It was as much as the two children could do, with that barrow-load of dripping Mermaid, to go at all. And very, very slowly they crept up the waste space. In the lane, under cover of the tall hedges, they paused.
“Go on,” said the Mermaid.

===Chinese===
“好了。”她说着，在水箱里撑起身子，双手搭上箱沿。“你们俩都得搭手——抓住我的尾巴，往上抬。进来吧——一边一个。”

这是一桩湿漉漉、黏答答、滑溜溜、死沉死沉的活儿。梅维斯觉得两条胳膊都要折了，可她嘴里一直念着：“被俘必死。”就在她觉得自己再撑一分钟也撑不住的当口，那份重量忽然一松——美人鱼已经蜷进车里了。

“好了，”那轻柔的声音说，“走——快走。”

说快走倒轻巧。车上载着那位滴滴答答直淌水的美人鱼，两个孩子能往前挪，就已使出了浑身解数。他们一点儿、一点儿，非常非常慢地蹭上了那片荒地。到了小巷里，躲进高高的树篱掩护下，他们停住了。

“接着走。”美人鱼说。

===Original===
“We can’t till we’ve rested a bit,” said Mavis, panting. “How did you manage to get that canvas cut?”
“My shell knife, of course,” said the person in the wheelbarrow. “We always carry one in our hair, in case of sharks.”
“I see,” said Francis, breathing heavily.
“You had much better go on,” said the barrow’s occupant. “This chariot is excessively uncomfortable and much too small. Besides, delays are dangerous.”
“We’ll go in half a sec,” said Francis, and Mavis added kindly:

===Chinese===
“不歇口气儿，我们走不动了。”梅维斯喘着气说。“那帆布，你是怎么割开的？”

“自然是使我的贝壳刀。”手推车里那位说。“我们总在头发里别一把，防着鲨鱼。”

“哦。”弗朗西斯说，喘得粗粗的。

“你们还是快些走的好。”车里那位说。“这辆战车硌得难受极了，也太小。再说，夜长梦多。”

“这就走，一会儿就走。”弗朗西斯说。梅维斯还好心好意地添了一句：

===Original===
“You’re really quite safe now, you know.”
“*You* aren’t,” said the Mermaid. “I don’t know whether you realize that I’m stolen property and that it will be extremely awkward for you if you are caught with me.”
“But we shan’t be caught with you,” said Mavis hopefully.
“Everybody’s sound asleep,” said Francis. It was wonderful how brave and confident they felt now that the deed was done. “It’s perfectly safe﻿—Oh, what’s that! Oh!”
A hand had shot from the black shadow of the hedge and caught him by the arm.

===Chinese===
“你如今真的很安全了，你知道吧。”

“*你*们可不安全。”美人鱼说。“你们是否明白，我如今是赃物，你们要是跟我一块儿被逮住，那可就难堪透顶了。”

“可我们不会跟你一块儿被逮住的。”梅维斯满怀希望地说。

“人人都睡得正香呢。”弗朗西斯说。事已办成，他们的胆气和信心竟出奇地足了。“保险得很——哎呀，那是什么！哎呀！”

一只手从树篱漆黑的暗影里猛地探出，一把攥住了他的胳膊。

===Original===
“What is it, France? What is it?” said Mavis, who could not see what was happening.
“What is it﻿—now what is it?” asked the Mermaid more crossly than she had yet spoken.
“*Who* is it? Oh, who is it?” gasped Francis, writhing in the grip of his invisible assailant. And from the dark shadow of the hedge came the simple and terrible reply:
“The police!”

===Chinese===
“怎么了，弗兰？怎么了？”梅维斯说，她看不见出了什么事。

“怎么了——这回又是怎么了？”美人鱼问，语气比先前哪一回都冲。

“*谁*？哦，是谁呀？”弗朗西斯喘着气问，在那看不见的袭击者的钳制中挣扎。从树篱的黑影里，传来了那句简单而可怕的回答：

“警察！”

## IV. Gratitude / 四、报恩

===Original===
It is hardly possible to imagine a situation less attractive than that of Mavis and Francis﻿—even the position of the Mermaid curled up in a dry barrow and far from her native element was not exactly luxurious. Still, she was no worse off than she had been when the lariat first curled itself about her fishy extremity. But the children! They had braved the terrors of night in an adventure of singular courage and daring, they had carried out their desperate enterprise, the Mermaid was rescued, and success seemed near﻿—no further off than the sea indeed, and that, in point of fact, was about a quarter of a mile away. To be within a quarter of a mile of achievement, and then to have the cup of victory dashed from your lips, the crown of victory torn from your brow by﻿—the police!

===Chinese===
很难想象还有什么处境，比梅维斯和弗朗西斯眼下的处境更不美气的了——就连美人鱼蜷在一辆干巴巴的独轮车里、远离她安身立命的水乡，也说不上多么奢华。不过，比起套索头一回缠上她那条鱼尾巴尖那会儿，她眼下的光景倒也不算更糟。可孩子们呢！他们以非凡的胆量与豪勇，顶着黑夜的重重恐怖，把那桩孤注一掷的营生干成了，美人鱼获救了，成功似乎近在眼前——说真的，远不过就是到海边，而海边嘛，实实在在只有四分之一英里。眼看离功成只差四分之一英里，胜利之杯却让人从你唇边打翻，胜利之冠却让人从你额上扯去——干这事的，竟是——警察！

===Original===
It was indeed hard. And what was more, it was dangerous.

===Chinese===
这实在是够惨的了。而且不仅如此，还危险着呢。

===Original===
“We shall pass the night in the cells,” thought Mavis, in agony; “and whatever will Mother do when she finds we’re gone?” In her mind “the cells” were underground dungeons, dark and damp and vaulted, where toads and lizards crawled, and no daylight ever penetrated. That is how dungeons are described in books about the Inquisition.

===Chinese===
"咱们要在牢房里过夜了，"梅维斯痛苦地想，"妈妈发现咱们不见了，可怎么办呀？"在她心目中，"牢房"是地底下的地牢，又黑又潮，顶着穹顶，癞蛤蟆和蜥蜴爬来爬去，一线天光也透不进去。讲宗教裁判所的书里，地牢就是这么写的。

===Original===
When the voice from the bush had said “The police,” a stricken silence followed. The mouth of Francis felt dry inside, just as if he had been eating cracknels, he explained afterward, and he had to swallow nothing before he could say:

===Chinese===
灌木丛里那个声音说出"警察"两个字以后，跟着来的是一片中了雷击似的沉默。弗朗西斯只觉得嘴里发干——照他事后的说法，就像刚嚼过一把硬脆饼——他得先咽下一口什么也没有，才说得出话来：

===Original===
“What for?”

===Chinese===
"干什么？"

===Original===
“Let go his arm,” said Mavis to the hidden foe. “We won’t run away. Really we won’t.”

===Chinese===
"放开他的胳膊，"梅维斯对那个藏在暗处的敌人说，"我们不会跑的。真的不跑。"

===Original===
“You can’t,” said the Mermaid. “You can’t leave me.”

===Chinese===
"你们跑不了，"美人鱼说，"你们不能丢下我。"

===Original===
“Leave go,” said Francis, wriggling. And then suddenly Mavis made a dart at the clutching hand and caught it by the wrist and whispered savagely:

===Chinese===
"松手。"弗朗西斯一边挣脱一边说。接着，梅维斯突然朝那只死抓住不放的手猛冲过去，一把攥住手腕，恶狠狠地低声道：

===Original===
“It’s not a policeman at all. Come out of that bush﻿—come out,” and dragged. And something did come out of the bush. Something that certainly was not a policeman. It was small and thin, whereas policemen are almost always tall and stout. It did not wear the blue coats our Roberts wear, but velveteen knickerbockers and a tweed jacket. It was, in fact, a very small boy.

===Chinese===
"根本就不是警察。从灌木丛里出来——出来，"她一边说一边往外拽。灌木丛里也真出来了样东西。一样肯定不是警察的东西。它又小又瘦，而警察几乎个个长得高高壮壮。它身上穿的，也不是咱们那些"罗伯特"警察穿的那种蓝制服，而是平绒灯笼裤配一件粗花呢外套。说到底，那是个很小很小的男孩。

===Original===
Francis broke into a cackle of relief.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯如释重负，咯咯地笑出了声。

===Original===
“You little﻿—animal,” he said. “What a fright you gave me.”

===Chinese===
"你这个小——畜生，"他说，"你可把人吓坏了。"

===Original===
“Animal yourself, if you come to that, let alone her and her tail,” the boy answered; and Mavis thought his voice didn’t sound unfriendly. “My! But I did take a rise out of you that time, eh? Ain’t she bit you yet, nor yet strook you with that there mackerel-end of hers?”

===Chinese===
"要说畜生，你自己才是呢——更别提她和她那条尾巴了。"男孩答道；梅维斯倒觉得，他的嗓音听不出什么恶意。"嚯！这一回我可把你们捉弄得够呛，嗯？她还没咬过你们？也没拿她那条鲭鱼尾巴梢抽过你们？"

===Original===
And then they recognized him. It was the little Spangled Boy. Only now, of course, being off duty he was no more spangled than you and I are.

===Chinese===
这时他们认出他来了。原来是小亮片小子（the Spangled Boy）。只不过眼下他不当班，身上自然一粒亮片也没有，跟你我一样。

===Original===
“Whatever did you do it for?” Mavis asked crossly. “It was horrid of you.”

===Chinese===
"你到底为着什么要这么干？"梅维斯没好气地质问，"你也太可恶了。"

===Original===
“It wasn’t only just a lark,” said the boy. “I cut around and listened this afternoon when you was jawing, and I thought why not be in it? Only I do sleep that heavy, what with the riding and the tumbling and all. So I didn’t wake till you’d got her out and then I cut up along ahind the hedge to be beforehand with you. An’ I was. It was a fair cop, matey, eh?”

===Chinese===
"可不单单是闹着玩，"男孩说，"今儿下午你们在那儿争争吵吵的时候，我绕过去听了一耳朵，心里就想，干吗不算我一股？只是我睡得实在死——又是骑马又是翻跟头，那些活计累人。等你们把她弄出来我才醒，我就抄到树篱后头，抢在你们前头。还真叫我抢着了。这回可算逮个正着，伙计，嗯？"

===Original===
“What are you going to do about it?” Francis asked flatly; “tell your father?” But Mavis reflected that he didn’t seem to have told his father yet, and perhaps wouldn’t.

===Chinese===
"这事你打算怎么着？"弗朗西斯干巴巴地问，"告诉你父亲去？"不过梅维斯心里想，他看样子还没告诉过他父亲，说不定压根儿也不会去说。

===Original===
“Ain’t got no father,” said the Spangled Boy, “nor yet mother.”

===Chinese===
"没父亲，"亮片小子说，"也没母亲。"

===Original===
“If you are rested enough you’d better go on,” said the Mermaid. “I’m getting dry through.”

===Chinese===
"你们要是歇够了，最好赶路，"美人鱼说，"我都快干透了。"

===Original===
And Mavis understood that to her that was as bad as getting wet through would be to us.

===Chinese===
梅维斯心里明白，对她来说，"干透"就跟咱们"湿透"一样难熬。

===Original===
“I’m so sorry,” she said gently, “but﻿—”

===Chinese===
"真对不起，"她轻声说，"可是——"

===Original===
“I must say I think it’s very inconsiderate of you to keep me all this time in the dry,” the Mermaid went on. “I really should have thought that even *you*﻿—”

===Chinese===
"我不得不说，你们让我在干地方待这么半天，实在太不体谅人了，"美人鱼接着说，"我真该想到，就连*你们*也——"

===Original===
But Francis interrupted her.

===Chinese===
可弗朗西斯打断了她。

===Original===
“What are you going to *do*?” he asked the Spangled Boy. And that surprising child answered, spitting on his hands and rubbing them:

===Chinese===
"你到底打算*怎么*办？"他问亮片小子。这个出人意表的孩子往手心啐了口唾沫，搓了搓手，答道：

===Original===
“Do? Why, give a ’and with the barrer.”

===Chinese===
"怎么办？嗨，搭把手，帮着推车呗。"

===Original===
The Mermaid put out a white arm and touched him.

===Chinese===
美人鱼伸出一条雪白的胳膊，碰了碰他。

===Original===
“You are a hero,” she said. “I can recognize true nobility even under a once-spangled exterior. You may kiss my hand.”

===Chinese===
"你是一位英雄，"她说，"哪怕外头裹的是一层缀过亮片的皮，我也认得出真正的高贵。你可以吻我的手。"

===Original===
“Well, of all the.﻿ ﻿…” said Francis.

===Chinese===
"嗬，这可真是……天底下少有……"弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
“Shall I?” the boy asked, more of himself than of the others.

===Chinese===
"我吻吗？"男孩问，与其说是问旁人，不如说是问他自己。

===Original===
“Do,” Mavis whispered. “Anything to keep her in a good temper.”

===Chinese===
"吻吧，"梅维斯悄声说，"只要她脾气好，怎么都行。"

===Original===
So the Spangled Boy kissed the still dampish hand of the Lady from the Sea, took the handles of the barrow and off they all went.

===Chinese===
于是亮片小子吻了吻海上来的夫人那只还微微发潮的手，抓起独轮车的车把，一行人便上了路。

===Original===
Mavis and Francis were too thankful for this unexpected help to ask any questions, though they could not help wondering exactly what it felt like to be a boy who did not mind stealing his own father’s Mermaid. It was the boy himself who offered, at the next rest-halt, an explanation.

===Chinese===
这份从天而降的援手，让梅维斯和弗朗西斯感激都来不及，哪里还想多问什么；只是他们免不了暗自琢磨：一个男孩，偷起自己父亲的美人鱼来毫不在乎，那心里到底是什么滋味。到了下一个歇脚处，倒是男孩自己，主动做了一番解释。

===Original===
“You see,” he said, “it’s like this here. This party in the barrow﻿—”

===Chinese===
"你瞧，"他说，"事情是这么档子事。车上这位'人儿'——"

===Original===
“I know you don’t mean it disrespectfully,” said the Mermaid, sweetly; “but *not* party﻿—and *not* a barrow.”

===Chinese===
"我知道你们没有不敬的意思，"美人鱼甜甜地说，"可是*不能*是'人儿'——也*不能*是'独轮车'。"

===Original===
“Lady,” suggested Mavis.

===Chinese===
"太太。"梅维斯提议道。

===Original===
“This lydy in the chariot, she’d been kidnapped﻿—that’s how I look at it. Same as what I was.”

===Chinese===
"这位坐御辇的太太呀，她是让人拐了来的——照我看就是这么回事。跟我当年一个样。"

===Original===
This was romance indeed; and Mavis recognized it and said:

===Chinese===
这可真是名副其实的罗曼史；梅维斯也听出了这一层，便道：

===Original===
“You, kidnapped? I say!”

===Chinese===
"你？被拐过？哎呀！"

===Original===
“Yus,” said Spangles, “when I was a baby kid. Old Mother Romaine told me, just afore she was took all down one side and never spoke no more.”

===Chinese===
"可不，"亮片儿说，"我还是个小奶娃的时候。老罗梅因大妈（Old Mother Romaine）亲口告诉我的，就在她瘫了半边身子、再没能开口说话之前不久。"

===Original===
“But why?” Mavis asked. “I never could understand in the books why gypsies kidnapped babies. They always seem to have so many of their own﻿—far, far more than anyone could possibly want.”

===Chinese===
"可是为什么呀？"梅维斯问，"书里写吉普赛人拐婴儿，我从来就没弄明白过。他们自己的娃娃好像总多得很——多得远远超过任何人用得着的数儿。"

===Original===
“Yes, indeed,” said the Mermaid, “they prodded at me with sticks﻿—a multitude of them.”

===Chinese===
"可不是嘛，"美人鱼说，"他们就拿棍子捅过我——还是一大群人呢。"

===Original===
“It wasn’t kids as was wanted,” said the boy, “it was revenge. That’s what Mother Romaine said﻿—my father he was a sort of Beak, so he give George Lee eighteen months for poaching. An’ the day they took him the church bells was ringing like mad, and George, as he was being took, he said: ‘What’s all that row? It ain’t Sunday.’ And then they tells him as how the bells was ringing ’cause him that was the Beak﻿—my father, you know﻿—he’d got a son and hare. And that was me. You wouldn’t think it to look at me,” he added, spitting pensively and taking up the barrow handles, “but I’m a son and hare.”

===Chinese===
"人家要的本来就不是娃娃，"男孩说，"是报仇。罗梅因大妈是这么说的——我爸爸他算是个治安法官（Beak）那一号人物，因为偷猎，判了乔治·李（George Lee）十八个月。他们把乔治带走那天，教堂的钟正敲得跟发了疯似的，乔治一边让人押着走，一边说：'吵吵嚷嚷这是干什么？今儿又不是礼拜天。'他们就告诉他，说钟敲得那么欢，是因为那位当法官的——就是我爸爸，你知道——他得了个'儿子和野兔'。那说的就是我。你光瞧我这样子准想不到，"他补了一句，若有所思地啐了口唾沫，抓起车把，"可我就是个'儿子和野兔'。"

===Original===
“And then what happened?” Mavis asked as they trudged on.

===Chinese===
"后来怎么样了呢？"他们吭哧吭哧往前赶路，梅维斯问。

===Original===
“Oh, George﻿—he done his time, and I was a kiddy then, year-and-a-half old, all lace and ribbons and blue shoes made of glove-stuff, and George pinched me, and it makes me breff short, wheeling and talking.”

===Chinese===
"哦，乔治——他把牢坐满了，那会儿我是个小不点儿，一岁半，浑身的花边呀、缎带呀，脚上一双小山羊皮手套料做的蓝鞋子，是乔治把我偷走的。又是推车又是说话的，我气都喘不上来了。"

===Original===
“Pause and rest, my spangled friend,” said the Mermaid in a voice of honey, “and continue your thrilling narrative.”

===Chinese===
"停下来歇歇吧，我缀亮片的朋友，"美人鱼用蜜糖似的嗓音说，"然后再继续讲你那惊心动魄的故事。"

===Original===
“There ain’t no more to it,” said the boy, “except that I got one of the shoes. Old Mother Romaine ’ad kep’ it, and a little shirt like a lady’s handkercher, with R. V. on it in needlework. She didn’t ever tell me what part of the country my dad was Beak in. Said she’d tell me next day. An’ then there wasn’t no next day for her﻿—not fer telling things in, there wasn’t.”

===Chinese===
"再没有别的了，"男孩说，"只除了那双鞋里我留下一只。老罗梅因大妈把它收着，还有一件小衬衫，小得像位太太的手帕，上头用刺绣绣着 R. V. 两个字母。她始终没告诉过我，我爸爸当年是在哪一乡当的法官。说是第二天告诉我。可对她来说，就再没有第二天了——再没有一个能拿来告诉我事儿的第二天了。"

===Original===
He rubbed his sleeve across his eyes.

===Chinese===
他用袖子在眼睛上抹了一把。

===Original===
“She wasn’t half a bad sort,” he explained.

===Chinese===
"她这人不赖。"他解释道。

===Original===
“Don’t cry,” said Mavis unwisely.

===Chinese===
"别哭。"梅维斯不知轻重地说。

===Original===
“Cry? Me?” he answered scornfully. “I’ve got a cold in me ’ead. You oughter know the difference between a cold in the head and sniveling. You been to school, I lay?﻿—they might have taught you that.”

===Chinese===
"哭？我吗？"他轻蔑地答道，"我这是脑袋瓜着了凉。伤风鼻涕跟哭鼻子有什么分别，你总该分得清吧。我敢说你上过学？——这个他们总该教过你。"

===Original===
“I wonder the gypsies didn’t take the shoe and the shirt away from you?”

===Chinese===
"我倒奇怪，吉普赛人怎么没把那只鞋和那件衬衫从你手里拿走？"

===Original===
“Nobody know’d I’d got ’em; I always kep’ ’em inside my shirt, wrapt up in a bit of paper, and when I put on me tights I used to hide ’em. I’m a-going to take the road one of these days, and find out who it was lost a kid with blue shoes and shirt nine years come April.”

===Chinese===
"没人知道我藏着它们；我一直把它们贴身揣在衬衫里头，拿一小块纸包着，每回穿演出紧身衣，我都先把它们藏好。过些日子我就要上路去，寻出那户人家——他们丢了个穿蓝鞋、带绣花小衬衫的娃娃，到四月，这事就满九年了。"

===Original===
“Then you’re ten and a half,” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
"那你现在十岁半。"梅维斯说。

===Original===
And the boy answered admiringly:

===Chinese===
男孩满脸佩服地答道：

===Original===
“How do you do it in your head so quick, miss? Yes, that’s what I am.”

===Chinese===
"小姐，你怎么脑子里一算就这么快？对啦，我正是这个岁数。"

===Original===
Here the wheelbarrow resumed its rather bumpety progress, and nothing more could be said till the next stoppage, which was at that spot where the seafront road swings around and down, and glides into the beach so gently that you can hardly tell where one begins and the other ends. It was much lighter there than up on the waste space. The moon was just breaking through a fluffy white cloud and cast a trembling sort of reflection on the sea. As they came down the slope all hands were needed to steady the barrow, because as soon as she saw the sea the Mermaid began to jump up and down like a small child at a Christmas tree.

===Chinese===
到这儿，独轮车又颠儿颠儿地往前走了，在下回停车之前，谁也没法再说话。那一站停的地方，正是滨海大道拐弯下坡、缓缓化进沙滩的所在——路和滩衔接得那么熨帖，你简直说不上哪里是路的头、哪里是滩的尾。那儿比上面的荒地亮堂得多。月亮正从一朵蓬松的白云里探出来，在海面上投下一道颤颤悠悠的倒影。他们下坡那阵子，得大伙儿一齐上手才稳得住独轮车，因为美人鱼一望见大海，就开始蹦上蹦下，活像圣诞树跟前的小娃娃。

===Original===
“Oh, look!” she cried, “isn’t it beautiful? Isn’t it the only home in the world?”

===Chinese===
"哦，瞧呀！"她喊道，"它多美呀！它难道不是世上独一无二的家吗？"

===Original===
“Not quite,” said the boy.

===Chinese===
"不见得。"男孩说。

===Original===
“Ah!” said the lady in the barrow, “Of course you’re heir to one of the﻿—what is it﻿ ﻿… ?”

===Chinese===
"啊！"独轮车里的这位太太说，"你当然是那一座座……那什么来着……的继承人喽？"

===Original===
“ ‘Stately homes of England﻿—how beautiful they stand,’ ” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
"'英格兰气派的庄园——它们矗立得多美，'"梅维斯念道。

===Original===
“Yes,” said the lady. “I knew by instinct that he was of noble birth.”

===Chinese===
"正是，"这位太太说，"我凭本能就知道，他出身于高贵的门第。"

===Original===
> “ ‘I bid ye take care of the brat,’ said he,
 

 ‘For he comes of a noble race,’ ”

===Chinese===
> "'替我把这小崽子照看好，'他说，
 

 '因为他出身于高贵的种族。'"

===Original===
Francis hummed. He was feeling a little cross and sore. He and Mavis had had all the anxious trouble of the adventure, and now the Spangled Boy was the only one the Mermaid was nice to. It was certainly hard.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯哼的正是这几句。他正觉得有点儿气恼委屈。这场冒险里操心受累的都是他和梅维斯，如今美人鱼偏偏单单对亮片小子一个人好。这实在叫人心里不平。

===Original===
“But your stately home would not do for me at all,” she went on. “My idea of home is all seaweed of coral and pearl﻿—so cosy and delightful and wet. Now﻿—can you push the chariot to the water’s edge, or will you carry me?”

===Chinese===
"不过，你那气派的庄园压根儿不适合我，"她接着说，"我理想中的家呀，满眼是珊瑚和珍珠的海草——那么舒适，那么可心，还湿漉漉的。好了——你们能把这辆御辇推到水边去吗？还是你们打算抱着我走？"

===Original===
“Not much we won’t,” the Spangled Boy answered firmly. “We’ll push you as far as we can, and then you’ll have to wriggle.”

===Chinese===
"怎么不能推。"亮片小子斩钉截铁地回答，"能推多远，我们推多远；再往后，你就得自己扭了。"

===Original===
“I will do whatever you suggest,” she said amiably; “but what is this wriggle of which you speak?”

===Chinese===
"你们怎么建议，我就怎么做，"她和和气气地说，"不过，你说的这个'扭'，究竟是怎么个扭法？"

===Original===
“Like a worm,” said Francis.

===Chinese===
"像蚯蚓那样扭。"弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
“Or an eel,” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
"或者像鳗鱼。"梅维斯说。

===Original===
“Nasty low things,” said the Mermaid; and the children never knew whether she meant the worm and the eel, or the girl and the boy.

===Chinese===
"下贱低微的东西。"美人鱼说；而这两个孩子始终没能弄明白，她指的是蚯蚓和鳗鱼呢，还是姑娘和小子。

===Original===
“Now then. All together,” said the Spangled Child. And the barrow bumped down to the very edge of the rocks. And at the very edge its wheel caught in a chink and the barrow went sideways. Nobody could help it, but the Mermaid was tumbled out of her chariot on to the seaweed.

===Chinese===
"来喽。一齐使劲儿。"亮片孩子说。独轮车一路颠到了礁石的边边上。不偏不倚，就在最边上，车轮卡进一道石缝，车身横了过去。这谁也没法子，美人鱼就从她的御辇里翻了出来，摔在了海草上。

===Original===
The seaweed was full and cushiony and soft, and she was not hurt at all﻿—but she was very angry.

===Chinese===
海草厚墩墩、软绵绵，她一点儿也没摔着——可她气坏了。

===Original===
“You have been to school,” she said, “as my noble preserver reminds you. You might have learned how not to upset chariots.”

===Chinese===
"你们上过学，"她说，"我那位高贵的救命恩人不是提醒过了嘛。你们本该学会怎样不把御辇掀翻。"

===Original===
“It’s we who are your preservers,” Francis couldn’t help saying.

===Chinese===
"救你的恩人可是我们。"弗朗西斯忍不住说。

===Original===
“Of course you are,” she said coolly, “plain preservers. Not noble ones. But I forgive you. You can’t help being common and clumsy. I suppose it’s your nature﻿—just as it’s his to be.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
"当然是你们，"她冷冷地说，"普普通通的救命恩人，不是高贵的恩人。不过我原谅你们。你们天生平庸、笨手笨脚，这怪不得你们。我想这就是你们的天性——就像他的天性，天生就是……"

===Original===
“Goodbye,” said Francis, firmly.

===Chinese===
"再见。"弗朗西斯斩钉截铁地说。

===Original===
“Not at all,” said the lady. “You must come with me in case there are any places where I can’t exercise the elegant and vermiform accomplishment you spoke about. Now, one on each side, and one behind, and don’t walk on my tail. You can’t think how annoying it is to have your tail walked on.”

===Chinese===
"那可不行。"这位太太说，"你们必须陪我一道走，以防遇上什么地段，我用不出你们刚才说的那种优雅而蠕行的高强本领。好了，一边一个，一个在后头，别踩着我的尾巴。尾巴让人踩着有多恼人，你们想象不出。"

===Original===
“Oh, can’t I,” said Mavis. “I’ll tell you something. My mother has a tail too.”

===Chinese===
"我怎么想象不出，"梅维斯说，"告诉你件事吧。我妈妈也有一条尾巴。"

===Original===
“I *say*!” said Francis.

===Chinese===
"*我的天*！"弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
But the Spangled Child understood.

===Chinese===
可亮片孩子听懂了。

===Original===
“She don’t wear it every day, though,” he said; and Mavis is almost sure that he winked. Only it is so difficult to be sure about winks in the starlight.

===Chinese===
"不过她不是天天都穿在身上罢了。"他说。梅维斯几乎可以肯定，他还挤了挤眼。只是星光底下的挤眼，实在难说得很。

===Original===
“Your mother must be better born than I supposed,” said the Mermaid. “Are you *quite* sure about the tail?”

===Chinese===
"你母亲的家世，想必比我原先料想的更为高贵。"美人鱼说，"关于那条尾巴，你*当真*拿得准？"

===Original===
“I’ve trodden on it often,” said Mavis﻿—and then Francis saw.

===Chinese===
"我常踩到它。"梅维斯说——到这时，弗朗西斯才恍然大悟。

===Original===
Wriggling and sliding and pushing herself along by her hands, and helped now and then by the hands of the others, the Mermaid was at last got to the edge of the water.

===Chinese===
美人鱼又扭又滑，用双手撑着地一寸一寸往前挪，还不时借旁人搭把手，总算给弄到了水边上。

===Original===
“How glorious! In a moment I shall be quite wet,” she cried.

===Chinese===
"多痛快呀！转眼我就要浑身湿透啦。"她喊道。

===Original===
In a moment everyone else was quite wet also﻿—for with a movement that was something between a squirm and a jump, she dropped from the edge with a splashing flop.

===Chinese===
转眼间，别人也个个湿透了——只见她身子一扭一纵，介于蠕动和跳跃之间，从边上翻落下去，扑通一声溅起老大的水花。

===Original===
And disappeared entirely.

===Chinese===
然后便消失得无影无踪。

## V Consequences / 五、后果

===Original===
The three children looked at each other.
“Well!” said Mavis.
“I do think she’s ungrateful,” said Francis.
“What did you expect?” asked the Spangled Child.
They were all wet through. It was very late﻿—they were very tired, and the clouds were putting the moon to bed in a very great hurry. The Mermaid was gone; the whole adventure was ended.
There was nothing to do but to go home, and go to sleep, knowing that when they woke the next morning it would be to a day in the course of which they would have to explain their wet clothes to their parents.

===Chinese===
三个孩子面面相觑。

“唉！”梅维斯（Mavis）说。

“我倒真觉得她忘恩负义。”弗朗西斯（Francis）说。

“你们原本还指望什么呢？”亮片孩子（Spangled Child）问。

他们全都湿了个透。天很晚了——他们累极了，云彩正十万火急地送月亮上床去。美人鱼走了；整场冒险就此收了场。

除了回家睡觉，再没有别的可做了；而且他们心里明白，第二天一早醒来，等着他们的将是这样一天：在这一天里，他们得向爸爸妈妈解释这些湿衣裳是怎么一回事。

===Original===
“Even *you*’ll have to do that,” Mavis reminded the Spangled Boy.
He received her remark in what they afterward remembered to have been a curiously deep silence.
“I don’t know how on earth we *are* to explain,” said Francis. “I really don’t. Come on﻿—let’s get home. No more adventures for me, thank you. Bernard knew what he was talking about.”
Mavis, very tired indeed, agreed.
They had got over the beach by this time, recovered the wheelbarrow, and trundled it up and along the road. At the corner the Spangled Boy suddenly said:
“Well then, so long, old sports,” and vanished down a side lane.

===Chinese===
“连*你*也逃不过这一关。”梅维斯提醒亮片小子。

他听了这话没有作声，那份沉默深得出奇，他们后来还一直记得。

“我实在不知道这事儿我们*究竟*要怎么解释，”弗朗西斯说，“真的不知道。走吧——回家去。冒险这玩意儿我可领教够了，多谢。伯纳德（Bernard）当初说得没错。”

梅维斯实在累得很，表示同意。

这时他们已经走过了海滩，找回独轮手推车，推着它上了大路，一路往前。到了拐角上，亮片小子忽然说：

“那么，回见啦，老伙计们。”说完，他一头钻进旁边的小巷，没影儿了。

===Original===
The other two went on together﻿—with the wheelbarrow, which, I may remind you, was as wet as any of them.
They went along by the hedge and the mill and up to the house.
Suddenly Mavis clutched at her brother’s arm.
“There’s a light,” she said, “in the house.”
There certainly was, and the children experienced that terrible empty sensation only too well known to all of us﻿—the feeling of the utterly-found-out.

===Chinese===
另外两个继续往前走——还推着那辆独轮手推车；不妨提醒您一句，这辆车湿得和他们俩谁也不相上下。

他们沿着树篱走，经过磨坊，一路走到屋子跟前。

梅维斯忽然一把抓住哥哥的胳膊。

“屋里，”她说，“有灯光。”

确实有灯光。于是两个孩子体验到了那种我们人人都再熟悉不过的、可怕的空落落的感觉——事情彻底败露了的感觉。

===Original===
They could not be sure which window it was, but it was a downstairs window, partly screened by ivy. A faint hope still buoyed up Francis of getting up to bed unnoticed by whoever it was that had the light; and he and his sister crept around to the window out of which they had crept; but such a very long time ago it seemed. The window was shut.
Francis suggested hiding in the mill and trying to creep in unobserved later on, but Mavis said:
“No. I’m too tired for anything. I’m too tired to *live*, I think. Let’s go and get it over, and then we can go to bed and sleep, and sleep, and sleep.”
So they went and peeped in at the kitchen window, and there was no one but Mrs. Pearce, and she had a fire lighted and was putting a big pot on it.
The children went to the back door and opened it.

===Chinese===
他们说不准是哪扇窗，只知道是楼下一扇，半掩在常春藤后头。弗朗西斯心里还托着一丝微弱的希望，指望能瞒过点灯的那个人，悄悄溜上床去；他和妹妹蹑手蹑脚地绕到他们当初溜出去的那扇窗前——那仿佛已经是很久很久以前的事了。窗户关着。

弗朗西斯提议先躲进磨坊，回头再想法子悄悄溜进去，可梅维斯说：

“不。我累得什么都干不动了。我看我连*活*着都嫌累。咱们去把这一关熬过去，然后就可以上床，睡呀，睡呀，睡个够。”

于是他们走过去，从厨房窗户往里张望：屋里只有皮尔斯太太（Mrs. Pearce）一个人，她已生起了火，正往上头坐一只大锅。

两个孩子走到后门口，推开了门。

===Original===
“You’re early, for sure,” said Mrs. Pearce, not turning.
This seemed a bitter sarcasm. It was too much. Mavis answered it with a sob. And at that Mrs. Pearce turned very quickly.
“What to gracious!” she said﻿—“whatever to gracious is the matter? Where’ve you been?” She took Mavis by the shoulder. “Why, you’re all sopping wet. You naughty, naughty little gell, you. Wait till I tell your Ma﻿—been shrimping I lay﻿—or trying to﻿—never asking when the tide was right. And not a shrimp to show for it, I know, with the tide where it is. You wait till we hear what your Ma’s got to say about it. And look at my clean flags and you dripping all over ’em like a fortnight’s wash in wet weather.”
Mavis twisted a little in Mrs. Pearce’s grasp. “Oh, don’t scold us, dear Mrs. Pearce,” she said, putting a wet arm up toward Mrs. Pearce’s neck. “We *are* so miserable.”
“And so you deserve to be,” said Mrs. Pearce, smartly. “Here, young chap, you go into the washhouse and get them things off, and drop them outside the door, and have a good rub with the jack-towel; and little miss can undress by the fire and put hern in this clean pail﻿—and I’ll pop up softlike and so as your Ma don’t hear, and bring you down something dry.”
A gleam of hope fell across the children’s hearts﻿—a gleam wild and watery as that which the moonlight had cast across the sea, into which the Mermaid had disappeared. Perhaps after all Mrs. Pearce wasn’t going to tell Mother. If she was, why should she pop up softlike? Perhaps she would keep their secret. Perhaps she would dry their clothes. Perhaps, after all, that impossible explanation would never have to be given.

===Chinese===
“回来得倒早，真是的。”皮尔斯太太说，头也没回。

这话听上去像一句刻毒的挖苦。实在受不了了。梅维斯用一声抽泣作了回答。皮尔斯太太一听，霍地转过身来。

“哎呀老天！”她说——“到底是出什么事啦？你们上哪儿去了？”她一把抓住梅维斯的肩膀。“天哪，你们浑身湿得透透的。你这淘气、淘气的小丫头片子，你呀。等我告诉你妈去——我敢保你们是捉小虾去了——要不就是想去捉——也不先问问潮水对不对时候。潮水是眼下这个光景，我知道，你们准连一只虾也拿不出来。等着瞧吧，听听你妈对这事怎么说。再瞧瞧我这块干干净净的石板地，叫你们滴得满地都是，活像阴雨天里晾了两个礼拜的衣裳。”

梅维斯在皮尔斯太太手里轻轻扭了扭。“哦，别骂我们了，亲爱的皮尔斯太太，”她说着，把一条湿漉漉的胳膊朝皮尔斯太太的脖子上伸过去，“我们*真是*惨透啦。”

“你们也活该落得这样。”皮尔斯太太回答得干脆利落。“喂，小伙子，你上洗衣房去，把身上那些脱下来搁在门外头，拿长毛巾好好擦一通；小姐儿就在火边上脱，把她的湿衣裳搁进这只干净桶里——我呢，蹑手蹑脚上楼去，不叫你们妈听见，给你们拿身干的下来。”

一线希望落进两个孩子心里——那一线希望空茫而水淋淋的，正像月光投在海上的一道微光，美人鱼就是消失在那片海里的。也许皮尔斯太太终究不会去告诉妈妈。她要是打算告诉，又何必蹑手蹑脚地怕人听见？也许她会替他们保守秘密。也许她会把他们的衣裳烘干。也许，那桩没法解释的事，到头来根本就用不着解释了。

===Original===
The kitchen was a pleasant place, with bright brasses and shining crockery, and a round three-legged table with a clean cloth and blue-and-white teacups on it.
Mrs. Pearce came down with their nightgowns and the warm dressing gowns that Aunt Enid had put in in spite of their expressed wishes. How glad they were of them now!
“There, that’s a bit more like,” said Mrs. Pearce; “here, don’t look as if I was going to eat you, you little Peter Grievouses. I’ll hot up some milk and here’s a morsel of bread and dripping to keep the cold out. Lucky for you I was up﻿—getting the boys’ breakfast ready. The boats’ll be in directly. The boys will laugh when I tell them﻿—laugh fit to bust their selves they will.”
“Oh, don’t tell,” said Mavis, “don’t, please don’t. Please, please don’t.”
“Well, I like that,” said Mrs. Pearce, pouring herself some tea from a pot which, the children learned later, stood on the hob all day and most of the night; “it’s the funniest piece I’ve heard this many a day. Shrimping at high tide!”

===Chinese===
厨房是个叫人舒坦的地方：铜器锃亮，陶器闪光，还有一张三条腿的圆桌，铺着干净的桌布，摆着蓝白花的茶杯。

皮尔斯太太抱着他们的睡衣下楼来，还有伊妮德姨妈（Aunt Enid）不顾他们明说过不要、硬给塞进箱子里的暖和晨衣。这会儿他们多庆幸有这些呀！

“喏，这样才像点儿样。”皮尔斯太太说，“行了，别一副我要把你们吃掉的样子，你们这帮小怨气包。我去热点儿牛奶，这儿还有一小块抹肉油的面包，给你们挡挡寒气。算你们运气好，我正起着呢——在给小伙子们备早饭。渔船眼瞅着就要进港了。等我把这事儿讲给他们听，他们准得笑——准得笑破肚皮。”

“哦，别说出去，”梅维斯说，“别说，千万别。求您了，千万别。”

“嘿，瞧这话说的。”皮尔斯太太说着，给自己倒了点茶——那壶茶，孩子们后来才知道，成天和大半夜都坐在炉架上——“这是多少日子以来我听到的最好笑的一桩事。满潮的时候去捉虾！”

===Original===
“I thought,” said Mavis, “perhaps you’d forgive us, and dry our clothes, and not tell anybody.”
“Oh, you did, did you?” said Mrs. Pearce. “Anything else﻿—?”
“No, nothing else, thank you,” said Mavis, “only I want to say thank you for being so kind, and it isn’t high tide yet, and please we haven’t done any harm to the barrow﻿—but I’m afraid it’s rather wet, and we oughtn’t to have taken it without asking, I know, but you were in bed and﻿—”
“The barrow?” Mrs. Pearce repeated. “That great hulking barrow﻿—you took the barrow to bring the shrimps home in? No﻿—I can’t keep it to myself﻿—that really I can’t﻿—” she lay back in the armchair and shook with silent laughter.
The children looked at each other. It is not pleasant to be laughed at, especially for something you have never done﻿—but they both felt that Mrs. Pearce would have laughed quite as much, or even more, if they had told her what it really was they had wanted the barrow for.

===Chinese===
“我原本想着，”梅维斯说，“您也许会饶了我们这回，把我们的衣裳烘干，谁也不告诉。”

“哦，你倒是想着来着，嗯？”皮尔斯太太说。“还要点儿别的吗——？”

“没了，没别的了，谢谢您。”梅维斯说，“我只是想说，您对我们这么好，谢谢您。还有，现在还没到满潮呢。还有求您了，我们没有把独轮车弄坏——不过恐怕它湿得厉害。我知道，我们不该不打声招呼就把它推走，可是那会儿您已经睡下了，而且——”

“独轮车？”皮尔斯太太重复道。“那么大那么沉的一辆车——你们推独轮车去，是为了把虾运回家？不——这个我可憋不住——这个实在憋不住——”她仰靠进扶手椅里，抖着肩膀，无声地笑。

两个孩子对望了一眼。被人笑话可不是件愉快的事，尤其是为一件你压根儿没做过的事——不过他们俩都觉得，要是把推这辆车的真正缘由讲给皮尔斯太太听，她准会笑得一样厉害，甚至有过之而无不及。

===Original===
“Oh, don’t go on laughing,” said Mavis, creeping close to Mrs. Pearce, “though you are a ducky darling not to be cross any more. And you won’t tell, will you?”
“Ah, well﻿—I’ll let you off this time. But you’ll promise faithful never to do it again, now, won’t you?”
“We faithfully won’t ever,” said both children, earnestly.
“Then off you go to your beds, and I’ll dry the things when your Ma’s out. I’ll press ’em tomorrow morning while I’m waiting for the boys to come in.”
“You *are* an angel,” said Mavis, embracing her.
“More than you are then, you young limbs,” said Mrs. Pearce, returning the embrace. “Now off you go, and get what sleep you can.”

===Chinese===
“哦，别笑个没完啦。”梅维斯说着，凑到皮尔斯太太身边，“不过您真是个亲爱的宝贝儿，不再生我们的气了。您不会说出去的，对吧？”

“唉，好吧——这回就饶了你们。可你们得老老实实保证，往后再也不这么干了，成不成？”

“我们老老实实保证，再也不了。”两个孩子都一本正经地说。

“那就上床去吧。等你妈出了门，我就把这些衣裳烘干。明儿早上等小伙子们回来的工夫，再熨一熨。”

“您*真是个*天使。”梅维斯说着，搂住了她。

“那也比你们两个强，你们这帮小淘气鬼。”皮尔斯太太回搂了她一把，“去吧去吧，能睡多少是多少。”

===Original===
It was with a feeling that Fate had not, after all, been unduly harsh with them that Mavis and Francis came down to a very late breakfast.
“Your Ma and Pa’s gone off on their bikes,” said Mrs. Pearce, bringing in the eggs and bacon, “won’t be back till dinner. So I let you have your sleep out. The little ’uns had theirs three hours ago and out on the sands. I told them to let you sleep, though I know they wanted to hear how many shrimps you caught. I lay they expected a barrowful, same as what you did.”
“How did you know they knew we’d been out?” Francis asked.
“Oh, the way they was being secret in corners, and looking the old barrow all over was enough to make a cat laugh. Hurry up, now. I’ve got the washing-up to do﻿—and your things is well-nigh dry.”
“You *are* a darling,” said Mavis. “Suppose you’d been different, whatever would have become of us?”
“You’d a got your desserts﻿—bed and bread and water, instead of this nice egg and bacon and the sands to play on. So now you know,” said Mrs. Pearce.
---

===Chinese===
梅维斯和弗朗西斯下楼来吃那顿晚得很的早饭时，心里都带着一种感觉：命运待他们，终究还不算太苛刻。

“你们爸妈骑自行车出去了。”皮尔斯太太端着鸡蛋和咸肉进来，“要到晚饭才回来。所以我就让你们睡了个够。小家伙们三个钟头前就睡足了，已经出门到沙滩上去了。我叫他们别吵醒你们——不过我知道，他们可急着要听听你们捉了多少虾了。我敢保他们还指望有一整车虾呢，跟你们俩原先想的一样。”

“您怎么知道他们晓得我们出去过？”弗朗西斯问。

“嘿，他们躲在角落里嘀嘀咕咕，又把那辆旧独轮车翻来覆去瞧个不停，那副样子连猫见了都得笑。快吃吧。我还得洗碗呢——你们的衣裳也差不多干了。”

“您*真是个*大好人。”梅维斯说，“要是您换了个样儿，真不知我们会落个什么下场呢。”

“那你们就吃到应得的报应了——关在床上啃面包喝白水，哪还有这么好的鸡蛋咸肉吃，哪还有沙滩玩。这下你们明白了吧。”皮尔斯太太说。

---

===Original===
On the sands they found Kathleen and Bernard, and it really now, in the bright warm sunshine, seemed almost worthwhile to have gone through last night’s adventures, if only for the pleasure of telling the tale of them to the two who had been safe and warm and dry in bed all the time.
“Though really,” said Mavis, when the tale was told, “sitting here and seeing the tents and the children digging, and the ladies knitting, and the gentlemen smoking and throwing stones, it does hardly seem as though there *could* be any magic. And yet, you know, there was.”
“It’s like I told you about radium and things,” said Bernard. “Things aren’t magic because they haven’t been found out yet. There’s always been Mermaids, of course, only people didn’t know it.”
“But she talks,” said Francis.
“Why not?” said Bernard placidly. “Even parrots do that.”
“But she talks English,” Mavis urged.

===Chinese===
在沙滩上，他们找到了凯思琳（Kathleen）和伯纳德。此刻，在明亮温暖的阳光里，昨夜那场冒险几乎算是值得的了——哪怕单为着能把那番经历讲给那两个自始至终安安稳稳、暖暖和和、干干爽爽躺在被窝里的人听一听，也是值得的。

“不过说真的，”故事讲完之后，梅维斯说，“坐在这儿，望着这些帐篷，望着挖沙子的孩子、织毛线的太太、抽烟扔石子的先生，简直叫人觉得根本*不可能*有什么魔法。可是您知道的，偏偏真就有过。”

“这就像我跟你们讲过的镭那一类东西。”伯纳德说，“东西还没被琢磨明白的时候，人们就说它是魔法。美人鱼其实一直都有，这不用说，只是人们不知道罢了。”

“可是她会说话呀。”弗朗西斯说。

“为什么不会？”伯纳德心平气和地说，“连鹦鹉都会呢。”

“可她说的是英语呀。”梅维斯分辩道。

===Original===
“Well,” said Bernard, unmoved, “what would you have had her talk?”
And so, in pretty sunshine, between blue sky and good sands, the adventure of the Mermaid seemed to come to an end, to be now only as a tale that is told. And when the four went slowly home to dinner all were, I think, a little sad that this should be so.
“Let’s go around and have a look at the empty barrow,” Mavis said; “it’ll bring it all back to us, and remind us of what was in it, like ladies’ gloves and troubadours.”
The barrow was where they had left it, but it was not empty. A very dirty piece of folded paper lay in it, addressed in penciled and uncertain characters

> To France
> To Be Opened.

===Chinese===
“哦。”伯纳德不为所动，“那你要她说什么话呢？”

就这样，在明媚的阳光里，在蓝天与好沙滩之间，美人鱼的冒险似乎到了头，如今只剩下一个讲过了的故事。四个孩子慢慢走回家去吃晚饭的时候，我想，人人都有点儿为此难过。

“咱们绕过去看看那辆空独轮车吧。”梅维斯说，“它会叫这一切都回到我们眼前，让我们想起车里装过的东西——比如女式手套呀，游吟诗人呀。”

独轮车还停在他们撂下它的地方，可它并不空。车里躺着一张折起来的脏纸，上面用铅笔写着歪歪扭扭的字，写着收信人：

> 致弗兰
> 须亲启。

===Original===
Francis opened it and read aloud:

> “I went back and she came back and she wants you to come back at ded of nite.
> Rube.”

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯拆开来，大声念道：

> “我回去了，她也回来了，她要你们生更半夜再来一趟。
> 鲁本。”

===Original===
“Well, I shan’t go,” said Francis.
A voice from the bush by the gate made them all start.
“Don’t let on you see me,” said the Spangled Boy, putting his head out cautiously.
“You seem very fond of hiding in bushes,” said Francis.
“I am,” said the boy briefly. “Ain’t you going﻿—to see her again, I mean?”
“No,” said Francis, “I’ve had enough dead of night to last me a long time.”

===Chinese===
“哼，我是不去的。”弗朗西斯说。

大门旁的灌木丛里忽然传来一个声音，把他们全都吓了一跳。

“别让人看出你们瞧见我了。”亮片小子说着，小心翼翼地探出头来。

“你倒好像挺爱往灌木丛里躲。”弗朗西斯说。

“是爱。”男孩答得简洁，“你们不去吗——我是说，不再去见她了？”

“不去了。”弗朗西斯说，“这顿深更半夜，够我受用好一阵子了。”

===Original===
“You a-going, miss?” the boy asked. “No? You are a half-livered crew. It’ll be only me, I suppose.”
“You’re going, then?”
“Well,” said the boy, “what do you think?”
“I should go if I were you,” said Bernard impartially.
“No, you wouldn’t; not if you were me,” said Francis. “You don’t know how disagreeable she was. I’m fed up with her. And besides, we simply *can’t* get out at dead of night now. Mrs. Pearce’ll be on the lookout. No﻿—it’s no go.”
“But you *must* manage it somehow,” said Kathleen; “you can’t let it drop like this. I shan’t believe it was magic at all if you do.”

===Chinese===
“小姐，你去不去？”男孩问，“也不去？你们这帮胆小鬼。那就剩我一个了，我瞧着。”

“那你是要去的？”

“那还用说。”男孩说，“你自个儿琢磨吧。”

“我要是你，我就去。”伯纳德不偏不倚地说。

“不，你才不会呢；你要真换成了我，就不会去了。”弗朗西斯说，“你不知道她有多难伺候。我受够她了。再说，如今我们压根儿*没法*在深更半夜溜出去了。皮尔斯太太会留神的。不成——这事儿没门儿。”

“可你们*一定*得想个法子。”凯思琳说，“不能就这么撒手不管了。你们要是撒了手，我就干脆不信那算什么魔法了。”

===Original===
“If you were us, you’d have had enough of magic,” said Francis. “Why don’t you go yourselves﻿—you and Bernard.”
“I’ve a good mind to,” said Bernard unexpectedly. “Only not in the middle of the night, because of my being certain to drop my boots. Would you come, Cathay?”
“You know I wanted to before,” said Kathleen reproachfully.
“But how?” the others asked.
“Oh,” said Bernard, “we must think about that. I say, you chap, we must get to our dinner. Will you be here after?”
“Yes. I ain’t going to move from here. You might bring me a bit of grub with you﻿—I ain’t had a bite since yesterday teatime.”

===Chinese===
“你们要是换了我们，早把魔法受够了。”弗朗西斯说，“干吗你们自己不去——你和伯纳德？”

“我还真有点儿想去呢。”伯纳德出人意料地说，“只是不能大半夜里去，因为我十有八九会把靴子弄掉在地上。你去不去，凯西（Cathay）？”

“你明知道我早先就想去。”凯思琳嗔怪道。

“可怎么去得了呢？”其他人问。

“哦，”伯纳德说，“那个得好好想想。喂，小哥儿，我们得回去吃晚饭了。回头你还在这儿吗？”

“在。我哪儿也不挪窝儿。你们兴许能给我捎点儿吃的来——打昨天下午茶到现在，我一口东西还没下肚呢。”

===Original===
“I say,” said Francis kindly, “did they stop your grub to punish you for getting wet?”
“They didn’t know nothing about my getting wet,” he said darkly. “I didn’t never go back to the tents. I’ve cut my lucky, I ’ave ’ooked it, skedaddled, done a bunk, run away.”
“And where are you going?”
“*I* dunno,” said the Spangled Boy. “I’m running *from*, not to.”

===Chinese===
“我说，”弗朗西斯好声好气地问，“是因为你弄湿了，他们罚你不许吃饭吗？”

“我弄湿的事，他们压根儿一点儿也不知道。”他神色阴沉地说，“我压根儿没回帐篷。我溜了——拔腿跑了，逃之夭夭，卷铺盖开溜，跑出来啦。”

“那你要上哪儿去？”

“*我*哪儿知道。”亮片小子说，“我这是*从*哪儿逃出来，不是*往*哪儿去。”

## VI. The Mermaid’s Home / 六、美人鱼的家

===Original===
The parents of Mavis, Francis, Kathleen and Bernard were extremely sensible people. If they had not been, this story could never have happened. They were as jolly as any father and mother you ever met, but they were not always fussing and worrying about their children, and they understood perfectly well that children do not care to be absolutely always under the parental eye. So that, while there were always plenty of good times in which the whole family took part, there were also times when Father and Mother went off together and enjoyed themselves in their own grown-up way, while the children enjoyed themselves in theirs. It happened that on this particular afternoon there was to be a concert at Lymington﻿—Father and Mother were going. The children were asked whether they would like to go, and replied with equal courtesy and firmness.

===Chinese===
梅维斯（Mavis）、弗朗西斯（Francis）、凯思琳（Kathleen）和伯纳德（Bernard）的爸爸妈妈，是极其通情达理的人。倘若不是这样，这个故事根本就无从发生。他们跟你见过的任何一对父母一样乐乐呵呵，却不是一天到晚为孩子操心个没完，而且他们心里十分明白：孩子并不乐意时时刻刻都待在爹妈的眼皮子底下。所以，虽说全家一起享受的好时光从来不少，却也有这样的时候——爸爸妈妈自己结伴出门，用他们大人的法子自得其乐，孩子们也用孩子们的法子自得其乐。偏偏这个下午，利明顿（Lymington）有一场音乐会——爸爸妈妈要去。孩子们被问到想不想一起去，他们回答得又客气又坚决。

===Original===
“Very well then,” said Mother, “you do whatever you like best. I should play on the shore, I think, if I were you. Only don’t go around the corner of the cliff, because that’s dangerous at high tide. It’s safe so long as you’re within sight of the coast guards. Anyone have any more pie? No﻿—then I think I’ll run and dress.”

“Mother,” said Kathleen suddenly, “may we take some pie and things to a little boy who said he hadn’t had anything to eat since yesterday?”

“Where is he?” Father asked.

Kathleen blushed purple, but Mavis cautiously replied, “Outside. I’m sure we shall be able to find him.”

“Very well,” said Mother, “and you might ask Mrs. Pearce to give you some bread and cheese as well. Now, I must simply fly.”

“Cathay and I’ll help you, Mother,” said Mavis, and escaped the further questioning she saw in her father’s eye. The boys had slipped away at the first word of what seemed to be Kathleen’s amazing indiscretion about the waiting Rube.

===Chinese===
“那好，”妈妈说，“你们爱做什么就做什么吧。我要是你们，我想，就会在岸上玩。只是别绕到峭壁拐角那边去，涨潮的时候那儿危险。只要待在海岸卫队看得见你们的地方，就平安无事。谁还要馅饼？没有——那我想我得赶紧跑去换衣服了。”

“妈妈，”凯思琳忽然说，“我们可以带点馅饼什么的，去给一个小男孩吗？他说他从昨天起就什么东西都没吃过。”

“他在哪儿？”爸爸问。

凯思琳的脸涨成了深紫色，梅维斯却谨慎地答道：“在外头。我们肯定能把他找到。”

“很好，”妈妈说，“你们还可以请皮尔斯太太（Mrs. Pearce）再给你们一些面包和奶酪。好了，我这就得飞奔去了。”

“凯西（Cathay）跟我来帮您，妈妈。”梅维斯说着，躲开了她看出爸爸眼里还要追问下去的意思。凯思琳嘴里那个正等着的鲁本（Reuben），眼看要变成一桩惊人的失言——两个男孩听到头一个字，就悄悄溜掉了。

===Original===
“It was quite all right,” Kathleen argued later, as they went up the field, carefully carrying a plate of plum pie and the bread and cheese with not so much care and a certain bundle not carefully at all. “I saw flying in Mother’s eye before I spoke. And if you *can* ask leave before you do a thing it’s always safer.”

“And look here,” said Mavis. “If the Mermaid wants to see us we’ve only got to go down and say ‘Sabrina fair,’ and she’s certain to turn up. If it’s just seeing us she wants, and not another deadly night adventure.”

Reuben did not eat with such pretty manners as yours, perhaps, but there was no doubt about his enjoyment of the food they had brought, though he only stopped eating for half a second, to answer, “Prime. Thank you,” to Kathleen’s earnest inquiries.

===Chinese===
“我这么着一点儿错也没有。”过了一会儿，他们顺着田野往上走时，凯思琳辩解道。她小心翼翼地捧着一盘子李子馅饼，面包和奶酪就拿得不那么经心，还有一个小包袱，更是完全谈不上经心。“我还没开口，就看出妈妈眼里那份急着要走的意思了。再说，做一件事之前*能*先讨得许可，总归稳妥些。”

“还有，你们听着，”梅维斯说，“要是美人鱼想见咱们，咱们只消到海边去念一声‘美丽的萨布丽娜’，她准会出现——只要她想见的是咱们，而不是又要来一场要命的夜间冒险。”

鲁本吃东西的样子，兴许不如你的那么文雅好看，可孩子们带来的食物他吃得有多香，那是毋庸置疑的。他只停下嘴半秒钟，回答凯思琳热切的问话：“好极了。多谢。”

===Original===
“Now,” said Francis when the last crumb of cheese had disappeared and the last trace of plum juice had been licked from the spoon (a tin one, because, as Mrs. Pearce very properly said, you never know)﻿—“now, look here. We’re going straight down to the shore to try and see her. And if you like to come with us we can disguise you.”

“What in?” Reuben asked. “I did disguise myself once in a false beard and a green-colored mustache, but it didn’t take no one in for a moment, not even the dogs.”

“We thought,” said Mavis gently, “that perhaps the most complete disguise for you would be girl’s clothes﻿—because,” she added hastily to dispel the thundercloud on Reuben’s brow﻿—“because you’re such a manly boy. Nobody would give vent to a moment’s suspicion. It would be so very unlike *you*.”

“G’a long﻿—” said the Spangled Child, his dignity only half soothed.

“And I’ve brought you some of my things and some sandshoes of France’s, because, of course, mine are just kiddy shoes.”

===Chinese===
“我说，”弗朗西斯开腔了——这时最后一粒奶酪渣已经不见了，最后一丝李子汁也已经从汤匙上舔净（那是一把锡汤匙，因为正如皮尔斯太太一句很在理的话：谁也说不准）——“我说，你们听着。我们这就一直下到海边去，想法子见她。你要是乐意跟我们一起去，我们可以给你化装。”

“用什么化？”鲁本问，“我从前化过一回装，贴了假胡子，还安了两撇绿胡子，可它连一小会儿也没骗着一个人，连狗都没骗着。”

“我们想，”梅维斯温温和和地说，“对你来说，最彻头彻尾的化装，只怕就得数穿女孩的衣裳——因为，”她见鲁本眉梢上聚起一团乌云，赶忙添上一句，“因为你是个这么有男子气的男孩。谁也不会有半刻起疑。那副模样跟*你*本人实在太不相像了。”

“去你的——”亮片孩子（the Spangled Child）说，他那点受了伤的尊严只被抚平了一半。

“我还给你捎来了几件我的衣裳，还有弗兰的沙地鞋——当然啦，我自己的鞋都只是小娃娃的鞋。”

===Original===
At that Reuben burst out laughing and then hummed: “ ‘Go, flatterer, go, I’ll not trust to thy vow,’ ” quite musically.

“Oh, do you know the ‘Gypsy Countess’? How jolly!” said Kathleen.

“Old Mother Romaine knew a power of songs,” he said, suddenly grave. “Come on, chuck us in the togs.”

“You just take off your coat and come out and I’ll help you dress up,” was Francis’s offer.

“Best get a skirt over my kicksies first,” said Reuben, “case anyone comes by and recognizes the gypsy cheild. Hand us in the silk attire and jewels have to spare.”

===Chinese===
一听这话，鲁本放声大笑，接着还相当好听地哼唱起来：“‘去吧，谄媚的人，去吧，你的誓言我再也不信啦。’”

“哦，你知道《吉普赛伯爵夫人》（Gypsy Countess）？真好！”凯思琳说。

“老罗梅因大妈（Old Mother Romaine）会好多的歌。”他忽然正色道，“来吧，把衣裳递进来。”

“你干脆脱了外套出来，我帮你穿戴。”弗朗西斯出了个主意。

“顶好先把裙子递给我，套上我这两条腿，”鲁本说，“免得有人打这儿过，认出这个吉普赛小子。把那身绫罗绸缎递进来，珠宝要是有富余，也一并递来。”

===Original===
They pushed the blue serge skirt and jersey through the branches, which he held apart.

“Now the ’at,” he said, reaching a hand for it. But the hat was too large for the opening in the bush, and he had to come out of it. The moment he was out the girls crowned him with the big rush-hat, around whose crown a blue scarf was twisted, and Francis and Bernard each seizing a leg, adorned those legs with brown stockings and white sandshoes. Reuben, the spangled runaway from the gypsy camp, stood up among his new friends a rather awkward and quite presentable little girl.

“Now,” he said, looking down at his serge skirts with a queer smile, “now we shan’t be long.”

Nor were they. Thrusting the tin spoon and the pie plate and the discarded boots of Reuben into the kind shelter of the bush they made straight for the sea.

===Chinese===
他们把蓝哔叽裙和针织上衣从枝杈间递了进去，鲁本扒开枝条接着。

“现在，递帽子。”他说着，伸手来拿。可那顶帽子对灌木丛的口子来说太大了，他只好从里头钻出来。他刚一露面，姑娘们就给他戴上那顶大灯芯草帽，帽顶上缠着一条蓝头巾；弗朗西斯和伯纳德一人抓住一条腿，给那两条腿套上棕色长袜和白沙地鞋。鲁本——那个从吉普赛营地跑出来的亮片孩子——就这样在新朋友中间站起身来，成了个有点局促、却也相当像样的小姑娘。

“好了，”他低头望着自己身上的哔叽裙子，古怪地笑了笑，“好了，这回用不了多大工夫啦。”

果然没用多大工夫。他们把锡汤匙、馅饼盘子，还有鲁本脱下的靴子，一股脑儿塞进灌木丛好心的荫蔽里，直奔海边去了。

===Original===
When they got to that pleasant part of the shore which is smooth sand and piled shingle, lying between low rocks and high cliffs, Bernard stopped short.

“Now, look here,” he said, “if Sabrina fair turns up trumps I don’t mind going on with the adventure, but I won’t do it if Kathleen’s to be in it.”

“It’s not fair,” said Kathleen; “you said I might.”

“Did I?” Bernard most handsomely referred the matter to the others.

“Yes, you did,” said Francis shortly. Mavis said “Yes,” and Reuben clinched the matter by saying, “Why, you up and asked her yourself if she’d go along of you.”

“All right,” said Bernard calmly. “Then I shan’t go myself. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
走到海岸上那块叫人愉快的地方——平整的细沙和堆叠的卵石，夹在低低的礁石和高高的峭壁之间——伯纳德猛地站住了。

“我说，你们听着，”他开口道，“要是‘美丽的萨布丽娜’真个灵验，我不在乎把这冒险接着干下去；可要是凯思琳也得掺和在里头，我就不干。”

“这不公平，”凯思琳说，“你说过我可以的。”

“我说过吗？”伯纳德十分大方地请大伙儿来裁决这件事。

“说过，你说过。”弗朗西斯简短地说。梅维斯说了声“说过”，鲁本则一锤定音：“可不是嘛，还是你自己走上前去，问她肯不肯跟你一起去的呢。”

“很好，”伯纳德心平气和地说，“那我自己也不去了。就这么着。”

===Original===
“Oh, bother,” said at least three of the five; and Kathleen said: “I don’t see why I should always be out of everything.”

“Well,” said Mavis impatiently, “after all, there’s no danger in just trying to *see* the Mermaid. You promise you won’t do anything if Bernard says not﻿—that’ll do, I suppose? Though why you should be a slave to him just because he chooses to say you’re his particular sister, I don’t see. Will *that* do, Bear?”

“I’ll promise *anything*,” said Kathleen, almost in tears, “if you’ll only let me come with you all and see the Mermaid if she turns out to be seeable.”

So that was settled.

===Chinese===
“哦，真讨厌。”五个人里至少有三个这么说。凯思琳则说：“我不明白，凭什么不管什么事，回回都得把我撇下。”

“哎呀，”梅维斯不耐烦地说，“说到底，想法子*见见*美人鱼，又没有什么危险。你保证伯纳德说不行，你就什么都不做——这样总行了吧，我想？虽说只因为他高兴说你是他特别的妹妹，你就得当他的奴隶，事事听他使唤，这我实在不明白。这样*行*了吗，小熊（Bear）？”

“我*什么都*保证。”凯思琳说，眼泪几乎要掉下来，“只要你们肯让我跟大伙儿一起去，万一美人鱼真能见到，就让我见一见。”

事情就这么定了。

===Original===
Now came the question of where the magic words should be said.

Mavis and Francis voted for the edge of the rocks where the words had once already been so successfully spoken. Bernard said, “Why not here where we are?” Kathleen said rather sadly that any place would do as long as the Mermaid came when she was called. But Reuben, standing sturdily in his girl’s clothes, said:

“Look ’ere. When you’ve run away like what I have, least said soonest mended, and out of sight’s out of mind. What about caves?”

“Caves are too dry, except at high tide,” said Francis. “And then they’re too wet. Much.”

“Not all caves,” Reuben reminded him. “If we was to turn and go up by the cliff path. There’s a cave up there. I hid in it t’other day. Quite dry, except in one corner, and there it’s as wet as you want﻿—a sort of ’orse trough in the rocks it looks like﻿—only deep.”

“Is it seawater?” Mavis asked anxiously. And Reuben said:

“Bound to be, so near the sea and all.”

===Chinese===
接下来是魔法口诀该在哪儿念的问题。

梅维斯和弗朗西斯主张到礁石边上去，那句话从前在那儿念过一回，念得那么灵验。伯纳德说：“干吗不就在咱们站的地方念？”凯思琳倒有点伤感地说，哪儿都行，只要一叫美人鱼肯来就好。可是鲁本，穿着女孩衣裳稳稳当当站定，说道：

“听着。像俺这样跑出来的人，话越少越好，眼不见心不烦。找个山洞怎么样？”

“山洞太干，涨潮的时候除外，”弗朗西斯说，“可到那时候又太湿。湿得不得了。”

“不见得个个山洞都这样，”鲁本提醒他，“咱们要是回转身，打峭壁小路上去。上头有一个洞。前几天我还躲在里头来着。到处都干得很，只有一个角落不算——那个角落要多湿有多湿——瞧着像石头里嵌的一个‘马槽’——只是深。”

“是海水吗？”梅维斯急切地问。鲁本答道：

“离海这么近，多半错不了。”

===Original===
But it wasn’t. For when they had climbed the cliff path and Reuben had shown them where to turn aside from it, and had put aside the brambles and furze that quite hid the cave’s mouth, Francis saw at once that the water here could not be seawater. It was too far above the line which the waves reached, even in the stormiest weather.

“So it’s no use,” he explained.
