The two Lobster sentries presented claws as the Princess passed with her Staff through the narrow arch and onto the sandy plain of the sea bottom. The children were astonished to find that they could see quite plain a long way through the water﻿—as far as they could have seen in air, and the view was very like one kind of land view. First, the smooth flat sand dotted with copses of branching seaweed﻿—then woods of taller treelike weeds with rocks shelving up and up to a tall, rocky mountain. This mountain sent out a spur, then ran along beside the Merkingdom and joined the rock behind it; and it was along the narrow gorge so formed that the Under Folk were expected to advance. There were balls of seaweed floating in the air﻿—at least, it really now had grown to seem like air, though, of course, it was water﻿—but no signs of Scouts.

===Chinese===
公主带着她的参谋班子穿过窄窄的拱门，走上海底的沙原，两只龙虾哨兵行了举爪礼。孩子们惊讶地发现，隔着水他们也能看得很远很远——和在空气里看得一样远——而这景致又很像陆地上的一种风光。先是平整光滑的沙地，点缀着一丛丛枝杈纷披的海草——再往前，是一片片更高大的树状海草林，岩石层层上叠，一直叠到一座高耸的石山跟前。这座大山伸出一道山梁，沿着人鱼王国的一侧延展过去，同后面的山岩连成一体；深渊族若要进攻，料想就得沿着这条夹出来的窄窄峡谷推进。水里漂着一团团海草——如今这水看上去真已经像空气了，尽管它当然还是水——却不见侦察兵的影踪。

===Original===
Suddenly the balls of seaweed drew together and the Princess murmured, “I thought so,” as they formed into orderly lines, sank to the ground, and remained motionless for a moment, while one ball of seaweed stood in front of them.

===Chinese===
忽然，那些海草团聚拢起来，排成整整齐齐的队伍，落到地上，一动不动地停了片刻，一团海草立在队前。"果然不出我所料。"公主低声道。

===Original===
“It’s the Boy Scouts,” she said. “Your Reuben is giving them their orders.”

===Chinese===
"是童子军。"她说。"你们的鲁本正在向他们发号施令呢。"

===Original===
It seemed that she was right, for next moment the balls of seaweed drifted away in different directions, and the one who had stood before them drifted straight to the arch where the Princess and the children stood. It drifted in, pulled off its seaweed disguise, and was, in effect, Reuben.

===Chinese===
看来她说得不错，因为转眼间，海草团便朝四面八方漂散开去，方才立在队前的那一团径直漂向公主和孩子们所站的拱门。它漂进门来，褪下海草伪装——果然是鲁本。

===Original===
“We’ve found out something more, your Highness,” he said, saluting the Princess. “The vanguard are to be Sea Horses; you know, not the little ones, but the great things they have in the depths.”

===Chinese===
"我们又探出新情况了，殿下。"他向公主敬了个礼。"打头阵的是海马——您知道，不是那种小不点儿，是深渊里养的那种大家伙。"

===Original===
“No use our attacking the horses,” said the Princess. “They’re as hard as ice. Who rides them?”

===Chinese===
"咱们去攻海马没用。"公主说。"他们硬得像冰。骑在马上的是谁？"

===Original===
“The First Dipsys,” said Reuben. “They’re the young Under Folk who want to cut a dash. They call them the Forlorn Hopers, for short.”

===Chinese===
"第一深潜团。"鲁本说。"都是些想出风头的深渊族小伙子。大伙儿给这支部队起了个简称，叫'敢死队'。"

===Original===
“Have they got armor?”

===Chinese===
"他们有铠甲吗？"

===Original===
“No﻿—that’s their swank. They’ve no armor but their natural scales. Those look thick enough, though. I say, Princess, I suppose we Sea Urchins are free to do exactly as we choose?”

===Chinese===
"没有——那正是他们摆阔的地方。他们除了天生的鳞片啥护身也不穿。不过那些鳞片瞧着倒也够厚的。我说公主，我们海胆是不是想咋干就咋干？"

===Original===
“Yes,” said the Princess, “unless orders are given.”

===Chinese===
"是的。"公主说，"只要没有接到命令。"

===Original===
“Well, then﻿—my idea is that the Lobsters are the fellows to tackle the Sea Horses. Hold on to their tails, see? They can’t hurt the Lobsters because they can’t get at their own tails.”

===Chinese===
"那么——我的主意是，收拾海马就得靠龙虾这班家伙。死死钳住海马的尾巴，明白不？海马伤不着龙虾，因为海马够不着自己的尾巴。"

===Original===
“But when the Lobsters let go?” said the Princess.

===Chinese===
"可龙虾一松钳呢？"公主问。

===Original===
“The Lobsters wouldn’t let go till they had driven back the enemy,” said the Lobster Captain, saluting. “Your Highness, may I ask if you propose to take this Urchin’s advice?”

===Chinese===
"龙虾不把敌人打退，是决不松钳的。"龙虾上尉敬礼说。"殿下，请允许属下问一句：您打算采纳这只海胆的进言吗？"

===Original===
“Isn’t it good?” she asked.

===Chinese===
"难道这进言不好吗？"她问。

===Original===
“Yes, your Highness,” the Lobster Captain answered, “but it’s impertinent.”

===Chinese===
"好是好，殿下，"龙虾上尉答道，"只是他僭越失礼。"

===Original===
“I am the best judge of that,” said the Princess gently; “remember that these are noble volunteers, who are fighting for us of their own free will.”

===Chinese===
"失礼不失礼，我最有资格评断。"公主温和地说。"别忘了，这些是高贵的志愿者，是全凭自愿来替我们打仗的。"

===Original===
The Lobster saluted and was silent.

===Chinese===
龙虾敬了个礼，不再作声。

===Original===
“I cannot send the Lobsters,” said the Princess, “we need them to protect the gate. But the Crabs﻿—”

===Chinese===
"龙虾我派不得。"公主说，"守卫城门全靠他们。可是螃蟹——"

===Original===
“Ah, Highness, let us go,” pleaded the Lobster Captain.

===Chinese===
"啊，殿下，让我们去吧。"龙虾上尉央告道。

===Original===
“The Crabs cannot keep the gate,” said the Princess kindly. “You know they are not narrow enough. Francis, will you be my aide-de-camp and take a message to the Queen?”

===Chinese===
"螃蟹守不住城门。"公主和气地说。"你知道，他们不够窄。弗朗西斯，你愿意当我的传令副官，给女王送封信去吗？"

===Original===
“May I go, too?” asked Mavis.

===Chinese===
"我也能一起去吗？"梅维斯问。

===Original===
“Yes. But we must deliver a double assault. If the Crabs attack the Horses, who will deal with the riders?”

===Chinese===
"可以。但我们得来一场双路夹击。要是螃蟹去攻海马，谁来收拾骑手呢？"

===Original===
“I have an idea about that, too,” said Reuben.

===Chinese===
"这个我也有主意。"鲁本说。

===Original===
“If we could have some good heavy shoving regiment﻿—and someone sharp to finish them off. The Swordfish, perhaps?”

===Chinese===
"要是能有一支顶会用蛮力猛撞的重装团——再有谁的尖家伙来结果他们。剑鱼，成不？"

===Original===
“You are a born general,” the Princess said; “but you don’t quite know our resources. The United Narwhals can do the shoving, as you call it﻿—and their horns are sharp and heavy. Now”﻿—she took a smooth white chalkstone from the seafloor, and a ready Lobster brought her a sharpened haddock bone. She wrote quickly, scratching the letters deep on the chalk. “Here,” she said, “take this to the Queen. You will find her at Headquarters at the Palace yard. Tell her everything. I have only asked for the two regiments; you must explain the rest. I don’t suppose there’ll be any difficulty in getting through our lines, but, if there should be, the password is ‘Glory’ and the countersign is ‘or Death.’ And hurry, hurry, hurry for your lives!”

===Chinese===
"你是个天生的将军。"公主说，"不过你对我们的家底还不摸门。联军独角鲸就能干你说的一桩'撞'的差事——他们的角又尖又沉。来——"她从海底拾起一块光滑的白垩石，一只手脚利落的龙虾给她递来一根磨尖的黑线鳕骨。她飞快地写起来，字划得深深地嵌进白垩。"喏，"她说，"把这个带给女王。她在王宫院子的总指挥部里。把一切原原本本告诉她。信上我只讨了两个团，其余的你们得讲清楚。我想，穿过咱们的防线不会有什么难处，万一真有，口令是'荣耀'，回令是'或死亡'。快去，快去，拿出性命地快去！"

===Original===
Never before had Mavis and Francis felt anything like the glow of excitement and importance which warmed them as they went up the long tunnel to take the message to the Queen.

===Chinese===
梅维斯和弗朗西斯顺着长隧道往上去给女王送信，一路只觉心头一团热烘烘的，又是激动，又觉得自己责任重大——这样的滋味，他们长这么大还是头一回尝到。

===Original===
“But where is the Palace?” Mavis said, and they stopped, looking at each other.

===Chinese===
"可是王宫在哪儿呀？"梅维斯说。两人停下脚步，你望望我，我望望你。

===Original===
“I’ll show you, please,” said a little voice behind them. They turned quickly to find a small, spruce, gentlemanly Mackerel at their heels. “I’m one of the Guides,” it said. “I felt sure you’d need me. This way, sir, please,” and it led the way across the gardens in and out of the clumps of trees and between the seaweed hedges till they came to the Palace. Rows and rows of soldiers surrounded it, all waiting impatiently for the word of command that should send them to meet the enemies of their country.

===Chinese===
"请让我来带路吧。"身后一个细细的声音说。他们急忙回头，只见一条干净利落、绅士派头的小鲭鱼跟在脚后。"我是向导队的一员。"它说，"我料定你们会用得着我。这边请，先生。"它领着他们穿过花园，绕进绕出树丛，又穿行在海草树篱之间，一直把他们领到王宫跟前。宫外四周列着一排又一排的士兵，个个心急火燎地等着一声令下，好去迎击祖国的仇敌。

===Original===
“Glory,” said the gentlemanly Mackerel, as he passed the outposts.

===Chinese===
"荣耀。"那位绅士派头的鲭鱼走过前哨时说。

===Original===
“Or Death,” replied the sentinel Sea Bream.

===Chinese===
"或死亡。"哨兵海鲷应道。

===Original===
The Queen was in the courtyard, in which the children had received their ovation﻿—so short a time ago, and yet how long it seemed. Then the courtyard had been a scene of the calm and charming gaiety of a nation at peace; now it was full of the ardent, intense inactivity of waiting warriors. The Queen in her gleaming coral armor met them as the password opened a way to her through the close-packed ranks of the soldiers. She took the stone and read it, and with true royal kindness she found time, even at such a moment, for a word of thanks to the messengers.

===Chinese===
女王正在院子里，孩子们不久前才在这院子里受过满城欢呼——明明过去没多久，却仿佛隔了许久许久。那时候，院子里是一派太平之邦从容可亲的欢乐景象；如今满院都是待命的战士，一片炽烈而紧张的按兵不动。口令替他们从密密层层的军阵中开出一条路，身着晶亮珊瑚铠甲的女王迎上前来。她接过石板，读罢，凭着真正王家的仁厚，纵在这样的时候，也匀出工夫向两位信使道了谢。

===Original===
“See the Narwhals start,” she added, “and then back to your posts with all speed. Tell your commanding officer that so far the Book People have made no sign, but the golden gate is strongly defended by the King’s Own Cod, and﻿—”

===Chinese===
"看着独角鲸开拔，"她又吩咐道，"然后火速赶回岗位。告诉你们的指挥官：书里人至今没有动静，不过金门有国王直属鳕鱼团重兵把守，还有——"

===Original===
“I didn’t know there was a King,” said Francis.

===Chinese===
"我还不知道有位国王呢。"弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
The Queen looked stern, and the Mackerel guide jerked Francis’s magic coattail warningly and whispered “Hush!”

===Chinese===
女王登时神色一凛，鲭鱼向导警告似的扯了扯弗朗西斯那件魔法甲衣的后襟，悄声道："嘘！"

===Original===
“The King,” said the Queen quietly, “is no more. He was lost at sea.”

===Chinese===
"国王，"女王平静地说，"已经不在了。他在海上失踪了。"

===Original===
When the splendid steady column of Narwhals had marched off to its appointed place the children bowed to the Queen and went back to their posts.

===Chinese===
威武而整齐的独角鲸纵队开赴指定的位置后，孩子们向女王鞠了一躬，回自己的岗位去了。

===Original===
“I’m sorry I said anything,” said Francis to the Mackerel, “but I didn’t know. Besides, how can a Mer-king be lost at sea?”

===Chinese===
"对不起，我不该多嘴。"弗朗西斯对鲭鱼说，"可我原先不知道啊。再说，人鱼国的国王怎么会在海上失踪呢？"

===Original===
“Aren’t your Kings lost on land?” asked the Mackerel, “or if not Kings, men quite as good? What about explorers?”

===Chinese===
"你们的国王在陆地上就不失踪了吗？"鲭鱼反问，"就算国王不失踪，同他们一样了不起的人呢？那些探险家怎么说？"

===Original===
“I see,” said Mavis; “and doesn’t anyone know what has become of him?”

===Chinese===
"我明白了。"梅维斯说。"那么，没有人知道他的下落吗？"

===Original===
“No,” said the Mackerel; “he has been lost for a very long time. We fear the worst. If he were alive he would have come back. We think the Under Folk have him. They bewitch prisoners so that they forget who they are. Of course, there’s the antidote. Every uniform is made with a little antidote pocket just over the heart.” He put his fin inside his scales and produced a little golden case, just like a skate’s egg. “You’ve got them, too, of course,” he added. “If you are taken prisoner swallow the contents at once.”

===Chinese===
"没有。"鲭鱼说，"他失踪很久很久了。我们只怕凶多吉少。他要还活着，早回来了。我们想，他是落在深渊族手里了。他们给俘虏施魔法，叫俘虏忘掉自己是谁。当然啦，有解药。每件军服都在心口正上方缝了一个小小的解药袋。"他把鱼鳍探进鳞片底下，摸出一只小小的金盒子，活像一枚鳐鱼卵。"当然，你们也一人有一只。"他补了一句。"万一被俘，立刻把里面的东西吞下去。"

===Original===
“But if you forget who you are,” said Francis, “don’t you forget the antidote?”

===Chinese===
"可要是你连自己是谁都忘了，"弗朗西斯说，"不会把解药也忘了吗？"

===Original===
“No charm,” the Mackerel assured him, “is strong enough to make one forget one’s counter-charm.”

===Chinese===
"天底下没有一道魔咒，"鲭鱼让他放心，"强得能叫人忘掉自己的解咒之物。"

===Original===
And now they were back at the Lobster-guarded gate. The Princess ran to meet them.

===Chinese===
这时他们回到了龙虾把守的城门。公主跑上前来迎接。

===Original===
“What a time you’ve been,” she said. “Is all well? Have the Narwhals taken up their position?”

===Chinese===
"你们去了这么半天。"她说。"一切都好吗？独角鲸进入位置了吗？"

===Original===
Satisfied on this point, she led the children up a way long and steep to a window in the wall whence they could look down on the ravine and see the advance of the foe. The Narwhals were halted about halfway up the ravine, where it widened to a sort of amphitheater. Here, among the rocks, they lay in ambush, waiting for the advance of the foe.

===Chinese===
弄清了这一点，她便领着孩子们登上一条又长又陡的路，来到城墙上一扇窗前，从窗口可以俯瞰峡谷，望见敌人的推进。独角鲸停在峡谷约莫半程的地方，那儿路面开阔，形成一个圆圆的看台似的谷地。他们就在那儿隐入岩石之间设伏，专候敌人前来。

===Original===
“If it hadn’t been for you, Reuben,” said the Princess, as they leaned their elbows on the broad rocky ledge of the window, “they might easily have stormed the North Tower﻿—we should not have been ready﻿—all our strongest defenses were massed on the south side. It was there they attacked last time, so the history books tell us.”

===Chinese===
"要是没有你，鲁本，"公主说——这时他们正把胳膊肘支在窗口宽阔的石沿上——"他们很可能轻而易举就攻陷了北塔——我们会措手不及——我们最坚固的防御都集结在南边。上一回他们就是从那儿进攻的，史书上是这么说的。"

===Original===
And now a heavy, thundering sound, faint yet terrible, announced the approach of the enemy﻿—and far away across the sea plain something could be seen moving. A ball of seaweed seemed to drift up the ravine.

===Chinese===
这时，一阵沉重如雷的轰响传来，隐隐约约却令人心悸，宣告敌人逼近了——远远的海原上，已经望得见有什么东西在移动。一团海草仿佛正顺着峡谷往上漂。

===Original===
“A Sea Urchin gone to give the alarm,” said the Princess; “what splendid things Boy Scouts are. We didn’t have them in the last war. My dear father only invented them just before﻿—” She paused and sighed. “Look,” she said.

===Chinese===
"是海胆去报警了。"公主说，"童子军真是好东西。上一场战争里我们还没有他们呢。是我亲爱的父亲在开战前不久才把他们创办起来的——"她顿住了，叹了口气。"看。"她说。

===Original===
The enemy’s heavy cavalry were moving in a solid mass toward Merland﻿—the great Sea Horses, twenty feet long, and their great riders, who must have been eight or ten feet high, came more and more quickly, heading to the ravine. The riders were the most terrible beings the children had ever seen. Clothed from head to feet in closely fitting scales, with large heads, large ears, large mouths and blunt noses and large, blind-looking eyes, they sat each erect on his armored steed, the long harpoons swaying lightly in their enormous hands.

===Chinese===
敌人的重骑兵正结成密不透风的队形朝人鱼国压过来——巨大的海马足有二十英尺长，马背上高大魁梧的骑手少说也有八到十英尺高，越走越快，直指峡谷。那些骑手是孩子们平生所见最可怕的家伙。他们从头到脚裹着紧身的鳞甲，大脑袋、大耳朵、大嘴巴、塌鼻梁，还有一双大而无神的眼睛。他们个个笔直地坐在披甲的战骑上，长长的鱼叉在巨大的手掌里轻轻晃动。

===Original===
The Sea Horses quickened their pace﻿—and a noise like a hoarse trumpet rang out.

===Chinese===
海马们加快了步子——一声嘶哑号角似的声响划破了海水。

===Original===
“They are sounding the charge,” said the Princess; and as she spoke the Under Folk charged at the ravine, in a determined, furious onrush.

===Chinese===
"他们在吹冲锋号了。"公主说。话音未落，深渊族已经朝峡谷猛冲过来，那是一股破釜沉舟般的狂暴势头。

===Original===
“Oh, no one can stand up against that﻿—they can’t,” said Cathay, in despair.

===Chinese===
"哦，谁也顶不住那样的冲势——顶不住的。"凯西绝望地说。

===Original===
From the window they could see right down onto the amphitheater, where the Narwhals were concealed.

===Chinese===
从窗口，他们可以一直俯瞰到那座看台似的谷地，独角鲸们就藏在那里。

===Original===
On came the Sea Cavalry﻿—so far unresisted﻿—but as they neared the ambush bunches of seaweed drifted in the faces of the riders. They floundered and strove to push away the clinging stuff﻿—and as they strove the Narwhals made their sortie﻿—drove their weight against the riders and hurled them from their horses, and from the covers of the rocks the Crabs advanced with an incredible speed and caught the tails of the Sea Horses in their inexorable claws. The riders lay on the ground. The horses were rearing and prancing with fear and pain as the clouds of seaweed, each with a prickly Sea Urchin in it, flung themselves against their faces. The riders stood up, fighting to the last; but the harpoons were no match for the Narwhal’s horns.

===Chinese===
海马骑兵冲过来了——一路无人抵挡——可等他们逼近埋伏，一团团海草便飘进了骑手们的脸。他们跌跌撞撞，拼命要扯掉那些缠脸的东西——趁他们挣扎的当口，独角鲸们杀了出来——用全身的分量撞向骑手，把他们掀下马背；螃蟹们也从岩石掩体后面以快得叫人难以置信的速度冲上来，用不依不饶的大钳钳住了海马的尾巴。骑手们跌倒在地。海马们又惊又痛，人立起来乱蹦乱跳，一团团海草——每团里都藏着一只浑身是刺的海胆——劈头盖脸地扑向它们的脸。骑手们爬起身，血战到最后；可是鱼叉终究敌不过独角鲸的角。

===Original===
“Come away,” said the Princess.

===Chinese===
"我们走吧。"公主说。

===Original===
Already the Sea Horses, urged by the enormous Crabs, were retreating in the wildest disorder, pursued by Narwhals and harassed by Sea Urchins.

===Chinese===
这时，海马们已被那些大螃蟹驱赶着，乱成一团地溃退，独角鲸在后追杀，海胆们四下骚扰。

===Original===
The Princess and the children went back to the Lobster sentries.

===Chinese===
公主和孩子们回到龙虾哨兵那里。

===Original===
“Repulsed,” said the Princess, “with heavy loss”﻿—and the Lobsters cheered.

===Chinese===
"击退了，"公主说，"敌人损失惨重。"龙虾们一听，欢呼起来。

===Original===
“How’s that, Princess?” said a ball of seaweed, uncurling itself at the gate and presenting the familiar features of Reuben.

===Chinese===
"战况如何，公主？"一团海草在门口舒展开来，露出鲁本那张熟面孔。

===Original===
“How is it?” she said. “It is Victory. And we owe it to you. But you’re wounded?”

===Chinese===
"如何？"她说。"是大胜。这全亏了你。可你受伤了？"

===Original===
“Only a scratch,” said Reuben; “harpoon just missed me.”

===Chinese===
"就擦破点儿皮。"鲁本说，"鱼叉差一丁点儿就扎着我了。"

===Original===
“Oh, Reuben, you are a hero,” said Cathay.

===Chinese===
"哦，鲁本，你真是个英雄。"凯西说。

===Original===
“Get along, you silly,” he answered gracefully.

===Chinese===
"去你的，小傻瓜。"他潇洒地答道。

## IX The Book People / 九、书里人

===Original===
Even in the midst of war there are intervals for refreshments. Our own soldiers, no matter how fierce, must eat to live, and the same is the case with the submarine regiments. The Crustacean Brigade took advantage of the lull in hostilities which followed the defeat of the Sea Horses to march back to the Palace and have a meal. A very plain meal it was, too, and very different from the “Banquet of Ovations,” as Cathay pointed out afterward. There were no prettily spread tables decorated with bunches of seaweed, no plates or knives or forks. The food was passed around by hand, and there was one drinking horn (a sea cow’s horn) to every six soldiers. They all sat on the ground as you do at a picnic, and the Queen came and spoke a few hurried words to them when on her way to strengthen the defenses of the golden gate. And, as I said, the food was plain. However, everyone had enough to eat, which was the main thing. Baskets of provisions were sent down to the Lobsters’ guardroom.

===Chinese===
就在战争的当口，也有用饭休整的间歇。我们自己的士兵再勇猛，也得吃饭才能活命，海底的军团同样如此。海马军败退之后，战事稍见间歇，甲壳旅便趁这个空当开回王宫，吃了一顿饭。这顿饭可真够简单，跟那回“欢呼礼赞的盛宴”大不相同——凯西后来特地点出这一层。没有一张张摆得漂漂亮亮、缀着海草束的桌子，没有盘子，没有刀叉。吃食用手传递分食，每六名士兵合用一只角杯（海牛角做的）。大家全坐在地上，像你们野餐时那样；女王在赶去加固金门防务的半道上，顺路来对他们匆匆讲了几句话。还有，正像我说的，饭食是简单的。不过人人都吃饱了，这才是顶要紧的。一筐筐口粮送下了龙虾卫队室。

===Original===
“It is important,” said Princess Freia, “that our men should be on the spot in case they are needed, and the same with the dinner. I shall go down with the provisions and keep their hearts up.”

===Chinese===
“要紧的是，”弗蕾亚公主（Princess Freia）说，“我们的人得守在原地，万一用得着马上就在；饭食也一样。我跟着口粮一块儿下去，给他们鼓鼓劲儿。”

===Original===
“Yes, dear, do,” said the Princess Maia; “but don’t do anything rash. No sorties now. You Lobsters are so terribly brave. But you know Mother said you weren’t to. Ah me! War is a terrible thing! What a state the rivers will get into with all this water going on, and the winds all loose and doing as they like. It’s horrible to think about. It will take ages to get things straight again.”

===Chinese===
“好，亲爱的，去吧，”迈亚公主说，“可千万别鲁莽。眼下可不许出击。你们龙虾一个个勇猛得吓人。可你知道，妈妈吩咐过不许你们出去。唉！战争真是可怕！这么多水闹个不停，那些河还不知要乱成什么样，风又全放了羊，爱怎么刮就怎么刮。想想都叫人心惊。要把一切重新理顺，得花上好多年月。”

===Original===
(Her fears were only too well founded. All this happened last year﻿—and you know what a wet summer that was.)

===Chinese===
（她的忧虑一点儿也没有落空。这一切都发生在去年——那年夏天雨多得什么样，你们是知道的。）

===Original===
“I know, dear,” said Freia; “but I know now who broke the sky, and it is very, very sorry﻿—so we won’t rub it in, will we?”

===Chinese===
“我知道，亲爱的，”弗蕾亚说，“不过我现在知道是谁捅破了天，而且他们已经非常、非常后悔了——咱们就别老提这茬儿了，好不好？”

===Original===
“I didn’t mean to,” said Maia, smiling kindly at the children, and went off to encourage her Lobsters.

===Chinese===
“我本来也不是有心的。”迈亚说着，朝孩子们和蔼地笑了笑，走开去给她的龙虾们打气了。

===Original===
“And now,” said Francis, when the meal was over, “what are we going to do next?”

===Chinese===
“那么现在，”饭吃完了，弗朗西斯说，“我们下一步做什么？”

===Original===
“We can’t do anything but wait for news,” said the Princess. “Our Scouts will let us know soon enough. I only hope the Book People won’t attack us at the same time as the Under Folk. That’s always the danger.”

===Chinese===
“除了等消息，什么也做不了。”公主说，“我们的斥候很快就会来报信。我只盼着书里人（the Book People）别跟深渊族同时打上来。那一向是最危险的事。”

===Original===
“How could they get in?” Mavis asked.

===Chinese===
“他们怎么进得来呢？”梅维斯问。

===Original===
“Through the golden door,” said the Princess. “Of course they couldn’t do anything if we hadn’t read the books they’re in. That’s the worst of Education. We’ve all read such an awful lot, and that unlocks the books and they can come out if anyone calls them. Even our fish are intolerably well read﻿—except the Porpoises, dear things, who never could read anything. That’s why the golden door is guarded by them, of course.”

===Chinese===
“从金门进来，”公主说，“当然啦，倘若我们不曾读过载着他们的那些书，他们便什么也做不成。这就是教育最糟糕的地方。我们人人读过的书都多得吓人，这就把那些书都解锁了，只要有人一唤，他们就能出来。连我们的鱼都博学得叫人吃不消——只有鼠海豚例外，亲爱的宝贝，它们从来什么都读不进。所以嘛，派它们守卫金门，正是这个缘故。”

===Original===
“If not having read things is useful,” said Mavis, “we’ve read almost nothing. Couldn’t we help guard the door?”

===Chinese===
“要是没读过书反倒顶用，”梅维斯说，“我们差不多什么都没读过。我们能不能帮着守门？”

===Original===
“The very thing,” said the Princess joyously; “for you possess the only weapon that can be used against these people or against the authors who created them. If you can truthfully say to them, ‘I never heard of you,’ your words become a deadly sword that strikes at their most sensitive spot.”

===Chinese===
“正合适！”公主喜滋滋地说，“因为你们拥有唯一一件用得上的兵器，对付得了这些人，也对付得了造出他们的那些作者。只要你们能真心实意地对他们说一句‘我从没听说过你’，这句话就化作一柄致命的剑，正正刺中他们最敏感的痛处。”

===Original===
“What spot?” asked Bernard. And the Princess answered, “Their vanity.”

===Chinese===
“什么痛处？”伯纳德问。公主答道：“他们的虚荣心。”

===Original===
So the little party went toward the golden door and found it behind a thick wall of Porpoises. Incessant cries came from beyond the gates, and to every cry they answered like one Porpoise, “We never heard of you. You can’t come in. You can’t come in. We never heard of you.”

===Chinese===
于是一小队人朝金门走去，发现门后排着一堵厚厚的鼠海豚墙。门外的叫喊声没个停歇，它们对每一声叫喊都齐声应答，像一只鼠海豚在说话：“我们从没听说过你。你不能进来。你不能进来。我们从没听说过你。”

===Original===
“We shan’t be any good here,” said Bernard, among the thick, rich voices of the Porpoises. “They can keep anyone back.”

===Chinese===
“我们在这儿派不上什么用场，”伯纳德在鼠海豚们浑厚圆润的嗓音中说，“任凭什么人它们都拦得住。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said the Princess; “but if the Book Folk look through the gate and see that they’re only Porpoises their wounded vanity will heal, and they’ll come on as strongly as ever. Whereas if they did find human beings who have never heard of them the wounds ought to be mortal. As long as you are able truthfully to say that you don’t know them they can’t get in.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”公主说，“可要是书里人隔着门一瞧，看见守门的不过是一群鼠海豚，他们受了伤的虚荣心就会痊愈，攻势就会跟先前一样猛。反过来，倘若他们发现守门的竟是从没听说过他们的人类，那伤口就该是致命的了。只要你们还能真心实意地说不认识他们，他们就进不来。”

===Original===
“Reuben would be the person for this,” said Francis. “I don’t believe he’s read *anything*!”

===Chinese===
“干这个的合适人选该数鲁本，”弗朗西斯说，“我看他*任何东西*都没读过！”

===Original===
“Well, we haven’t read much,” said Cathay comfortably; “at least, not about nasty people.”

===Chinese===
“呃，我们也没读过多少，”凯西心安理得地说，“至少，讲讨厌家伙的书我们没读过。”

===Original===
“I wish I hadn’t,” sighed the Princess through the noise of the voices outside the gate. “I know them all. You hear that cold squeak? That’s Mrs. Fairchild. And that short, sharp, barking sound﻿—that’s Aunt Fortune. The sort of growl that goes on all the time is Mr. Murdstone, and that icy voice is Rosamund’s mother﻿—the one who was so hateful about the purple jar.”

===Chinese===
“我倒情愿我没读过。”公主透过门外种种嗓音的喧嚣叹道，“他们我个个认识。你听见那声冷冰冰的尖叫没有？那是费尔柴尔德太太。还有那短促尖厉的汪汪声——那是福琼姨妈（Aunt Fortune）。那响个不停的低吼是默德斯通先生（Mr. Murdstone）；那个冰凉的声音是罗莎蒙德的妈妈——就是为了那只紫罐子可恶得不得了的那个。”

===Original===
“I’m afraid we know some of those,” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
“这些人里头，恐怕有几个我们是认得的。”梅维斯说。

===Original===
“Then be careful not to say you don’t. There are heaps you don’t know﻿—John Knox and Machiavelli and Don Diego and Tippoo Sahib and Sally Brass and﻿—I *must* go back. If anything should happen, fling your arms round the nearest Porpoise and trust to luck. These Book People can’t kill﻿—they can only stupefy.”

===Chinese===
“那可当心，别说不认得。你们不认得的多着呢——约翰·诺克斯（John Knox）、马基雅维利（Machiavelli）、唐·迭戈（Don Diego）、提普·萨希布（Tippoo Sahib）、萨莉·布拉斯（Sally Brass），还有——我*必须*回去了。万一出什么事，就张开胳膊抱住离得最近的鼠海豚，剩下的听天由命。这些书里人杀不了人——他们只会把人变傻。”

===Original===
“But how do you know them all?” Mavis asked. “Do they often attack you?”

===Chinese===
“可你怎么会全认识他们？”梅维斯问，“他们常来攻打你们吗？”

===Original===
“No, only when the sky falls. But they always howl outside the gate at the full moon.”

===Chinese===
“不，只有天塌下来的时候才来。不过每逢月圆，他们总在门外嚎。”

===Original===
So saying she turned away and disappeared in the crowd of faithful Porpoises.

===Chinese===
说着，她转过身去，消失在那群忠心耿耿的鼠海豚当中。

===Original===
And outside the noise grew louder and the words more definite.

===Chinese===
门外的喧声越来越响，字句也越来越分明。

===Original===
“I am Mrs. Randolph. Let me in!”

===Chinese===
“我是兰道夫太太（Mrs. Randolph）。放我进去！”

===Original===
“I am good Mrs. Brown. Let me in!”

===Chinese===
“我是好心的布朗太太。放我进去！”

===Original===
“I am Eric, or Little by Little. I *will* come in!”

===Chinese===
“我是埃里克，就是那个‘一点又一点’（Eric, or Little by Little）。我*偏要*进去！”

===Original===
“I am Elsie, or Like a Little Candle. Let me in﻿—let me in!”

===Chinese===
“我是埃尔茜，就是那个‘像一支小蜡烛’（Elsie, or Like a Little Candle）。放我进去——放我进去！”

===Original===
“I am Mrs. Markham.”

===Chinese===
“我是马卡姆太太。”

===Original===
“I am Mrs. Squeers.”

===Chinese===
“我是斯奎尔斯太太（Mrs. Squeers）。”

===Original===
“I am Uriah Heep.”

===Chinese===
“我是尤赖亚·希普（Uriah Heep）。”

===Original===
“I am Montdidier.”

===Chinese===
“我是蒙迪迪耶（Montdidier）。”

===Original===
“I am King John.”

===Chinese===
“我是约翰王（King John）。”

===Original===
“I am Caliban.”

===Chinese===
“我是凯列班（Caliban）。”

===Original===
“I am the Giant Blunderbore.”

===Chinese===
“我是巨人布伦德博（the Giant Blunderbore）。”

===Original===
“I am the Dragon of Wantley.”

===Chinese===
“我是旺特利之龙（the Dragon of Wantley）。”

===Original===
And they all cried, again and again: “Let us in! Let me in! Let me in!”

===Chinese===
接着他们全体一遍又一遍地喊：“让我们进去！放我进去！放我进去！”

===Original===
The strain of listening for the names and calling out “I don’t know you!” when they didn’t, and saying nothing when they did, became almost unbearable. It was like that horrid game with the corners of the handkerchief, “Hold fast” and “Let loose,” and you have to remember to do the opposite. Sooner or later an accident is bound to happen, and the children felt a growing conviction that it would be sooner.

===Chinese===
竖着耳朵听名字，不认识的就得喊一声“我不认识你！”，认识的就得闭紧嘴巴——这份紧张几乎叫人受不住。这就像手绢角上那个叫人头疼的游戏，“抓紧”和“松手”，偏偏你得记住反着来。早晚要出岔子，这是免不了的；孩子们心里越来越确信，这个“早晚”多半是“早”。

===Original===
“What will happen if they do get in?” Cathay asked a neighboring Porpoise.

===Chinese===
“他们要是真进来了，会怎么样？”凯西问旁边的一只鼠海豚。

===Original===
“Can’t say, miss, I’m sure,” it answered.

===Chinese===
“说不上，小姐，真的说不上。”它答道。

===Original===
“But what will you do?”

===Chinese===
“那你们会怎么做？”

===Original===
“Obstruct them in the execution of our duty,” it answered. “You see, miss, they can’t kill; they can only stupefy, and they can’t stupefy us, ’cause why? We’re that stupid already we can’t hold no more. That’s why they trust us to defend the golden gate,” it added proudly.

===Chinese===
“阻挠他们，执行我们的职责。”它答道，“您瞧，小姐，他们杀不了人，只会把人变傻；可他们没法把我们变傻，为什么呢？我们已经傻到了家，再傻也傻不进去了。所以才放心把守卫金门的差事托给我们。”它又骄傲地补了一句。

===Original===
The babel of voices outside grew louder and thicker, and the task of knowing when to say “I don’t know you,” and so wound the vanity of the invaders, grew more and more difficult. At last the disaster, foreseen for some time, with a growing plainness, came upon them.

===Chinese===
门外的嘈杂声越来越响、越来越稠，要分清什么时候该说“我不认识你”好刺伤入侵者的虚荣心，也越来越难了。终于，那桩被他们料想了许久、而且料想得一天比一天明白的祸事，落到他们头上来了。

===Original===
“I am the Great Seal,” said a thick, furry voice.

===Chinese===
“我是御玺。”一个又粗又毛茸茸的嗓音说。

===Original===
“I don’t know you,” cried Cathay.

===Chinese===
“我不认识你。”凯西喊道。

===Original===
“You do﻿—he’s in history. James the Second dropped him in the Thames,” said Francis. “Yes, you’ve done it again.”

===Chinese===
“你认识——他在历史书里。詹姆斯二世把他掉进了泰晤士河。”弗朗西斯说，“得，你又闯祸了。”

===Original===
“Shut up,” said Bernard.

===Chinese===
“闭嘴。”伯纳德说。

===Original===
The last two remarks were made in a deep silence, broken only by the heavy breathing of the Porpoises. The voices behind the golden gate had died down and ceased. The Porpoises massed their heavy bulk close to the door.

===Chinese===
最后这两句话是在一片深深的寂静里说出来的，只被鼠海豚们粗重的呼吸声打破。金门后面的声音渐渐低下去，终于停息了。鼠海豚们把沉重的身躯紧紧挤凑在门边。

===Original===
“Remember the Porpoises,” said Francis. “Don’t forget to hold on to a Porpoise.”

===Chinese===
“记住鼠海豚，”弗朗西斯说，“别忘了抱紧一只鼠海豚。”

===Original===
Four of these amiable if unintellectual creatures drew away from their companions, and one came to the side of each child.

===Chinese===
这些家伙说和蔼也和蔼，说没脑子也没脑子；其中四只从伙伴们中间退了出来，一只来到一个孩子身边。

===Original===
Every eye was fixed on the golden door, and then slowly﻿—very slowly, the door began to open. As it opened it revealed the crowd that stood without﻿—cruel faces, stupid faces, crafty faces, sullen faces, angry faces, not a single face that you ever could wish to see again.

===Chinese===
每一只眼睛都盯住金门，随后，慢慢地——非常慢地，门开了。门一开，便露出门外站着的那群人——狰狞的脸、愚蠢的脸、奸诈的脸、阴沉的脸、狂怒的脸，没有一张是你愿意再看见第二眼的。

===Original===
Then slowly, terribly, without words, the close ranks of the Book People advanced. Mrs. Fairchild, Mrs. Markham, and Mrs. Barbauld led the van. Closely following came the Dragon of Wantley, the Minotaur, and the Little Man that Sintram knew. Then came Mr. Murdstone, neat in a folded white neckcloth, and clothes as black as his whiskers. Miss Murdstone was with him, every bead of her alight with gratified malice. The children found that they knew, without being told, the name of each foe now advancing on them. Paralyzed with terror, they watched the slow and terrible advance. It was not till Eric, or Little by Little, broke the silence with a whoop of joy and rushed upon them that they remembered their own danger, and clutched the waiting Porpoises. Alas! it was too late. Mrs. Markham had turned a frozen glare upon them, Mrs. Fairchild had wagged an admonitory forefinger, wave on wave of sheer stupidity swept over them, and next moment they lost consciousness and sank, each with his faithful Porpoise, into the dreamless sleep of the entirely unintelligent. In vain the main body of the Porpoises hurled themselves against the intruders; their heroism was fruitless. Overwhelmed by the heavy truisms wielded by the enemy, they turned and fled in disorder, and the conquering army entered Merland.

===Chinese===
随后，缓慢地、可怕地、一言不发地，书里人的密集队列开动了。费尔柴尔德太太、马卡姆太太和巴鲍德太太（Mrs. Barbauld）打头阵。紧跟在后面的是旺特利之龙、弥诺陶洛斯（the Minotaur）和辛特拉姆（Sintram）认得的那个小人儿。再后面是默德斯通先生，脖颈上端端正正系着叠好的白领巾，衣裳黑得跟他的连鬓胡子一样。默德斯通小姐（Miss Murdstone）随在他身边，浑身每颗珠子都因得逞的恶意而亮闪闪的。孩子们发觉，此刻朝他们逼近的每一个仇敌，他们不用谁说，就认得名字。他们吓瘫了，眼睁睁望着那缓慢而可怕的逼近。直到埃里克——“一点又一点”——一声欢呼划破寂静、朝他们猛扑过来，他们才想起自身的危险，慌忙抓住等候在旁的鼠海豚。唉！太迟了。马卡姆太太朝他们投来一道冷凝的怒视，费尔柴尔德太太摇了摇训诫的食指，一阵又一阵纯粹的愚蠢席卷过他们，下一刻他们便失去了知觉，各自带着自己忠心的鼠海豚，沉入了全然无智者的无梦酣睡。鼠海豚的主力朝入侵者们猛扑过去，却是徒劳；它们的英勇毫无结果。它们被敌人抡起的沉沉的大道理压垮了，转身溃逃，得胜的大军开进了人鱼国。

===Original===
Francis was the first to recover consciousness. The Porpoise to which he had clung was fanning him with its fin, and imploring him, for its sake, to look up, to speak.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯第一个恢复知觉。他抱住的那只鼠海豚正用鳍给他扇风，看在它自己的份上，恳求他抬头，恳求他说话。

===Original===
“All right, old chap,” said Francis. “I must have fallen asleep. Where are the others?”

===Chinese===
“好了，老伙计，”弗朗西斯说，“我准是睡着了。其他人呢？”

===Original===
They were all there, and the devoted Porpoises quickly restored them to consciousness.

===Chinese===
他们全都在，忠心耿耿的鼠海豚们很快把他们一个个都弄醒了。

===Original===
The four children stood up and looked at each other.

===Chinese===
四个孩子站起身，面面相觑。

===Original===
“I wish Reuben was here,” said Cathay. “He’d know what to do.”

===Chinese===
“鲁本要是在这儿就好了，”凯西说，“他准知道该怎么办。”

===Original===
“He wouldn’t know any more than we do,” said Francis haughtily.

===Chinese===
“他也未必比我们知道得多。”弗朗西斯傲里傲气地说。

===Original===
“We *must* do *something*,” said Mavis. “It’s our fault again.”

===Chinese===
“我们*必须*做*点什么*，”梅维斯说，“又是我们的错。”

===Original===
“It’s mine,” said Cathay, “but I couldn’t help it.”

===Chinese===
“是我的错，”凯西说，“可我实在没法子呀。”

===Original===
“If you hadn’t, one of us would have,” said Bernard, seeking to console. “I say, why do only the nasty people come out of the books?”

===Chinese===
“你不出这个错，我们里头总有一个会出的。”伯纳德说，想安慰安慰她，“我说，为什么从书里出来的净是讨厌的家伙？”

===Original===
“*I* know that,” said his Porpoise, turning his black face eagerly toward them. “The stupidest people can’t help knowing something. The Under Folk get in and open the books﻿—at least, they send the Bookworms in to open them. And, of course, they only open the pages where the enemies are quartered.”

===Chinese===
“这个*我*知道，”他的鼠海豚说着，急切地把它那张黑脸转向他们，“再笨的家伙也免不了知道点什么。深渊族溜进来，把书打开——至少，他们派书虫（Bookworms）进去开。当然啦，他们只打开敌人驻扎的那些书页。”

===Original===
“Then﻿—” said Bernard, looking at the golden gate, which swung open, its lock hanging broken and useless.

===Chinese===
“那么——”伯纳德说着，望向那扇金门。门荡开着，锁头破了，挂在那儿毫无用处。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mavis, “we could, couldn’t we? Open the other books, we mean!” She appealed to her Porpoise.

===Chinese===
“对呀，”梅维斯说，“我们能行的，不是吗？把别的书打开，我是说！”她向自己的鼠海豚求证。

===Original===
“Yes,” it said, “perhaps you could. Human children can open books, I believe. Porpoises can’t. And Mer-people can’t open the books in the Cave of Learning, though they can unlock them. If they want to open them they have to get them from the Public Mer Libraries. I can’t help knowing that,” it added. The Porpoises seemed really ashamed of not being thoroughly stupid.

===Chinese===
“是呀，”它说，“兴许你们能。人类的孩子会开书，我相信。鼠海豚不会。人鱼族呢，开不了学问洞里的书，尽管他们解得开锁。他们要想开书，得去人鱼公共图书馆（Public Mer Libraries）借。这个我忍不住知道了。”它补了一句。这些鼠海豚似乎真为自己不够彻底的愚蠢感到羞愧。

===Original===
“Come on,” said Francis, “we’ll raise an army to fight these Book People. Here’s something we can do that *isn’t* mischief.”

===Chinese===
“来吧，”弗朗西斯说，“我们去召一支大军来打这些书里人。这可是一件我们干得来、又*不*闯祸的事。”

===Original===
“You shut up,” said Bernard, and thumping Cathay on the back told her to never mind.

===Chinese===
“你闭嘴。”伯纳德说着，捶了捶凯西的背，叫她别往心里去。

===Original===
They went toward the golden gate.

===Chinese===
他们朝金门走去。

===Original===
“I suppose all the nasty people are out of the books by now?” Mavis asked her Porpoise, who followed her with the close fidelity of an affectionate little dog.

===Chinese===
“我猜到这会儿，讨厌的家伙们全从书里出来了吧？”梅维斯问她的鼠海豚；它紧跟在她身边，忠心得像只亲亲热热的小狗。

===Original===
“*I* don’t know,” it said, with some pride. “I’m stupid, I am. But I can’t help knowing that no one can come out of books unless they’re called. You’ve just got to tap on the back of the book and call the name and then you open it, and the person comes out. At least, that’s what the Bookworms do, and I don’t see why you should be different.”

===Chinese===
“*我*可不知道，”它带点儿骄傲地说，“我笨，我是真笨。可有件事我忍不住知道：没人叫，谁也出不了书。你只要在书背面敲一敲，喊出名字，再把书打开，那人就出来了。至少，书虫们就是这么干的，我看你们也没什么两样。”

===Original===
What *was* different, it soon appeared, was the water in the stream in the Cave of Learning, which was quite plainly still water in some other sense than that in which what they were in was water. That is, they could not walk in it; they had to swim. The cave seemed dark, but enough light came from the golden gate to enable them to read the titles of the books when they had pulled away the seaweed which covered many of them. They had to hold on to the rocks﻿—which were books﻿—with one hand, and clear away the seaweed with the other.

===Chinese===
不过*真正*两样的地方，很快就显出来了：学问洞里那条溪中的水，明明白白是另一种意义上的水，跟他们此刻泡在里头的那种“水”不是一个意思——也就是说，他们在里面走不动，只好游。洞里显得挺暗，但从金门透进来的光，足够让他们看清一本本书的标题——只要先揭掉许多书上盖着的海草。他们得一只手抓住岩石（岩石就是书），用另一只手扒开海草。

===Original===
You can guess the sort of books at which they knocked﻿—Kingsley and Shakespeare and Marryat and Dickens, Miss Alcott and Mrs. Ewing, Hans Andersen and Stevenson, and Mayne Reid﻿—and when they had knocked they called the name of the hero whose help they desired, and “Will you help us,” they asked, “to conquer the horrid Book People, and drive them back to cover?”

===Chinese===
他们敲的是些什么样的书，你们猜也猜得着——金斯利（Kingsley）和莎士比亚，马里亚特（Marryat）和狄更斯，奥尔科特小姐（Miss Alcott）和尤因太太（Mrs. Ewing），汉斯·安徒生和史蒂文森，还有梅恩·里德（Mayne Reid）——敲过之后，他们就唤出想要请其相助的那位英雄的名字，问道：“你肯帮我们打败那些可恶的书里人，把他们赶回老窝里去吗？”

===Original===
And not a hero but said, “Yes, indeed we will, with all our hearts.”

===Chinese===
而英雄们没有一个不回答：“肯，当然肯，我们全心全意地肯。”

===Original===
And they climbed down out of the books, and swam up to the golden gate and waited, talking with courage and dignity among themselves, while the children went on knocking at the backs of books﻿—which are books’ front doors﻿—and calling out more and more heroes to help in the fight.

===Chinese===
于是他们从书里爬下来，游到金门边等候，一边彼此勇敢而庄重地谈着话；孩子们呢，接着敲书的背面——那正是书的前门——唤出一位又一位英雄来助战。

===Original===
Quentin Durward and Laurie were the first to come out, then Hereward and Amyas and Will Cary, David Copperfield, Rob Roy, Ivanhoe, Caesar and Anthony, Coriolanus and Othello; but you can make the list for yourselves. They came forth, all alive and splendid, with valor and the longing to strike once more a blow for the good cause, as they had been used to do in their old lives.

===Chinese===
头两个出来的是昆廷·达沃德（Quentin Durward）和劳里（Laurie），接着是赫里沃德（Hereward）、阿米亚斯（Amyas）和威尔·凯里（Will Cary），大卫·科波菲尔（David Copperfield）、罗布·罗伊（Rob Roy）、艾凡赫（Ivanhoe），恺撒（Caesar）和安东尼，科里奥拉努斯（Coriolanus）和奥赛罗（Othello）；不过这份名单，你们自己就能开出来。他们一个个走将出来，个个鲜活，个个光彩照人，怀着勇气，渴望着像从前在各自的人生里那样，再为正义的事业挥出一击。

===Original===
“These are enough,” said Francis, at last. “We ought to leave some, in case we want more help later.”

===Chinese===
“这些够了，”弗朗西斯终于说，“该留下一些，万一往后还要人帮忙。”

===Original===
You see for yourselves what a splendid company it was that swam to the golden gate﻿—there was no other way than swimming, except for Perseus﻿—and awaited the children. And when the children joined them﻿—rather nervous at the thought of the speeches they would have to make to their newly recruited regiment﻿—they found that there was no need of speeches. The faithful Porpoises had not been too stupid to explain the simple facts of danger and rescue.

===Chinese===
游到金门边等候孩子们的是怎样一支堂堂之师，你们自己也能想见——除了珀尔修斯（Perseus），谁都只能游着去。孩子们跟他们会合时，心里还直打鼓，想着总得对这支新召来的队伍训几句词，却发现根本用不着讲话。忠心的鼠海豚们还没有笨到连危险和搭救这点简单情形都讲不明白。

===Original===
It was a proud moment for the children when they marched toward the Palace at the head of the band of heroes whom they had pressed into the service of the Merland. Between the clipped seaweed hedges they went, and along the paths paved with pearl and marble, and so, at last, drew near the Palace. They gave the watchword “Glory.”

===Chinese===
孩子们领着这支被他们强征来为人鱼国效力的英雄队伍朝王宫进发，那是他们顶风光的一刻。他们走在修剪齐整的海草树篱之间，沿着珍珠和大理石铺就的路，终于来到王宫近前。他们报上口令：“光荣。”

===Original===
“Or Death,” said the sentry. And they passed on to the Queen.

===Chinese===
“或者死亡。”哨兵应道。他们便过去见女王。

===Original===
“We’ve brought a reinforcement,” said Francis, who had learned the word from Quentin Durward as they came along. And the Queen gave one look at her reinforcement’s faces and said simply:

===Chinese===
“我们带来了一支增援。”弗朗西斯说，这个用词是来的路上跟昆廷·达沃德学的。女王朝她这支增援的面孔看了一眼，只简简单单地说：

===Original===
“We are saved.”

===Chinese===
“我们有救了。”

===Original===
The horrible Book People had not attacked the Palace; they had gone furtively through the country killing stray fish and destroying any beautiful thing they happened to find. For these people hate beauty and happiness. They were now holding a meeting in the Palace gardens, near the fountain where the Princesses had been wont to do their source-service, and they were making speeches like mad. You could hear the dull, flat murmur of them even from the Palace. They were the sort of people who love the sound of their own silly voices.

===Chinese===
可恶的书里人并没有攻打王宫；他们鬼鬼祟祟地窜遍全国，见散游的鱼就杀，撞见什么美的东西就毁什么。因为这路人仇恨美和快乐。此刻他们正在御花园里开会，就在公主们惯常行守源值役的那座泉边，一个个演讲得如疯似狂。那种沉闷平板的嗡嗡声，连王宫里都听得见。这路人就爱自己那副蠢嗓音的动静。

===Original===
The newcomers were ranged in orderly ranks before the Queen, awaiting her orders. It looked like a pageant or a fancy-dress parade. There was St. George in his armor, and Joan of Arc in hers﻿—heroes in plumed hats and laced shirts, heroes in ruffs and doublets﻿—brave gentlemen of England, gallant gentlemen of France. For all the differences in their dress, there was nothing motley about the band which stood before the Queen. Varied as they were in dress and feature, they had one quality in common, which marked them as one company. The same light of bravery shone on them all, and became them like a fine uniform.

===Chinese===
新来的英雄们在女王面前排成整整齐齐的队列，听候她的号令。那场面像一场盛典，又像一场化装游行。圣乔治（St. George）一身铠甲，贞德（Joan of Arc）也是一身铠甲——有戴羽饰帽、穿花边衬衫的英雄，有戴皱领、穿紧身衣的英雄——英格兰的勇武士，法兰西的俊儿郎。尽管装束千差万别，站在女王面前的这支队伍却丝毫不显杂乱。他们服饰各异、面貌各别，却有一种共同的气质，把他们标明为一支队伍。同样的勇敢之光在每个人身上闪耀，像一身精美的军装那样为他们增色。

===Original===
“Will you,” the Queen asked of their leader﻿—a pale, thin-faced man in the dress of a Roman﻿—“will you do just as you think best? I would not presume,” she added, with a kind of proud humility, “to teach the game of war to Caesar.”

===Chinese===
“您肯不肯，”女王问他们的统帅——一个面色苍白、脸庞瘦削、一身罗马装束的人——“凡事都照您认为最妥当的办？我不敢僭越，”她带着一种骄矜的谦逊补充道，“在恺撒面前讲授战争这门学问。”

===Original===
“Oh, Queen,” he answered, “these brave men and I will drive back the intruders, but, having driven them back, we must ourselves return through those dark doors which we passed when your young defenders called our names. We will drive back the *men*﻿—and by the look of them ’twill be an easy task. But Caesar wars not with women, and the women on our side are few, though each, I doubt not, has the heart of a lioness.”

===Chinese===
“哦，女王，”他答道，“我和这些勇士会把入侵者赶回去；不过赶回去之后，我们自己也得从你们的小卫士们唤我们名字时进来的那几道黑门回去。*男人*，我们来赶——瞧他们那副样子，这差事不难。可恺撒不与妇人交战，而我们这边的妇人不多，虽然我毫不怀疑，她们个个都有母狮的心。”

===Original===
He turned toward Joan of Arc with a smile and she gave him back a smile as bright as the sword she carried.

===Chinese===
他微笑着转向贞德，贞德回他一笑，那笑容亮得像她随身挎着的剑。

===Original===
“How many women are there among you?” the Queen asked, and Joan answered:

===Chinese===
“你们中间有几位女性？”女王问。贞德答道：

===Original===
“Queen Boadicea and Torfrida and I are but three.”

===Chinese===
“博阿迪西亚女王（Boadicea）、托弗里达（Torfrida），加上我，不过三人。”

===Original===
“But we three,” cried Torfrida, “are a match for three hundred of such women as those. Give us but whips instead of swords, and we will drive them like dogs to their red and blue cloth-bound kennels.”

===Chinese===
“可我们三个，”托弗里达喊道，“抵得上三百个那样的女人。只消给我们鞭子代替刀剑，我们就把她们像狗一样赶回她们那红红蓝蓝的布面狗窝里去。”

===Original===
“I’m afraid,” said the Queen, “they’d overcome you by sheer weight. You’ve no idea how heavy they are.” And then Kathleen covered herself with glory by saying, “Well, but what about Amazons?”

===Chinese===
“恐怕，”女王说，“她们光凭体重就能压倒你们。她们有多沉，你们想也想不到。”这时凯思琳说了一句让自己满身荣光的话：“那么——亚马孙女战士（Amazons）怎么样？”

===Original===
“The very thing,” said Caesar kindly. “Would you mind running back? You’ll find them in the third book from the corner where the large purple starfish is; you can’t mistake it.”

===Chinese===
“正是这个主意。”恺撒和颜悦色地说，“劳驾你们跑一趟？你们会在大紫海星待的那个角落往里数第三本书里找到她们；错不了的。”

===Original===
The children tore off to the golden gate, rushed through it, and swam to the spot where, unmistakably, the purplish starfish spread its violet rays. They knocked on the book, and Cathay, by previous arrangement, called out﻿—

===Chinese===
孩子们飞奔到金门，一冲而过，游到那个地点——那只发紫的海星确凿无疑地舒展着紫罗兰色的腕足。他们敲了敲那本书，凯西按事先的约定喊道——

===Original===
“Come out, please, Queen of the Amazons, and bring all your fighting ladies.”

===Chinese===
“请出来吧，亚马孙女王，把您所有能征惯战的女将都带出来。”

===Original===
Then out came a very splendid lady in glorious golden armor. “You’d better get some boats for us,” she said, standing straight and splendid on a ledge of rock, “enough to reach from here to the gate, or a bridge. There are all these things in Caesar’s books. I’m sure he wouldn’t mind your calling them out. We must not swim, I know, because of getting our bowstrings wet.”

===Chinese===
于是出来了一位气派非凡的女郎，穿一身辉煌的金铠。“你们顶好给我们弄些船来，”她说着，笔挺而神气地立在一方岩石上，“要够从这儿排到门口的，要么就来一座桥。这些东西恺撒的书里全有。我敢说，你们把它们唤出来，他准不会见怪。我们不能游水，这个我知道——弓弦可不能弄湿了。”

===Original===
So Francis called out a bridge, and when it was not long enough to reach the golden gate he called another. And then the Queen called her ladies, and out came a procession, which seemed as though it would never end, of tall and beautiful women armed and equipped for war. They carried bows, and the children noticed that one side of their chests was flatter than the other. And the procession went on and on, passing along the bridge and through the golden gate, till Cathay grew quite dizzy; and at last Mavis said, “Oh, your Majesty, do stop them. I’m sure there are heaps, and we shall be too late if we wait for any more.”

===Chinese===
于是弗朗西斯唤出一座桥，桥够不到金门那么长，他又唤了一座。随后亚马孙女王唤她的女将们，于是走出来一队望不到头的人——全是身材高挑、容貌美丽、披挂整齐、备战出征的女子。她们都带着弓，孩子们还注意到，她们一边的胸脯比另一边平。队伍走啊走啊，沿着桥、穿过金门，走得凯西头都晕了；末了梅维斯说：“哦，陛下，快叫她们停下吧。人数肯定够多了，再等下去我们就要误大事啦。”

===Original===
So the Queen stopped the procession and they went back to the Palace, where the Queen of the Amazons greeted Joan of Arc and the other ladies as though they were old acquaintances.

===Chinese===
于是女王叫队伍停了下来，大家回到王宫。亚马孙女王向贞德和其他女士们致意，仿佛她们是多年的旧相识。

===Original===
In a few moments their plans were laid. I wish I could describe to you the great fight between the Nice Book People and the others. But I have not time, and besides, the children did not see all of it, so I don’t see why *you* should. It was fought out in the Palace gardens. The armies were fairly evenly matched as to numbers, because the Bookworms had let out a great many Barbarians, and these, though not so unpleasant as Mr. Murdstone and Mrs. Fairchild, were quite bad enough. The children were not allowed to join in the battle, which they would dearly have liked to do. Only from a safe distance they heard the sound of steel on steel, the whir of arrows, and the war cries of the combatants. And presently a stream of fugitives darkened the pearly pathways, and one could see the heroes with drawn swords following in pursuit.

===Chinese===
不消几分钟，作战方略就定当了。我真希望能把书里好人和其余那些人的那场大战讲给你们听，可惜我没有工夫，再说孩子们也没有看全，所以我看不出*你们*凭什么该听全。仗是在御花园里打完的。两军的人数倒还势均力敌，因为书虫们放出来了一大群蛮族；这些蛮人虽然不像默德斯通先生和费尔柴尔德太太那么叫人恶心，也够坏的了。孩子们是不许参战的，尽管他们巴不得参加。他们只是从安全的距离外，听见了钢刀相击、箭矢破空和交战者的呐喊。不多时，一股溃兵的浊流把珍珠铺的小路都遮暗了，只见英雄们拔剑随后追杀。

===Original===
And then, among those who were left, the shouts of war turned suddenly to shouts of laughter, and the Merlish Queen herself moved toward the battlefield. And as she drew near she, too, laughed. For, it would seem, the Amazons had only shot their arrows at the men among their foes﻿—they had disdained to shoot the women, and so good was their aim that not a single woman was wounded. Only, when the Book Hatefuls had been driven back by the Book Heroes, the Book Heroines advanced and, without more ado, fell on the remaining foes. They did not fight them with swords or spears or arrows or the short, sharp knives they wore﻿—they simply picked up the screaming Bookwomen and carried them back to the books where they belonged. Each Amazon caught up one of the foe and, disregarding her screaming and scratching, carried her back to the book where she belonged, pushed her in, and shut the door.

===Chinese===
随后，在留下来的人中间，战争的呐喊忽然变成了大笑，人鱼女王亲自朝战场走去。走近了，她也笑了。原来，亚马孙女战士们只朝敌军中的男人放箭——她们不屑朝女人放箭——而她们的箭法又那么准，竟没有一个女人受了伤。只是，当书里可恶人被书里英雄们打退之后，书里的女英雄们便上前去，二话不说，扑向剩下的敌人。她们不用刀剑长矛，不用弓箭，也不用她们随身那短短的利刃跟她们交手——干脆就把尖叫着的书里婆娘们一个个抱起来，送回她们各自所属的书里去。每个亚马孙女战士抓起一个对头，任凭她又叫又抓，把她抱回她那本书，往里一塞，随手关上了门。

===Original===
Boadicea carried Mrs. Markham and her brown silk under one bare, braceleted arm as though she had been a naughty child. Joan of Arc made herself responsible for Aunt Fortune, and the Queen of the Amazons made nothing of picking up Miss Murdstone, beads and all, and carrying her in her arms like a baby. Torfrida’s was the hardest task. She had, from the beginning, singled out Alftruda, her old and bitter enemy, and the fight between them was a fierce one, though it was but a battle of looks. Yet before long the fire in Torfrida’s great dark eyes seemed to scorch her adversary, she shrank before it, and shrank and shrank till at last she turned and crept back to her book and went in of her own accord, and Torfrida shut the door.

===Chinese===
博阿迪西亚把马卡姆太太连同她的棕色绸衣裳，夹在一只裸露的、戴着臂镯的胳膊底下，像夹一个淘气的孩子。福琼姨妈归了贞德照管；亚马孙女王满不当回事地拎起默德斯通小姐，连人带珠子，像抱婴儿似的抱在怀里。托弗里达的差事最难。她从一开始就单挑了阿尔夫特鲁达（Alftruda）——她宿怨深重的老对头——两人之间的这一仗打得着实凶，虽然不过是一场眼色的交锋。可不消多久，托弗里达那双又大又黑的眼睛里的火，似乎灼得她的对头痛苦难当；她在那火前缩了又缩，缩了又缩，末了终于转过身，爬回自己那本书里，自己走了进去，托弗里达关上了门。

===Original===
“But,” said Mavis, who had followed her, “don’t you live in the same book?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”一直跟着她的梅维斯说，“你们不是住在同一本书里吗？”

===Original===
Torfrida smiled.

===Chinese===
托弗里达微微一笑。

===Original===
“Not quite,” she said. “That would be impossible. I live in a different edition, where only the Nice People are alive. In hers it is the nasty ones.”

===Chinese===
“不全是，”她说，“那是不可能的。我住在另一个版本里，那里面只有好人活着。她那本里活着的，是坏家伙们。”

===Original===
“And where is Hereward?” Cathay asked, before Mavis could stop her. “I do love him, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
“那赫里沃德在哪儿？”梅维斯还没来得及拦住，凯西就问了，“我真爱他，你呢？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Torfrida, “I love him. But he is not alive in the book where I live. But he will be﻿—he will be.”

===Chinese===
“爱，”托弗里达说，“我爱他。可在我住的那本书里，他没有活着。但他会活过来的——他会的。”

===Original===
And smiling and sighing, she opened her book and went into it, and the children went slowly back to the Palace. The fight was over, the Book People had gone back into their books, and it was almost as though they had never left them﻿—not quite, for the children had seen the faces of the heroes, and the books where these lived could never again now be the same to them. All books, indeed, would now have an interest far above any they had ever held before﻿—for any of these people might be found in any book. You never know.

===Chinese===
她微笑着、叹息着，翻开自己的书走了进去；孩子们慢慢走回王宫。战斗结束了，书里人回到他们的书里去了，那情形差不多就像他们从不曾离开过——只是不尽然，因为孩子们已经见过那些英雄的面容，从此以后，英雄们所住的那些书，在他们眼里再也不是原先那些书了。说真的，从今往后，所有的书在他们眼里都会生出一种前所未有的大趣味——因为这些人里的随便哪一个，都可能藏在随便哪本书里。你永远说不准。

===Original===
The Princess Freia met them in the Palace courtyard, and clasped their hands and called them the preservers of the country, which was extremely pleasant. She also told them that a slight skirmish had been fought on the Mussel-beds south of the city, and the foe had retreated.

===Chinese===
弗蕾亚公主在王宫院子里迎住他们，握住他们的手，称他们为国家的拯救者——这话听着实在叫人愉快。她还告诉他们，城南的贻贝滩上打过一场小小的前哨战，敌人退走了。

===Original===
“But Reuben tells me,” she added﻿—“that boy is really worth his weight in pearls﻿—that the main body are to attack at midnight. We must sleep now, to be ready for the call of duty when it comes. Sure you understand your duties? And the power of your buttons and your antidotes? I might not have time to remind you later. You can sleep in the armory﻿—you must be awfully tired. You’ll be asleep before you can say Jack Sprat.”

===Chinese===
“不过鲁本告诉我，”她又补充道，“那孩子真是身价抵得上和他等重的珍珠——敌军主力定在午夜进攻。我们现在得睡了，好在职责的召唤到来时准备停当。你们清楚自己的职责了吧？还有你们那些纽扣和解药的神通？回头我未必来得及提醒你们。你们就睡在军械库里吧——你们一定累坏了。保准你们还没来得及说完‘杰克·斯普拉特’（Jack Sprat）就睡着了。”

===Original===
So they lay down on the seaweed, heaped along one end of the Oysters’ armory, and were instantly asleep.

===Chinese===
于是他们在海草上躺下——海草在牡蛎军械库的一头堆得厚厚的——转眼就睡着了。

===Original===
It may have been their natures, or it may have been the influence of the magic coats. But whatever the cause, it is certain that they lay down without fear, slept without dreams, and awoke without alarm when an Oyster corporal touched their arms and whispered, “Now!”

===Chinese===
也许是他们天生的性子，也许是那几件魔法甲衣的影响。但不管缘故是什么，可以肯定的是：他们无所畏惧地躺下，无梦无扰地睡着，又毫不惊慌地醒来——那时一只牡蛎下士碰了碰他们的胳膊，悄声说：“时候到了！”

===Original===
They were wide awake on the instant and started up, picking their oyster shields from the ground beside them.

===Chinese===
他们霎时全醒了，一跃而起，从身边地上捡起各自的牡蛎盾。

===Original===
“I feel just like a Roman soldier,” Cathay said. “Don’t you?”

===Chinese===
“我觉得自己活像个罗马士兵，”凯西说，“你们呢？”

===Original===
And the others owned that so far as they knew the feelings of a Roman soldier, those feelings were their own.

===Chinese===
其他人也承认，凡他们所能知道的罗马士兵的心情，那份心情正是他们自己的。

===Original===
The shadows of the guardroom were changed and shifted and flung here and there by the torches carried by the busy Oysters. Phosphorescent fish these torches were, and gave out a moony light like that of the pillars in the Cave of Learning. Outside the Lobster-guarded arch the water showed darkly clear. Large phosphorescent fish were twined round pillars of stone, rather like the fish you see on the lampposts on the Thames Embankment, only in this case the fish were the lamps. So strong was the illumination that you could see as clearly as you can on a moonlit night on the downs, where there are no trees to steal the light from the landscape and bury it in their thick branches.

===Chinese===
卫队室里的影子，被忙碌的牡蛎们手中高举的火把改变着、挪移着、甩得到处都是。那些火把是些发光鱼，吐出月亮似的清辉，就跟学问洞里那些柱子的光一样。龙虾把守的拱门之外，水色幽暗而清亮。大条的发光鱼盘绕在石头柱子上，颇像你们在泰晤士河堤的灯柱上见过的那些鱼，只不过在这儿，鱼本身就是灯。光照那么强，看起东西来清清楚楚，就像你们在月夜里站在丘陵草场上那样——那儿没有树把亮光从风景里偷走、埋进它们浓密的枝桠里去。

===Original===
All was hurry and bustle. The Salmoners had sent a detachment to harass the flank of the enemy, and the Sea Urchins, under the command of Reuben, were ready in their seaweed disguises.

===Chinese===
到处是匆匆忙忙、来来往往。鲑赈员们派了一支分队去袭扰敌军的侧翼；海胆们在鲁本的指挥下，已经披着海草的伪装待命。

===Original===
There was a waiting time, and the children used it to practice with their shells, using the thick stems of seaweed﻿—thick as a man’s arm﻿—to represent the ankles of the invading force, and they were soon fairly expert at the trick which was their duty. Francis had just nipped an extra fat stalk and released it again by touching the secret spring when the word went around, “Every man to his post!”

===Chinese===
有一段等候的时光，孩子们便拿它来练贝壳：用海草粗壮的茎——粗得像人的胳膊——冒充入侵大军的脚踝。不多时，他们对自己职责所在的这套本领已经相当在行。弗朗西斯刚夹住一根格外肥的茎、又按动暗簧把它松开，口令就传开了：“各就各位！”

===Original===
The children proudly took up their post next to the Princess, and hardly had they done so when a faint yet growing sound knocked gently at their ears. It grew and grew and grew till it seemed to shake the ground on which they stood, and the Princess murmured, “It is the tramp of the army of the Under Folk. Now, be ready. We shall lurk among these rocks. Hold your good oyster shell in readiness, and when you see a foot near you clip it, and at the same time set down the base of the shell on the rock. The trusty shell will do the rest.”

===Chinese===
孩子们骄傲地在公主身旁各就各位，刚一站定，一阵隐隐约约、渐渐增大的声响便轻轻叩响他们的耳朵。那声音一阵大似一阵，一阵大似一阵，直到仿佛震得他们脚下的地面都在晃。公主低声说：“是深渊族大军的脚步声。好了，准备。我们就埋伏在这些岩石中间。握好你们的好牡蛎壳，一看见有脚挨近，就夹住它，同时把贝壳的另一头搭在岩石上。剩下的，信得过的贝壳自会办妥。”

===Original===
“Yes, we know, thank you, dear Princess,” said Mavis. “Didn’t you see us practicing?”

===Chinese===
“知道了，谢谢你，亲爱的公主，”梅维斯说，“你没看见我们刚才在练吗？”

===Original===
But the Princess was not listening; she had enough to do to find cover for her troops among the limpet-studded rocks.

===Chinese===
可公主没在听；她忙着在遍布帽贝的岩石间给她的队伍找掩体，已经够她忙的了。

===Original===
And now the tramp, tramp, tramp of the great army sounded nearer and more near, and through the dimly lighted water the children could see the great Deep Sea People advancing.

===Chinese===
这时，那支大军的脚步声——咚，咚，咚——越响越近，透过幽幽发亮的水，孩子们看得见伟大的深渊族开过来了。

===Original===
Very terrible they were, big beyond man-size, more stalwart and more finely knit than the Forlorn Hopers who had led the attack so happily and gloriously frustrated by the Crabs, the Narwhals and the Sea Urchins. As the advance guard drew near all the children stared, from their places of concealment, at the faces of these terrible foes of the happy Merland. Very strong the faces were, and, surprisingly, very, very sad. They looked﻿—Francis at least was able to see it﻿—like strong folk suffering proudly an almost intolerable injury﻿—bearing, bravely, an almost intolerable pain.

===Chinese===
他们非常可怕，身量超过人的尺寸，比那些敢死队员（the Forlorn Hopers）更魁伟、更筋骨结实——这批敢死队员打头阵打得正欢，却叫螃蟹、独角鲸和海胆们欢天喜地地挫败了。先头部队逼近时，孩子们都从各自的藏身之处，盯着这些快活人鱼国的可怕仇敌的脸。那些脸非常刚强，而且出人意料地、非常非常悲哀。他们看上去——至少弗朗西斯看得出来——像是一些刚强的人，骄傲地忍受着一场几乎无法忍受的屈辱——英勇地承受着一种几乎无法忍受的疼痛。

===Original===
“But I’m on the other side,” he told himself, to check a sudden rising in his heart of﻿—well, if it was not sympathy, what was it?

===Chinese===
“可我是站在另一边的。”他对自己说，好按住心头猛然涌起的那个——嗯，那要不是同情，又能是什么呢？

===Original===
And now the head of the advancing column was level with the Princess. True to the old tradition which bids a commander lead and not to follow his troops, she was the first to dart out and fix a shell to the heel of the left-rank man. The children were next. Their practice bore its fruit. There was no blunder, no mistake. Each oyster shell clipped sharp and clean the attached ankle of an enemy; each oyster shell at the same moment attached itself firmly to the rock, thus clinging to his base in the most thorough and military way. A spring of joy and triumph welled up in the children’s hearts. How easy it was to get the better of these foolish Deep Sea Folk. A faint, kindly contempt floated into the children’s minds for the Mer-people, who so dreaded and hated these stupid giants. Why, there were fifty or sixty of them tied by the leg already! It was as easy as﻿—

===Chinese===
这时，开进纵队的队首已经跟公主齐平。恪守着指挥官应当身先士卒、而非尾随其后的老传统，她第一个窜出去，把一只贝壳夹上了左列一个兵的脚后跟。孩子们紧随其后。他们的练习结出了果实。没有一次失手，没有一处差错。每只牡蛎壳都干脆利落地夹住了敌人挨上来的那只脚踝；每只牡蛎壳同时又牢牢附在岩石上，以最彻底、最军纪严明的做派扣住了敌人的根基。一股欢乐与得胜的春泉从孩子们心底涌了上来。原来制住这些愚蠢的深渊族这么容易啊。对人鱼族——那么惧怕、那么憎恶这些蠢巨人的人鱼族——孩子们心里不禁浮起一丝善意的轻蔑。瞧呀，已经有五六十个被拴住腿啦！这简直容易得就像——

===Original===
The pleasant nature of these reflections had kept our four rooted to the spot. In the triumphant performance of one duty they failed to remember the duty that should have followed. They stood there rejoicing in their victory, when by all the rules of the Service they should have rushed back to the armory for fresh weapons.

===Chinese===
这些念头个个惬意，害得我们四个在原地生了根。他们得胜似的尽完了一桩职责，却忘了紧跟着该尽的那一桩。他们站在那儿为胜利扬扬得意，却不知按照勤务条例的全部规矩，他们本该奔回军械库去取新的兵器。

===Original===
The omission was fatal. Even as they stood there rejoicing in their cleverness and boldness and in the helpless anger of the enemy, something thin and string-like spread itself around them﻿—their feet caught in string, their fingers caught in string, string tweaked their ears and flattened their noses﻿—string confined their elbows and confused their legs. The Lobster-guarded doorway seemed farther off﻿—and farther, and farther.﻿ ﻿… They turned their heads; they were following backward, and against their will, a retreating enemy.

===Chinese===
这一疏忽是致命的。就在他们站在那儿，为自己的机灵大胆、为敌人那无可奈何的狂怒而得意的时候，一样细细的、绳子似的东西在他们四周张开了——脚被绳缠住，手指被绳缠住，绳子扯他们的耳朵、压扁他们的鼻子——绳子捆住他们的胳膊肘，搅乱他们的两腿。龙虾把守的那道门显得远了——更远了，又更远了…… 他们回过头去；他们正倒退着、身不由己地，跟随着一支后撤的敌军。

===Original===
“Oh, why didn’t we do what she said?” breathed Cathay. “Something’s happened!”

===Chinese===
“哦，我们怎么不听她的话呢？”凯西喘着气说，“出事了！”

===Original===
“I should think it had,” said Bernard. “We’re caught﻿—in a net.”

===Chinese===
“可不，真出事了。”伯纳德说，“我们被逮住了——落进网里了。”

===Original===
They were. And a tall Infantryman of the Under Folk was towing them away from Merland as swiftly and as easily as a running child tows a captive air balloon.

===Chinese===
可不是嘛。一个高大的深渊族步兵正拖着他们离开人鱼国，又快又轻巧，就像一个奔跑的孩子拖着他俘获的氢气球。

## X. The Under Folk / 十、深渊族

===Original===
Those of us who have had the misfortune to be caught in a net in the execution of our military duty, and to be dragged away by the enemy with all the helpless buoyancy of captive balloons, will be able to appreciate the sensations of the four children to whom this gloomy catastrophe had occurred.

===Chinese===
我们当中凡是有过这等不幸的人——执行军务时一头撞进网里，又让敌人拖着走，浑身带着被俘气球那种身不由己的浮劲儿——准能体会这四个孩子的感受：这场阴惨的大祸，偏偏落在了他们头上。

===Original===
The net was very strong﻿—made of twisted fibrous filaments of seaweed. All efforts to break it were vain, and they had, unfortunately, nothing to cut it with. They had not even their oyster shells, the rough edges of which might have done something to help, or at least would have been useful weapons, and the discomfort of their position was extreme. They were, as Cathay put it, “all mixed up with each other’s arms and legs,” and it was very difficult and painful to sort themselves out without hurting each other.

===Chinese===
那张网非常结实——是用搓扭的海草纤维丝编的。想挣破它全是白费力气，不幸的是，他们手头又没有一件能割网的东西。他们连牡蛎壳都没带在身上，那壳上粗粝的边缘本可以帮上点忙，至少也能当件顶用的兵器。他们眼下的处境难受到了极点。用凯西的话说，他们是"胳膊腿儿全缠作了一团"，要想不弄疼彼此就把各自择出来，实在又难又痛。

===Original===
“Let’s do it, one at a time,” said Mavis, after some minutes of severe and unsuccessful struggle. “France first. Get right away, France, and see if you can’t sit down on a piece of the net that isn’t covered with *us*, and then Cathay can try.”

===Chinese===
"咱们一个一个来。"梅维斯说。他们已经狠狠挣扎了好几分钟，一无所获。"弗兰先来。使劲挪远些，弗兰，看看你能不能坐到一块没被*咱们*压着的网面上，然后凯西再试。"

===Original===
It was excellent advice and when all four had followed it, it was found possible to sit side by side on what may be called the floor of the net, only the squeezing of the net walls tended to jerk one up from one’s place if one wasn’t very careful.

===Chinese===
这主意好极了。等四个人都照办之后，果然可以并排坐在那张网堪称"网底"的地方了，只是两侧的网壁不住地往里挤，稍一不留神，就会把人从座位上颠起来。

===Original===
By the time the rearrangement was complete, and they were free to look about them, the whole aspect of the world had changed. The world, for one thing, was much darker, in itself that is, though the part of it where the children were was much lighter than had been the sea where they were first netted. It was a curious scene﻿—rather like looking down on London at night from the top of St. Paul’s. Some bright things, like trams or omnibuses, were rushing along, and smaller lights, which looked mighty like cabs and carriages, dotted the expanse of blackness till, where they were thick set, the darkness disappeared in a blaze of silvery light.

===Chinese===
等这一切重新安顿停当、他们总算能四下打量时，整个世界的面貌已经变了。首先，这世界本身暗多了——虽说孩子们如今所在的这一角，比他们最初落网的那片海亮堂得多。这是一幅奇景——很像从圣保罗大教堂（St. Paul's）顶上俯瞰夜里的伦敦。一些发亮的东西，像电车啦、公共马车啦，匆匆来去；更小的光点，瞧着活脱是些出租马车和四轮马车，星星点点洒满黑沉沉的一大片，它们密的地方，黑暗便消融在一片银辉里。

===Original===
Other light-bearers had rows of round lights like the portholes of great liners. One came sweeping toward them, and a wild idea came to Cathay that perhaps when ships sink they go on living and moving underwater just as she and the others had done. Perhaps they do. Anyhow, this was not one of them, for, as it came close, it was plainly to be perceived as a vast fish with phosphorescent lights in rows along its gigantic sides. It opened its jaws as it passed, and for an instant everyone shut their eyes and felt that all was over. When the eyes were opened again, the mighty fish was far away. Cathay, however, was discovered to be in tears.

===Chinese===
另一些带光的家伙，身上排着一溜溜圆圆的光，像大邮轮的舷窗。有一头朝他们直扫过来，凯西心里冒出一个疯狂的念头：莫非船沉了以后，也跟她和大伙儿一样，在水底下照旧活着、照旧走动？也许真是这样吧。反正这一位不是什么船，因为等它凑近了，明明白白看得出那是一条巨鱼，庞大的侧腹上排着一列列磷光。它擦身而过时张开了大嘴，一时间人人都闭上眼睛，觉得这回全完了。等再睁开眼，那条巨鱼已经去远了。大家却发现，凯西哭了起来。

===Original===
“I wish we hadn’t come,” she said; and the others could not but feel that there was something in what she said. They comforted her and themselves as best they could by expressing a curious half-certainty which they had that everything would be all right in the end. As I said before, there are some things so horrible that if you can bring yourself to face them you see at once that they can’t be true. The barest idea of poetic justice﻿—which we all believe in at the bottom of our hearts﻿—made it impossible to think that the children who had nobly (they couldn’t help feeling it *was* noble) defended their friends, the Mer Folk, should have anything really dreadful happen to them in consequence. And when Bernard talked about the fortunes of war he did it in an unconvinced sort of way and Francis told him to shut up.

===Chinese===
"我真后悔来了。"她说。其余几个也不由得觉得，她这话有几分道理。他们只好竭力安慰她、也安慰自己，把心里那种说不清道不明的半吊子把握说出口来：到头来一切总会好的。我从前就说过，世上有些事情可怕到这般地步，只要你逼着自己去正面看它一眼，立刻就会看出它不可能是真的。单凭"善有善报"这最起码的公道观念——它在我们每个人心底里都信着——就不可能想象：这几个孩子如此高尚地（他们不由得不觉得那*就是*高尚）卫护了他们的朋友人鱼族，反倒要因此遭上什么真正可怕的大难。伯纳德说起"战争的胜负无常"时，语气里连自己也不大信，弗朗西斯索性叫他闭嘴。

===Original===
“But what are we to do,” sniffed Cathay for the twentieth time, and all the while the Infantryman was going steadily on, dragging the wretched netful after him.

===Chinese===
"可咱们到底怎么办呢？"凯西抽抽搭搭地问——这已经是第二十遍了。而那名步兵照旧不紧不慢地一路前进，身后拖着满满一网的倒霉蛋。

===Original===
“Press our pearl buttons,” suggested Francis hopefully. “Then we shall be invisible and unfeelable and we can escape.” He fumbled with the round marble-like pearl.

===Chinese===
"按咱们的珍珠纽扣呀。"弗朗西斯满怀希望地出主意，"那样咱们就隐了身，又叫人摸不着、碰不上，就能逃出去了。"他摸弄着那颗弹珠似的圆珍珠。

===Original===
“No, no,” said Bernard, catching at his hand, “don’t you see? If we do, we may never get out of the net. If they can’t see us or feel us they’ll think the net’s empty, and perhaps hang it up on a hook or put it away in a box.”

===Chinese===
"不，不行。"伯纳德一把抓住他的手，"你还不明白？咱们要是隐了身，只怕就永远出不了这张网了。他们瞧不见咱们、摸不着咱们，只当网是空的，说不定就把它往钩子上一挂，或者收进哪口箱子里去。"

===Original===
“And forget it while years roll by. *I* see,” said Cathay.

===Chinese===
"然后一忘就是一年又一年。*我*明白了。"凯西说。

===Original===
“But we can undo them the minute we’re there. Can’t we?” said Mavis.

===Chinese===
"可咱们一到那儿，马上就能把纽扣解开呀。不是吗？"梅维斯说。

===Original===
“Yes, of course,” said Bernard; but as a matter of fact they couldn’t.

===Chinese===
"对，那当然。"伯纳德说。可事实上，他们解不开。

===Original===
At last the Infantryman, after threading his way through streets of enormous rocky palaces, passed through a colossal arch, and so into a hall as big as St. Paul’s and Westminster Abbey into one.

===Chinese===
那步兵终于穿过一条条岩石巨宫夹成的街巷，钻过一道庞然拱门，进了一座大厅——把圣保罗大教堂和威斯敏斯特教堂（Westminster Abbey）合起来拼成一座，才抵得上它的大小。

===Original===
A crowd of Under Folk, who were seated on stone benches around rude tables, eating strange luminous food, rose up, and cried, “What news?”

===Chinese===
一群深渊族围坐在石凳上，守着粗陋的桌子吃些发着亮光的怪食物，这时纷纷起身喊道："有什么新闻？"

===Original===
“Four prisoners,” said the Infantryman.

===Chinese===
"四个俘虏。"步兵说。

===Original===
“Upper Folk,” the Colonel said; “and my orders are to deliver them to the Queen herself.”

===Chinese===
"上界族。"上校说，"我奉的命令，是要把他们亲自呈交女王本人。"

===Original===
He passed to the end of the hall and up a long wide flight of steps made of something so green and clear that it was plainly either glass or emerald, and I don’t think it could have been glass, because how could they have made glass in the sea? There were lights below it which shone through the green transparency so clear and lovely that Francis said dreamily﻿—

===Chinese===
他走到大厅尽头，登上一道又长又宽的台阶。台阶用一种又绿又透的东西砌成，明摆着不是玻璃就是绿宝石；我看不会是玻璃，因为他们在大海里哪能造得出玻璃来？台阶底下有灯光，透过那片绿莹莹的透明体照上来，又清亮又可爱，弗朗西斯恍恍惚惚地念道——

===Original===
> “ ‘Sabrina fair,
 

 Listen where thou art sitting,
 

 Under the glassie, cool, translucent wave﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
>"'美丽的萨布丽娜，
 

 请听，就在你端坐之处，
 

 在玻璃般清凉澄澈的水波底下——'"

===Original===
and quite suddenly there was much less room in the net, and they were being embraced all at once and with tears of relief and joy by the Princess Freia﻿—their own Mer Princess.

===Chinese===
话音未落，网里忽然局促起来，他们猛地被一齐搂住了——是弗蕾亚公主（Princess Freia），他们自己的人鱼公主，噙着宽慰与欢喜的泪水，把他们统统抱了个满怀。

===Original===
“Oh, I *didn’t* mean to﻿—Princess dear, I *didn’t*,” said Francis. “It was the emerald steps made me think of translucent.”

===Chinese===
"哦，我不是故意的——亲爱的公主，我*不是*故意的。"弗朗西斯说，"是那绿宝石台阶害我想起了'澄澈'两个字。"

===Original===
“So they are,” she said, “but oh, if you knew what I’ve felt﻿—you, our guests, our knights-errant, our noble defenders﻿—to be prisoners and all of us safe. I did so hope you’d call me. And I’m so proud that you didn’t﻿—that you were brave enough not to call for me until you did it by accident.”

===Chinese===
"可不是嘛。"她说，"可是唉，你们哪里知道我心里是什么滋味——你们，我们的贵客，我们的游侠骑士，我们高尚的卫护者——竟成了俘虏，而我们大家倒个个平安。我一心盼着你们召唤我；可你们竟然没有——你们竟有那么大的勇气，硬是忍着不叫我来，直到无意之间才把那句话念了出来——这又让我多么骄傲。"

===Original===
“We never thought of doing it,” said Mavis candidly, “but I hope we shouldn’t have, if we *had* thought of it.”

===Chinese===
"我们压根儿没想到要那么做。"梅维斯坦白说，"不过就算*想到*了，我也希望我们不至于真那么干。"

===Original===
“Why haven’t you pressed your pearl buttons?” she asked, and they told her why.

===Chinese===
"你们怎么不按珍珠纽扣？"她问。他们把缘故讲给她听。

===Original===
“Wise children,” she said, “but at any rate we must all use the charm that prevents our losing our memories.”

===Chinese===
"聪明的孩子。"她说，"不过不管怎样，咱们都得把防失忆的解药用上。"

===Original===
“I shan’t use mine,” said Cathay. “I don’t want to remember. If I didn’t remember I should forget to be frightened. Do please let me forget to remember.” She clung pleadingly to the Princess, who whispered to Mavis, “Perhaps it would be best,” and they let Cathay have her way.

===Chinese===
"我不用我的。"凯西说，"我不想记住。我要是不记得了，就会忘了害怕。求求你们，就让我把'要记住'这回事忘掉吧。"她缠着公主苦苦央求，公主悄悄对梅维斯说："也许这样最好。"于是他们依了凯西。

===Original===
The others had only just time to swallow their charms before the Infantryman threw the net onto a great table, which seemed to be cut out of one vast diamond, and fell on his face on the ground. It was his way of saluting his sovereign.

===Chinese===
其余几个刚刚来得及吞下各自的解药，那步兵就把网甩上一张大桌——那桌子像是一整块巨大的钻石雕出来的——随即扑倒在地。脸贴地扑倒，是他向君主行礼的方式。

===Original===
“Prisoners, your Majesty,” he said when he had got up again. “Four of the young of the Upper Folk﻿—” and he turned to the net as he spoke, and stopped short﻿—“there’s someone else,” he said in an altered voice, “someone as wasn’t there when we started, I’ll swear.”

===Chinese===
"陛下，俘虏押到。"他爬起来后说，"四名上界族的小孩儿——"他一边说一边转向网，猛地顿住了——"这里头多出一位，"他换了副声调说，"出发的时候明明没有这位，我可以起誓。"

===Original===
“Open the net,” said a strong, sweet voice, “and bid the prisoners stand up that I may look upon them.”

===Chinese===
"把网打开。"一个有力而甜美的声音说，"叫俘虏们站起来，好让我看看他们。"

===Original===
“They might escape, my love,” said another voice anxiously, “or perhaps they bite.”

===Chinese===
"他们兴许会逃走，亲爱的。"另一个声音焦急地说，"没准儿还会咬人呢。"

===Original===
“Submersia,” said the first voice, “do you and four of my women stand ready. Take the prisoners one by one. Seize each a prisoner and hold them, awaiting my royal pleasure.”

===Chinese===
"萨布默西娅（Submersia），"头一个声音说，"你带上我的四名女官，各就各位，把俘虏一个一个提出来。你们一人擒住一个，牢牢握定，听候我的旨意发落。"

===Original===
The net was opened and large and strong hands took Bernard, who was nearest the mouth of the net back, and held him gently but with extreme firmness in an upright position on the table. None of them could stand because of their tails.

===Chinese===
网打开了，几只又大又有力的手抓住了伯纳德——他离网口最近——把他轻轻却又极其牢固地扶直了，立在桌上。他们几个谁也没法自己站直，因为都拖着鱼尾。

===Original===
They saw before them, on a throne, a tall and splendid Queen, very beautiful and very sad, and by her side a King (they knew the royalty by their crowns), not so handsome as his wife, but still very different from the uncouth, heavy Under Folk. And he looked sad too. They were clad in robes of richest woven seaweed, sewn with jewels, and their crowns were like dreams of magnificence. Their throne was of one clear blood-bright ruby, and its canopy of green drooping seaweed was gemmed with topazes and amethysts. The Queen rose and came down the steps of the throne and whispered to her whom she had called Submersia, and she in turn whispered to the four other large ladies who held, each, a captive.

===Chinese===
他们看见面前宝座上端坐着一位修长华贵的女王，美丽极了，也忧愁极了；她身旁是一位国王（他们是凭王冠认出这一对王者的），不及妻子俊美，却仍然跟那些粗野笨重的深渊族大不相同。他也一脸愁容。夫妻俩身披织得最华丽的海草袍，缀满宝石，头上的王冠华贵得如同一场梦境。宝座是一整块清透、血一般鲜红的红宝石，座顶垂下的绿海草华盖上嵌着黄玉和紫水晶。女王起身走下宝座的台阶，对她唤作萨布默西娅的那位低语了几句，那位又转身向其余四位高大的女官低声传话——她们一人擒着一个俘虏。

===Original===
And with a dreadful unanimity the five acted; with one dexterous movement they took off the magic jackets, and with another they removed the useful tails. The Princess and the four children stood upon the table on their own ten feet.

===Chinese===
接着，五个人以吓人的一致齐齐动手：一个利落的手势，剥下那几件魔法珠甲；又一个手势，褪下那几条顶用的鱼尾。公主和四个孩子凭自己那十只脚，站上了桌面。

===Original===
“What funny little things,” said the King, not unkindly.

===Chinese===
"多有趣的小东西。"国王说，口气并不刻薄。

===Original===
“Hush,” said the Queen, “perhaps they can understand what you say﻿—and at any rate that Mer-girl can.”

===Chinese===
"嘘。"女王说，"没准儿他们听得懂你的话——反正那条人鱼姑娘一定听得懂。"

===Original===
The children were furious to hear their Princess so disrespectfully spoken of. But she herself remained beautifully calm.

===Chinese===
听见他们的公主被人这样不敬地议论，孩子们个个气得要命。公主本人却雍容地镇静如常。

===Original===
“Now,” said the Queen, “before we destroy your memories, will you answer questions?”

===Chinese===
"那么，"女王说，"在抹掉你们的记忆之前，肯不肯回答几个问题？"

===Original===
“Some questions, yes﻿—others, no,” said the Princess.

===Chinese===
"有的问题肯——有的不肯。"公主说。

===Original===
“Are these human children?”

===Chinese===
"这些是人类的孩子吗？"

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
"是。"

===Original===
“How do they come under the sea?”

===Chinese===
"他们怎么到海底下来的？"

===Original===
“Mer-magic. You wouldn’t understand,” said the Princess haughtily.

===Chinese===
"人鱼的魔法。说了你们也不懂。"公主傲然道。

===Original===
“Were they fighting against us?”

===Chinese===
"他们同我们作过战吗？"

===Original===
“Yes,” cried Bernard and Mavis before the Princess answered.

===Chinese===
"作过！"不等公主开口，伯纳德和梅维斯就喊了出来。

===Original===
“And lucky to do it,” Francis added.

===Chinese===
"而且能打这一仗还是我们的运气。"弗朗西斯补了一句。

===Original===
“If you will tell us the fighting strength of the Merlanders, your tails and coats shall be restored to you and you shall go free. Will you tell?”

===Chinese===
"你们要是肯把人鱼族的兵力说出来，就把鱼尾和甲衣都还给你们，放你们自由。说不说？"

===Original===
“Is it likely?” the Princess answered. “I am a Mer-woman, and a Princess of the Royal House. Such do not betray their country.”

===Chinese===
"你觉得像吗？"公主答道，"我是个人鱼，又是王室的公主。这样的人，是不会背叛自己的国家的。"

===Original===
“No, I suppose not,” said the Queen. And she paused a moment before she said, “Administer the cup of forgetfulness.”

===Chinese===
"是啊，想来是不会。"女王说。她顿了片刻，才道："把遗忘之杯端上来。"

===Original===
The cup of forgetfulness was exceedingly pleasant. It tasted of toffee and coconuts, and pineapple ices, and plum cake, and roast chicken, with a faint underflavor of lavender, rose leaves and the very best eau de cologne.

===Chinese===
遗忘之杯的味道好极了。喝着是太妃糖和椰子的味儿，还有菠萝冰、葡萄干蛋糕、烤鸡，底子里又隐隐泛出一缕薰衣草、玫瑰花瓣和最上等古龙水的香气。

===Original===
The children had tasted cider-cup and champagne-cup at parties, and had disliked both, but oblivion-cup was delicious. It was served in a goblet of opal color, in dreamy pink and pearl﻿—and green and blue and gray﻿—and the sides of the goblet were engraved with pictures of beautiful people asleep. The goblet passed from hand to hand, and when each had drunk enough the Lord High Cupbearer, a very handsome, reserved-looking fish, laid a restraining touch on the goblet and, taking it between his fins, handed it to the next drinker. So, one by one, each took the draught. Kathleen was the last.

===Chinese===
孩子们在宴会上尝过苹果甜酒宾治和香槟宾治，两样都不喜欢，可这遗忘杯却美味无比。它盛在一只蛋白石色的高脚杯里，梦幻的粉红与珠光——还有绿、蓝和灰——杯身上刻着一个个酣睡的美丽人像。酒杯在众人手里传递，谁喝够了，御前大斟酒官——一条仪表堂皇、神情矜持的鱼——便往杯上轻轻一按止住他，再用两片鱼鳍夹住杯子，递给下一位饮者。就这样，一个接一个，人人都饮下了那一口。凯思琳是最后一个。

===Original===
The draught had no effect on four out of the five﻿—but Kathleen changed before their eyes, and though they had known that the draught of oblivion would make her forget, it was terrible to see it do its fell work.

===Chinese===
这一口下去，五个人里四个安然无恙——凯思琳却在他们眼前变了样。他们原就知道这遗忘之饮会叫她忘掉一切，可亲眼看着它施展这份毒手，仍旧可怕极了。

===Original===
Mavis had her arm protectingly around Kathleen, and the moment the draught had been swallowed Kathleen threw off that loving arm and drew herself away. It hurt like a knife. Then she looked at her brothers and sisters, and it is a very terrible thing when the eyes you love look at you as though you were a stranger.

===Chinese===
梅维斯一直用胳膊护着凯思琳；那一口刚下肚，凯思琳就甩开那条爱怜的手臂，把身子挪开了。那一甩，疼得像刀割。接着她把目光转向自己的兄弟姐妹——你挚爱的那双眼睛，竟像看陌生人似的望着你，天底下再没有比这更叫人胆寒的事了。

===Original===
Now, it had been agreed, while still the captives were in the net, that all of them should pretend that the cup of oblivion had taken effect, that they should just keep still and say nothing and look as stupid as they could. But this coldness of her dear Cathay’s was more than Mavis could bear, and no one had counted on it. So when Cathay looked at Mavis as at a stranger whom she rather disliked, and drew away from her arm, Mavis could not bear it, and cried out in heart-piercing tones, “Oh, Cathay, darling, what is it? What’s the matter?” before the Princess or the boys could stop her. And to make matters worse, both boys said in a very loud, plain whisper, “Shut up, Mavis,” and only the Princess kept enough presence of mind to go on saying nothing.

===Chinese===
原来，还在网里当俘虏的时候，他们就讲定了：人人都装出遗忘杯已经见效的样子，只消一动不动、一言不发，尽量摆出一副呆相。可是亲爱的小凯西这般冷淡，实在超出了梅维斯的承受，这是谁也没料到的。于是当凯西望着梅维斯，像望一个她相当厌恶的陌生人，又从她臂弯里挣开时，梅维斯再也忍不住，没等公主或两个男孩拦住，便发出一声撕心裂肺的呼喊："哦，凯西，亲爱的，你怎么了？出什么事了？"更糟的是，两个男孩都用又响又明白的耳语说："闭嘴，梅维斯。"只有公主还镇定自若，继续一言不发。

===Original===
Cathay turned and looked at her sister.

===Chinese===
凯西回过头来，望着姐姐。

===Original===
“Cathay, darling,” Mavis said again, and stopped, for no one could go on saying “darling” to anyone who looked at you as Cathay was looking.

===Chinese===
"凯西，亲爱的。"梅维斯又叫了一声，就打住了。任谁也没法对着一个用凯西此刻这种眼神望着你的人，再说下去一声"亲爱的"。

===Original===
She turned her eyes away as Cathay looked toward the Queen﻿—looked, and went, to lean against the royal knee as though it had been her mother’s.

===Chinese===
凯西朝女王望过去，梅维斯慌忙把自己的视线挪开——那孩子望着望着，竟走了过去，偎在那王家的膝头，仿佛那是她亲母亲的膝盖。

===Original===
“Dear little thing,” said the Queen; “see, it’s quite tame. I shall keep it for a pet. Nice little pet then!”

===Chinese===
"亲爱的小东西。"女王说，"瞧，它已经很驯服了。我要把它留下当宠物。乖乖的小宠物哟！"

===Original===
“You shan’t keep her,” cried Mavis, but again the Princess hushed her, and the Queen treated her cry with contemptuous indifference. Cathay snuggled against her new mistress.

===Chinese===
"你们不许把她留下！"梅维斯喊道。可公主又一次让她噤声，女王对这声喊叫报以不屑一顾的淡漠。凯西紧紧偎进了新主人的怀抱。

===Original===
“As for the rest of you,” said the Queen, “it is evident from your manner that the draught of oblivion has not yet taken effect on you. So it is impossible for me to make presents of you to those prominent members of the nobility, who are wanting pets, as I should otherwise have done. We will try another draught tomorrow. In the meantime﻿ ﻿… the fetters, Jailer.”

===Chinese===
"至于你们其余几个，"女王说，"看你们这副样子就明白，遗忘之饮在你们身上还没见效。这么着，我就没法把你们当礼物，送给那些正缺宠物的显赫贵族了——不然我原是要这么办的。明天再换一副药试试。眼下嘛……上镣铐，狱卒。"

===Original===
A tall sour-looking Under-man stepped forward. Hanging over his arm were scaly tails, which at first sight of the children’s hearts leaped, for they hoped they were their own. But no sooner were the tails fitted on than they knew the bitter truth.

===Chinese===
一个高个子、一脸酸相的深渊族男人走上前来。他胳膊上搭着几条鳞光闪闪的鱼尾，孩子们乍一望见，心口都猛地一跳，还满心指望那就是他们自己那几条呢。可尾巴一上身，他们就明白了那苦涩的真相。

===Original===
“Yes,” said the Queen, “they are false tails. You will not be able to take them off, and you can neither swim nor walk with them. You can, however, move along quite comfortably on the floor of the ocean. What’s the matter?” she asked the Jailer.

===Chinese===
"不错。"女王说，"这是假尾巴。你们自己脱不下来，戴着它又游不得、走不得。不过在海底的地面上蹭着走动，倒是相当自在的。怎么回事？"她问狱卒。

===Original===
“None of the tails will fit this prisoner, your Majesty,” said the Jailer.

===Chinese===
"回陛下，这些尾巴一条也扣不到这位俘虏身上。"狱卒说。

===Original===
“I am a Princess of the reigning Mer House,” said Freia, “and your false, degrading tails cannot cling to me.”

===Chinese===
"我是当朝人鱼王室的公主。"弗蕾亚说，"你们那虚假而侮辱人的尾巴，休想沾上我的身。"

===Original===
“Oh, put them all in the lockup,” said the King, “as sullen a lot of prisoners as ever I saw﻿—what?”

===Chinese===
"哦，把他们统统关进拘留所吧。"国王说，"这么一帮子闷葫芦俘虏，我生平还是头一回见——嗯？"

===Original===
The lockup was a great building, broader at the top than at the bottom, which seemed to be balanced on the sea floor, but really it was propped up at both ends with great chunks of rock. The prisoners were taken there in the net, and being dragged along in nets is so confusing, that it was not till the Jailer had left them that they discovered that the prison was really a ship﻿—an enormous ship﻿—which lay there, perfect in every detail as on the day when it first left dock. The water did not seem to have spoiled it at all. They were imprisoned in the saloon, and, worn out with the varied emotions of the day, they lay down on the comfortable red velvet cushions and went to sleep. Even Mavis felt that Kathleen had found a friend in the Queen, and was in no danger.

===Chinese===
拘留所是一座巨大的建筑，上宽下窄，瞧着像在海底上放得四平八稳，其实是靠两端几大块岩石撑住的。俘虏们装在网里被拖了去——在网里被拖着走实在叫人晕头转向，直到狱卒离开以后，他们才发现这座监狱原来是一条船——一条好大的船——卧在那儿，一钉一板处处完好，跟它头一天出坞时一模一样。海水似乎一点儿也没糟蹋它。他们被关在船上的大客厅里，这一天的百般滋味早把他们熬得筋疲力尽，便往那舒舒服服的红丝绒坐垫上一躺，睡着了。连梅维斯也觉得，凯思琳在女王跟前找到了一位朋友，不会有什么危险。

===Original===
The Princess was the last to close her eyes. She looked long at the sleeping children.

===Chinese===
公主是最后一个合眼的。她久久地望着熟睡的孩子们。

===Original===
“Oh, *why* don’t they think of it?” she said, “and why mustn’t I tell them?”

===Chinese===
"哦，他们*为什么*就想不到呢？"她说，"我又为什么偏偏不能告诉他们？"

===Original===
There was no answer to either question, and presently she too slept.

===Chinese===
两个问题都没有答案。不多时，她也睡着了。

===Original===
I must own that I share the Princess’s wonder that the children did not spend the night in saying “Sabrina fair” over and over again.
